  - **One transitional doc state** during 11.0 → 11.7 where DEC-025 is Active and Phase 11 sub-bullets in `STATE.md` accrete sub-step-by-sub-step. Same pattern as Phase 10's 10.0 → 10.8 rollout; if it becomes confusing in practice, capture as a follow-up sweep.

Brief Ask-mode planning checkpoint at the top of 11.4 to confirm the `_DescentBudget` shape and the `_STATELESS_LIB_DENYLIST` initial seed before the slicer code lands (mirrors the 10.6 wire-shape checkpoint).

If the qwen3.5:35b runtime can't use the larger `num_ctx`, revert and tighten the per-decision prose payload instead. Document the outcome in 11.6's `STATE.md` sub-bullet so v3 contributors know the local-LLM context-window posture.

- **v2 closing note (2026-04-30):**
  - Sub-steps 11.0 → 11.7 all landed across two calendar days per planning window. The closed-economy `_DescentBudget` discipline held up — 11.4's bounded inter-procedural method descent + 11.5's same-class field-write-site walking compose under a single shared budget without quadratic blow-up; per-anchor cost stays bounded and predictable across both tiers (a top-level slice with `MAX_SLICE_DEPTH = 2` budget can compose any of 2 method hops, 2 field-write hops, 1 field + 1 method, or 1 method + 1 field — verified end-to-end via the `gateFieldWriteFromMethodCall` and `gateFieldWriteFromDeepChain` fixtures in 11.5). The visited-set cycle terminator works as designed (`(class_smali, method_name, descriptor)` keys; field cycles share the same set with a structurally-distinct third tuple slot — "type" instead of "descriptor" — so methods and fields never collide).
  - The `descent_depth: int = 0` schema additivity held (per Q1 (A) of the 11.6 planning checkpoint) — `MethodCallOrigin` and `FieldReadOrigin` gain one optional `int` field; no breaking change to `BehaviorAnchor` / `DecisionPoint` / `BypassPlan` / `PredicateOrigin`'s discriminated-union members; `dataclasses.asdict` round-trip carries the field with default `0` for backward compat. The decision to limit `descent_depth` to the two cap-stop / blocked-terminal variants (rather than tagging all five `*Origin` variants) means success-path resolutions to `Const` / `Param` / `Composite` terminals stay v1-shaped — operators see the v1 terminal answer they expect, and the depth pill only fires when descent terminated as a cap-stop / blocked terminal where the depth signal is operator-actionable.
  - The `trace.sqlite` `SCHEMA_VERSION` 1 → 2 cache invalidation worked exactly as designed — the existing `get_status()` reader returns `error="schema_version mismatch"` on v1 reads, the route layer's "missing → build" fallback re-fires the slicer + LLM, operators see "freshly built" once per anchor with no migration code, no UI banner, no operator action required. The same "drop-the-cache invalidation" model DEC-024 used for v1 → v1.x worked without modification for v1 → v2.
  - All four planning-checkpoint pre-answers from this DEC's `decision:` block held in implementation: (1) **LLM budget = bump both `num_ctx` and `num_predict`** — landed in 11.6 (`num_ctx: 16384` + `num_predict: 12288`) alongside the schema bump; both wired through `Config` + `CONFIG_FIELD_MAP` + `LIVE_RELOADABLE_FIELDS` + env overrides; LLM client `_stream_ollama` and `_complete_ollama` both forward `num_ctx` to Ollama's `options` dict alongside `temperature` / `num_predict`. (2) **Deny-list shape = type-driven `is_stateless(method)` analyzer** — landed in 11.4 (~120 LOC; walks method bodies for `iput-*` / `sput-*` / `aput-*` / `monitor-*` / `throw vN` / non-stateless invokes / reflection; cycles defensively classify as stateful per the v2 defensive default). v1 deny-list seed: `Ljava/lang/Math;` (whole), 8 primitive boxing classes (whole), `Ljava/lang/String;` getter allowlist (length / charAt / substring / equals / hashCode / isEmpty / indexOf), `Lkotlin/jvm/internal/Intrinsics;` (whole), `Ljava/lang/Object;` getter allowlist (hashCode / equals / toString / getClass — extremely common in `if obj.equals(...)` predicates; added in 11.4 on top of the spec's seed). (3) **Cache invalidation = `trace.sqlite` schema_version bump from 1 to 2** — landed in 11.6 with a multi-paragraph schema-version-history comment block in `androscan/internal/trace_cache.py`. (4) **UI density on deeper chains = pill-only depth indicator on `PredicateOriginView`** — landed in 11.6 as the `DepthPill` sub-component rendering `via N helper method(s)` (for `MethodCallOrigin`) or `via N field write(s)` (for `FieldReadOrigin`) when `descent_depth >= 1`; tooltip explains the depth; new `.trace-predicate-origin-depth-pill` CSS uses the 11.3 trace-anchor purple accent (`rgba(157, 109, 255, 0.45)`) so it visually clusters with the existing origin tag without competing for attention.
  - **ISSUE-013 status closes to "Resolved (Phase 11 v2)"** against the v1-vs-v2 corpus regression-floor measurement test (`tests/test_decisions_slicing.py::test_v1_vs_v2_corpus_measurement_v2_resolves_strictly_more_terminals`) — runs both v1 and v2 slicing pipelines over the entire `trace_smali` fixture corpus and asserts (a) v2 strictly resolves more terminals than v1, (b) v2 produces ≥ 1 tagged origin, (c) v2 introduces no new slice failures. This locks the regression floor in CI per Q4 (A) of the 11.6 planning checkpoint. **The > 50% production-dogfood threshold from this DEC's `tradeoffs / consequences:` block remains a separate pending verification item** — the corpus measurement test confirms v2 is strictly better than v1, but the original DEC-025 framing called for measuring whether v2 closes > 50% of v1's None-cases on the dogfood app; that needs real-app traces accrued under v2, which haven't accumulated within the planning window. ISSUE-013 can re-open as "Open (partial v2 progress)" if real-app measurement shows v2 still leaves > 50% of v1's None-cases unresolved; the CI-locked corpus floor is the regression-prevention guarantee in either case.
  - **Honest deferral on the LLM-budget measurement outcome** per Q1 (A) of the 11.7 planning checkpoint — 11.6 shipped the bumped budgets per spec but the actual real-app prompt + output token-usage measurement was deferred (no dogfood-app traces accrued under v2 within the planning window). Follow-up tracked separately when telemetry surfaces, with the existing measure-first-tighten-if-needed posture intact: if the qwen3.5:35b runtime can't actually use the larger context (some local-LLM setups silently fall back to the model's smaller training-time context regardless of the runtime setting), revert and tighten the per-decision prose payload in `_format_decision_for_llm` instead. The original 11.6 follow-up bullet ("11.6's LLM budget verification: measure actual prompt + output token usage on the dogfood app's existing cached anchors freshly rebuilt under v2 slicer") remains live and is carried forward into the v3 candidate menu in the Active Task block of `docs/TASKS.md` as the "LLM-budget real-app measurement follow-up" candidate — Phase 12 will decide whether to take it on as part of the v3 scope or as a standalone follow-up commit.
  - **One material implementation deviation worth flagging for v3:** the original DEC text said the slicer's `_walk_field_write_sites` walker would handle "the same class's `iput-*` / `sput-*` write sites" without specifying the picking rule when multiple write sites exist. 11.5 implemented the **constructor-priority rule** (Q1 (A) of the 11.5 planning checkpoint) — partition write sites into `<init>`/`<clinit>` vs other methods, prefer the textually-last write within the constructor partition (matches operator intuition that the last `<init>` assignment is what the field's value will be after construction), fall back to the textually-last non-constructor write only when no constructor write exists. This is a more principled rule than the original DEC text implied and is captured here so a future contributor reading the DEC + the implementation doesn't trip over the asymmetry.
  - **v3 follow-up DEC and Phase 12 plan** (per Q2 (C) of the 11.7 planning checkpoint): **Phase 12 — Behavior Trace v3 / cross-tab follow-ups planning checkpoint (12.0)** is the next active item; **DEC-026 to be ratified as 12.0** picking the operator-demand-gated v3 candidates from this DEC's "Explicitly deferred to v3 / Phase 12" list — ISSUE-014 (per-overload precision on two-register comparisons), auto-verify bypass after injection, free-form LLM JS for bypass plans, per-app TTL on cached anchors, iOS / WebView / native-binary `BehaviorAnchor` adapters, Smali patching as a bypass technique, plus the carried-over LLM-budget real-app measurement follow-up from 11.6 / 11.7 Q1 (A). Strictly operator-demand-gated: 12.0 will pick the deliverables that show up in real-app telemetry from Phase 11 v2 use, not all of the deferral list at once. DEC-025 stays Active for the v3 follow-ups it explicitly defers — same posture as DEC-024 stayed Active for the cross-platform follow-ups it deferred to v2 — and DEC-026 will reference back to DEC-025's deferral-list rationale rather than restating it. **Parallel non-blocking thread:** **CI.0 — Phase 4 CI** was originally promoted to Active Task by this DEC in service of the Phase 11 slicer changes but ultimately shipped *after* Phase 11 v2 (the suite passed locally throughout 11.x, and operator's "Lab engine before CI" direction (2026-04-30) deprioritized it relative to v3 work); currently deprioritized — promote when the Lab engine reaches a stable v3 baseline. **NOTE:** the "next active item" framing in this bullet held until 2026-05-05 when the v2.1 follow-up release was ratified — see "v2.1 follow-up release ratified (2026-05-05)" block below; Phase 12 / DEC-026 is now deferred-after-v2.1.
- **v2.1 follow-up release ratified (2026-05-05):**
  - **Scope.** The v2.1 follow-up release addresses one operator-friction signal surfaced after Phase 11 v2 shipped: "the Smali entry-method input in Lab → Trace is unintuitive for non-advanced users." Scope locks at three coherent deliverables shipped across a strictly-linear 7-sub-step backlog (v2.1.0 → v2.1.6, mirrors Phase 11's 11.0 → 11.7 cadence): (1) **Deterministic UX backbone** — `ClassMethodTree` browser embedded in Trace mode (collapsible "Browse classes" section above the entry form, click-to-seed Smali class-prefix → `MethodPicker` for overload confirmation → auto-fires trace; raw Smali input field hidden behind an "Advanced: type Smali signature directly" toggle, `Hops` field always visible inline). (2) **Backend coalescer + spinner** — new `POST /api/trace/{app_id}/normalise-entry` endpoint translating dotted input (`com.example.Foo.onClick`) → Smali class-prefix (`Lcom/example/Foo;->onClick(`) + validating against the call graph, with frontend debounced 400ms inline spinner + ✓/⚠ validation pill. (3) **Three-tier LLM integration** — Tier 1 "Find similar classes" button on validation failure (LLM-backed candidates as suggestion pills); Tier 2(a) "Ask AI" button opening chat dock with pre-filled prompt template (leverages existing `search_decompiled_sources` + `query_call_graph` skills via the new `pendingChatPrefill` plumbing in `WorkbenchContext`); Tier 3 — new `androscan/skills/suggest_trace_entry.py` skill (RAG over decompiled sources + call-graph context + LLM ranking) **surfaced via the existing chat dock** (operator's preferred UI per Q3 Tier-3 feedback — chat handles natural-language reasoning + selection buttons + follow-up conversation; no new dedicated textarea in Trace mode).
  - **Five planning-checkpoint pre-answers (Q7-Q11) locked at v2.1.0 ratification:**
    - **Q7 (ii) — Skill-response schema extension for chat-interactive widgets.** Extend `SkillResult` (in `androscan/skills/base.py`) with an optional `widgets: tuple[SkillWidget, ...] = ()` field alongside the existing `text` field. `SkillWidget` is a typed union; v2.1.5 ships its first member (`TraceEntryCandidateWidget(kind="trace_entry_candidate", smali_id, rationale, confidence)`); future widget kinds add as new union members without breaking the schema (additive-by-design). The chat agentic loop's SSE event vocabulary extends with a new `widget` event kind alongside the existing `skill_request` / `skill_result` event kinds (per DEC-022 § "Extend the SSE vocabulary"). The frontend `<ChatWidgetRenderer>` dispatcher gracefully handles unknown widget kinds (renders the skill's `text` field only, ignores the unknown widget) so a server / client version skew is non-fatal. **Why (ii) over (i) magic Markdown links / (iii) attachment type:** (i) is hacky (URL-encoded Smali sigs are gnarly + hard to express richer state like rationale paragraphs + button rows); (iii) is semantically wrong (attachments today are operator-supplied context flowing INTO chat — using them for LLM-emitted UI flowing OUT of chat inverts the data flow direction). (ii) is the cleanest, most extensible, and sets up a pattern future LLM-driven workflow features (Hook Builder template suggestions, Inspect "did you mean this UI element?", etc.) can reuse. Cost: moderate but one-time. **DEC-022 cross-link:** the chat-interactive-widget pattern extends DEC-022's chat agentic loop with a new outbound channel (LLM-emitted UI in addition to LLM-emitted text); DEC-022's per-skill output budget of ~6 KB still applies; DEC-022's `MAX_SKILLS_PER_TURN = 3` and per-skill timeout = 5 s still apply.
    - **Q8 (a) — Auto-fire trace on candidate click in chat-rendered widget.** When the operator clicks a "Trace this candidate" button in the chat-rendered `<TraceEntryCandidateWidget>`, write `pendingTraceEntry = {appId, entryPrefix: candidate.smali_id, sourceLabel: "Chat → suggest_trace_entry"}` (re-use the 10.8 / 11.2 plumbing) + flip `labMode` to `"trace"` + `setTab("lab")`, and the existing `looksLikeCompleteSmaliSignature` + auto-fire path fires the trace immediately on landing. **Why (a) over (b) seed-only-no-fire:** (a) matches the existing pattern (Inspect → Trace seed already auto-fires on complete signatures via `looksLikeCompleteSmaliSignature`); the candidate already came from an LLM rec the operator explicitly chose, so they've consented; the extra click of (b) adds friction without adding safety (the trace is read-only per DEC-024 § "Trace itself is read-only" so auto-firing has no device-touching effect).
    - **Q9 — 7 sub-steps (v2.1.0 → v2.1.6).** Mirrors Phase 11's 11.0 → 11.7 discipline (one sub-step per Agent-mode session, strictly linear). Sub-step breakdown: v2.1.0 (planning checkpoint — docs-only — this commit), v2.1.1 (deterministic UX backbone — FE-only on `LabTraceMode.tsx` + `ClassMethodTree.tsx` + `App.css`), v2.1.2 (backend coalescer endpoint + frontend debounce + spinner — BE+FE), v2.1.3 (Tier 1 — BE+FE+LLM), v2.1.4 (Tier 2(a) + `pendingChatPrefill` plumbing — FE-only), v2.1.5 (Tier 3 skill + skill-response `widgets[]` schema extension + `<TraceEntryCandidateWidget>` chat renderer + auto-fire handoff — BE+FE+LLM, biggest sub-step), v2.1.6 (docs sweep + this DEC's v2.1 closing-note extension grown with the v2.1.1 → v2.1.5 implementation outcomes — docs-only). **Why 7 sub-steps over 3 collapsed sub-steps:** easier review per-PR, easier rollback per tier, matches the Phase 11 discipline operators are familiar with, and the sub-steps are individually shippable into a hand-run pytest gate (the same gate every Phase 10 / 11 sub-step shipped into).
    - **Q10 (α) — Extend DEC-025's closing-note with the v2.1 ratification block; no new DEC for v2.1.** v2.1 is still in the v2 family (DEC-025's scope = "Phase 11 — Behavior Trace v2"; v2.1 is a follow-up release within that same DEC's framing — same posture as the post-DEC-024 MethodPicker shipping under DEC-024's umbrella before Phase 11 / DEC-025 was ratified). The architectural pattern decision (Q7 ii — chat-interactive-widget pattern) is significant enough to warrant explicit documentation but not significant enough to warrant a new DEC — DEC-022 is its parent (chat agentic loop) and this DEC's v2.1 closing-note extension is the right home for the pattern's first ratification. **Why (α) over (β) new mini-DEC for v2.1:** opening a new DEC per follow-up release would balloon the DECISIONS.md file count (one per release) without adding clarity — the v2.1 closing-note extension is a single-anchor narrative that's easier to read in context than a separate DEC that would have to restate v2's scope to make sense.
    - **Q11 (α) — `androscan/skills/suggest_trace_entry.py` next to `trace_behavior.py`.** Confirmed: `androscan/skills/` is the canonical skills directory (no `androscan/llm/skills/` subdirectory exists in the codebase as of 2026-05-05; verified at v2.1.0 ratification via `Glob androscan/skills/*.py` returning 18 modules and `Glob androscan/llm/skills/*.py` returning 0). The new skill lives at `androscan/skills/suggest_trace_entry.py` registered in `androscan/skills/__init__._SKILL_MODULES`; `requires_confirmation=False` (read-only — no device-touching surface).
  - **Architectural pattern lock — chat-interactive widgets (Q7 ii).** v2.1.5 introduces the **first instance of LLM-emitted interactive widgets in chat** — a deliberately new pattern for AndroScan that extends DEC-022's chat agentic loop with a new outbound channel. The pattern is designed to be extensible: `SkillWidget` is a typed union (additive new members don't break the schema), the `<ChatWidgetRenderer>` dispatcher gracefully handles unknown widget kinds, and the SSE event vocabulary extension (`widget` kind alongside the existing `skill_request` / `skill_result` kinds) is additive at the chat-stream layer. **Why this matters for v3 / Phase 12 contributors:** the pattern is ready to be reused by future LLM-driven workflow features (Hook Builder template suggestions emitting "Stage this template" widgets, Inspect "did you mean this UI element?" emitting "Open in Code browser" widgets, etc.); reach for the `widgets[]` schema first before reaching for a new chat-render seam. **DEC-022 cross-link:** this pattern is documented in DEC-022's prose as a v2 / Hook Lab follow-up but lands earlier (in v2.1) because the operator-friction signal in Trace mode forced its hand; DEC-022's per-skill output budget + per-turn skill-output budget + `MAX_SKILLS_PER_TURN` discipline all still apply to widget-emitting skills.
  - **Sub-step backlog cross-link:** see `docs/TASKS.md` § **Phase 11 v2.1 follow-up release — sub-step backlog** for the strictly-linear v2.1.0 → v2.1.6 sub-step table (deliverables, dependencies, verification gates) and the v2.1-specific risks block (coalescer false-negatives, LLM budget squeeze on `suggest_trace_entry`, chat-widget pattern adoption beyond v2.1.5, `pendingChatPrefill` plumbing collision risk).
  - **Phase 12 plan revision (post-v2.1 ratification):** Phase 12 / DEC-026 was **deferred-after-v2.1** between 2026-05-05 v2.1.0 ratification and 2026-05-05 v2.1.6 wrap-up; flipped back to Active at v2.1.6 (was the next active item per Q2 (C) of the 11.7 planning checkpoint pre-v2.1). v3 candidate menu (carried forward verbatim from this DEC's "Explicitly deferred to v3 / Phase 12" list) is unchanged by v2.1: ISSUE-014, auto-verify, free-form LLM JS, per-app TTL, iOS / WebView / native-binary adapters, Smali patching, LLM-budget real-app measurement follow-up. Phase 12 ratification still operator-demand-gated and now also depends on real-app telemetry from the new v2.1 entry-method discoverability + chat-widget pattern (operators may surface new v3 candidates from v2.1 use that aren't currently in the deferral list — e.g. additional widget kinds, additional coalescer-input shapes, additional Tier-1 fuzzy-match strategies).
- **v2.1 closing note (2026-05-05) — extension to the v2.1 follow-up release ratified block above:**
  - **Sub-steps v2.1.0 → v2.1.6 landed within a single calendar day (2026-05-05).** Rapid back-to-back per-sub-step Agent-mode sessions; commit timeline (per `git log`): v2.1.0 16:45 → v2.1.1 17:13 → v2.1.2 18:08 → v2.1.3 18:20 → v2.1.4 20:33 → v2.1.5 21:14 → v2.1.6 (this commit). Each sub-step shipped through the standard pytest + `tsc --noEmit` + `vite build` + ReadLints gate before commit; no rollbacks needed within the release.
  - **Test-count delta: +28 tests end-to-end** (test count baseline at v2.1.0 was 907; HEAD at v2.1.6 is 935). Per-sub-step distribution: v2.1.1 +0 (FE-only); v2.1.2 +8 (`tests/test_trace_routes.py::TestNormaliseEntry`); v2.1.3 +7 (`tests/test_trace_routes.py::TestSuggestSimilarClasses`); v2.1.4 +0 (FE-only — the `pendingChatPrefill` plumbing is test-covered transitively via the existing `pendingTraceEntry` / `pendingHookPrefill` patterns); v2.1.5 +37 (`tests/test_suggest_trace_entry_skill.py` +25 new file + `tests/test_skills.py` +4 + `tests/test_chat_stream.py` +8); v2.1.6 +0 (docs only). The +37 in v2.1.5 dwarfs every other sub-step because v2.1.5 shipped both the new skill (with its own happy-path / fail-open / LLM-budget / malformed-JSON test coverage) AND the chat agentic loop refactor (with its own dispatch / SSE-event-forwarding / bounded-turn-halt / consent-class-halt test coverage). Frontend bundle deltas across v2.1.1 → v2.1.5: JS +~5 KB gzipped; CSS +~3 KB gzipped (new namespaces: `.trace-class-browser*` + `.trace-form-inline` + `.trace-advanced-toggle*` in v2.1.1; `.trace-validation-pill*` + `.trace-coalescer-spinner*` in v2.1.2; `.trace-similar-classes*` in v2.1.3; `.chat-skill-calls*` + `.chat-widget*` + `.chat-widget-trace-entry-candidate*` in v2.1.5).
  - **Architectural deviation worth flagging — v2.1.5 implementing the bounded chat agentic loop substrate as ISSUE-009's side-effect closure.** The v2.1.0 spec for v2.1.5 was scoped to "Tier 3 skill + skill-response `widgets[]` schema extension + `<TraceEntryCandidateWidget>` chat renderer + auto-fire handoff" — the spec assumed `androscan/web/chat.py` already had the bounded agentic skill loop that **DEC-022 § "Extend the SSE vocabulary"** had recommended. This was not the case: ISSUE-009 (Workbench chat cannot dig deeper than the initial RAG sweep) had been Open since 2026-04 because `androscan/web/chat.py` was single-pass throughout Phase 11 v2 (the agentic loop existed only in the CLI's `androscan/internal/workflow.py`, not in the chat-streaming code path). The chat-widget pattern needed an agentic loop substrate to live on (`widget` SSE events forwarded between turns; consent-class skills emitting `skill_pending` and halting; skill-result text appended to message history for subsequent turns) — so v2.1.5 had to ship the bounded agentic loop refactor first as the substrate the widget pattern is built on, then ship the widget pattern + the skill on top. **This is an explicit architectural deviation from the v2.1.0 spec** and is captured here so a future contributor reading the DEC + the v2.1.5 commit doesn't trip over the asymmetry. The deviation is in scope (still extends DEC-025 + DEC-022 — no new DEC needed per Q10 (α)), and the side-effect — closing ISSUE-009 — is a strict positive (the bounded agentic loop refactor was DEC-022's explicit recommended fix; v2.1.5 just shipped it earlier than expected and as part of a different release than originally framed).
  - **Chat agentic loop substrate (v2.1.5) — load-bearing details for v3 / Phase 12 contributors.** New constants `MAX_AGENTIC_TURNS = 5` and `MAX_SKILLS_PER_TURN = 3` in `androscan/web/chat.py`; new `agentic_loop: bool` request body field (optional, defaults to `False` for backward compatibility); `stream_chat_request` dispatches to the new `_stream_chat_agentic_request` async generator if `body.agentic_loop` is `True`, else falls back to the legacy single-pass streaming path. `_stream_chat_agentic_request` orchestrates multiple blocking `llm.client.complete()` calls within the bounded loop, parses LLM JSON responses (`thinking`, `content`, `skill_requests`), executes non-confirmation skills server-side, and emits four new SSE events: `skill_request` (every skill invocation), `skill_result` (success + `text`), `skill_pending` (consent-class skills that halt the loop — `requires_confirmation=True` skills emit this and halt with explanatory `content` instead of auto-executing; **safer than auto-execution** — explicitly gates `generate_frida_hook` / `extract_apk` / device-touching skills behind operator UI consent, which simply isn't wired yet so the loop halts there), `widget` (every `SkillResult.widgets` item, emitted as a separate SSE event after `skill_result`). Skill results (including `SkillResult.text`) are appended to the message history to provide context for subsequent LLM turns. Frontend opt-in via `AGENTIC_LOOP_TABS = new Set(["lab"])` in `ChatDock.tsx` — Lab tab opts into agentic loop; other tabs (Reports, Inspect) stay on the legacy single-pass path. **Why the opt-in:** v2.1.5's chat-widget pattern is most useful for the Lab tab's Trace-mode entry-method discovery; rolling the agentic loop out to all tabs in v2.1.5 would have ballooned the test surface and the rollback blast radius. v3 / Phase 12 contributors should consider rolling agentic loop opt-in out to Inspect (already covered by ISSUE-009's recommended fix) once the v2.1.5 pattern has proven itself in operator hands.
  - **Chat-widget pattern empirical operator-hands behaviour: pending real-app telemetry.** v2.1.5 ships the first instance of LLM-emitted interactive widgets in chat (`TraceEntryCandidateWidget`); the pattern is deliberately scoped to one consumer in v2.1 with a "ship the pattern, add more in subsequent releases as demand surfaces" framing per the Q7 (ii) lock-in record above. As of 2026-05-05 v2.1.6 wrap-up, **zero real-app dogfood traces have accrued under the chat-widget pattern** — the pattern's adoption by operators (does the auto-fire-on-click handoff feel right? do operators trust LLM rationales enough to click candidate cards? does the confidence-bucket badge ("high" / "medium" / "low") align with operator perception?) is a Phase 12 input. Phase 12 should treat the chat-widget pattern as a **load-bearing architectural addition** that will likely sprout new widget kinds (Hook Builder template suggestions, Inspect "did you mean this UI element?", Settings "this config knob looks wrong" — DEC-022 cross-link applies to all of these); the additive-by-design `SkillWidget` typed union is the ratified extension seam.
  - **DEC-022 cross-link (chat-interactive-widget pattern is now ratified beyond v2.1).** DEC-022 originally framed the chat agentic loop as a v2 / Hook Lab follow-up but it landed earlier (in v2.1.5) because the v2.1 follow-up release's Tier 3 deliverable forced its hand. The chat-widget pattern (LLM emits structured `SkillWidget`s alongside `text`; chat renderer detects via the `widgets[]` field on `SkillResult` and dispatches via `<ChatWidgetRenderer>`) extends DEC-022's chat agentic loop with a **new outbound channel** — the LLM can now emit interactive UI in addition to text. DEC-022's per-skill output budget of ~6 KB still applies (rationales capped at 200 chars + candidates capped at 3 keep `suggest_trace_entry` well within the budget); DEC-022's `MAX_SKILLS_PER_TURN = 3` and per-skill timeout = 5 s still apply (verified end-to-end in `tests/test_chat_stream.py` agentic-loop test suite); DEC-022's consent-class halt-and-`skill_pending` policy is the safer choice than auto-executing `requires_confirmation=True` skills (verified end-to-end in the same test suite).
  - **ISSUE-009 closure cross-link.** ISSUE-009 (Workbench chat cannot dig deeper than the initial RAG sweep) flips from Open → Resolved (Phase 11 v2.1.5) as a side-effect of v2.1.5's chat agentic loop substrate; see `docs/KNOWN_ISSUES.md` § ISSUE-009 for the closure paragraph + cross-link to DEC-022's now-implemented recommended fix.
  - **What v2.1 did NOT change.** Trace-mode workflow shape (Anchor → Locate → Trace → Classify → Manipulate → Verify) is unchanged from v2; `BehaviorAnchor` / `DecisionPoint` / `BypassPlan` / `PredicateOrigin` schemas are unchanged from v2; bypass-plan templates (`force_return_value` / `force_method_skip` / `force_string_compare_equal`) are unchanged from v1; `trace.sqlite` `SCHEMA_VERSION` stays at `"2"` (no v2.1 schema bump); `Config` knobs are unchanged from v2 (no new v2.1 knobs); device-touching surface area is unchanged from v2 (zero new device-touching surface — Trace stays read-only, the new coalescer endpoints are read-only over the call graph, the new `suggest_trace_entry` skill is `requires_confirmation=False`, the chat-widget pattern's auto-fire on click is bounded to the existing `pendingTraceEntry` plumbing). `docs/SAFETY_AND_SECURITY.md` is **explicitly unchanged** in v2.1.6 per this discipline.
- **v2.1 follow-up patches (2026-05-06 v2.1.7 + 2026-05-07 v2.1.8) — closure of the v2.1.4 / 10.8 "Ask AI" collision.**
  - **v2.1.4's two latent collisions surfaced post-release.** The v2.1.4 implementation of the Tier-2(a) "Ask AI" button shipped two latent collisions that the v2.1.0 spec didn't catch but that operator dogfooding flushed out within ~24 hours of v2.1.6 wrap-up. **Collision 1 (empty-Smali-entry case):** v2.1.4's button was disabled while `entryDraft` was empty — but v2.1.1 had hidden the Smali entry field by default (it lives behind the "Advanced" toggle, with Browse classes + MethodPicker as the operator-easy-mode path), so the operators who most needed the rescue rope (the ones who didn't know which class to trace) couldn't reach the button because the entry field they were supposed to type into was hidden. **Collision 2 (Trace mode mounting shape):** v2.1.4's button writes `pendingChatPrefill = {tab: "lab", message, sourceLabel}` to `WorkbenchContext` and v2.1.4's spec assumed a `<ChatDock tab="lab">` was reachable from Trace mode — but Phase 10.8 had **deliberately** removed the chat dock from Trace mode (`LabTraceMode.tsx` doc-block lines 52-57 stated the contract: "Trace mode itself still has no chat dock of its own — operators who want to ask the LLM about a trace switch to Manual Hooks mode, which carries the ChatDock"). The only consumer (`LabTab.ManualHooksMode`'s `chatRef.expand()` effect at lines ~258-267 + the `<ChatDock>` it renders at line 557) was unmounted whenever `labMode === "trace"`. Net effect: clicking "Ask AI" in Trace mode silently set the prefill on `WorkbenchContext` with no consumer mounted to receive it — operator-visible no-op. **Both collisions are direct consequences of v2.1.4 being scoped wider than the v2.1.0 spec realised** — the spec assumed the chat dock was always reachable from the Lab tab (which was true at v2.1.0 ratification time but was about to become not-true the moment v2.1.1 hid the Smali entry field and v2.1.4 added the Ask AI button targeting that hidden field).
  - **v2.1.7 (commit `8d84e64` 2026-05-06) — empty-entry case.** Closes Collision 1 only. Removed the `disabled={!trimmedEntry}` gate from the button + added a free-form prompt template ("I'm looking for a trace entry method in this app. ") for the empty case (vs. the original v2.1.4 template "I want to trace `<entry>`. What entry methods should I consider?" for the non-empty case). The operator picks up where the prefill leaves off — same UX as typing into the chat dock directly, just one click closer. v2.1.7 was a stealth patch (no STATE.md / TASKS.md / DECISIONS.md updates at the time — the commit mentioned the patch in the LabTraceMode.tsx doc-block lines 131-165 but the doc tree didn't get an explicit pointer until v2.1.8's docs sweep below).
  - **v2.1.8 (2026-05-07) — Trace mode mounting shape.** Closes Collision 2. Embeds a `<ChatDock tab="lab">` directly into `LabTraceMode` as a collapsible right-side panel of a new horizontal `react-resizable-panels` `PanelGroup` (`autoSaveId="lab-trace-h"` so size + collapsed state persist via `localStorage` mirrors the `lab-manual-h` pattern). New `chatRef: ImperativePanelHandle` + `chatCollapsed: boolean` state inside `LabTraceMode`; the same `chatRef.expand()` effect on `pendingChatPrefill?.ts` that lives in `LabTab.ManualHooksMode` is duplicated here so the two modes have parallel expand-on-prefill consumers (only one mode is mounted at a time so they never race). The two modes share the per-tab chat history (`WorkbenchContext.chats["lab"]`) by virtue of both rendering `<ChatDock tab="lab">` — they're never co-mounted (mutually-exclusive `labMode === "..."` branches), so there's no rendering conflict, and the unified history reads naturally as one "Lab conversation" rather than per-mode scratch surfaces. Consequence: clicking "Ask AI" in Trace mode now expands the right-pane chat dock with the prefilled prompt (or the v2.1.7 empty-entry template) without the operator having to mode-hop to Manual Hooks. **Per-Lab-mode `<AppPicker />`** (operator UX request bundled into the same commit): each of the three Lab sub-modes now carries its own `<AppPicker />` instance (Trace mode in the existing `<header className="pane-head">`; Manual Hooks + Graph modes in a new `<header className="lab-mode-head">` strip) — all three local pickers + the global header `<AppPicker />` share `appId` via `WorkbenchContext` for **bidirectional sync without explicit plumbing**. **Module extraction:** `_renderTraceAttachment` + `CHAT_TRACE_BUDGET` + `CHAT_TRACE_TOP_PLANS` + `CHAT_TRACE_MAX_DECISIONS` lifted out of `LabTab.tsx` into a new shared `androscan/web/frontend/src/util/traceChatAttachment.ts` so both `LabTab.ManualHooksMode` and `LabTraceMode` import the same renderer (the `trace` `ChatAttachment` shape is byte-identical regardless of which mode triggered the chat — the LLM sees a consistent attachment format).
  - **v2.1.9 (2026-05-07, same calendar day as v2.1.8) — AppPicker placement migration (closes v2.1.8 visual-stacking regression).** v2.1.8's per-Lab-mode `<AppPicker />` UX request — bundled into the same atomic ship vehicle as the Trace-mode `<ChatDock>` embed — was implemented as full-width header strips in Manual Hooks + Graph (`<header className="lab-mode-head">` with title-on-left + AppPicker-on-right) and a right-side actions slot in Trace's existing `<header className="pane-head">`. Operator dogfood the same morning caught that Manual Hooks's strip stacked vertically directly under the global header's AppPicker — two identical "Select project…" dropdowns on consecutive vertical lines — read as a layout bug rather than a deliberate redundancy. **What v2.1.9 ships.** **Trace mode:** `<span className="pane-head-actions"><AppPicker /></span>` removed from the existing `<header className="pane-head">` (reverts pane-head to its pre-v2.1.8 shape — title + subtitle only); existing one-line `{!appId && <p className="muted small">No app selected — pick a project from the dropdown above to start tracing behaviour.</p>}` empty-state replaced with a centered card (`.lab-empty-state` shell + `.lab-empty-state-card` body) carrying a more prominent "No app selected" + secondary copy + the `<AppPicker />` as the CTA. Once `appId` is set, no in-pane picker renders at all. **Manual Hooks:** v2.1.8's `<header className="lab-mode-head">` strip + `<div className="lab-manual-hooks-mode">` wrapper both removed; the entire 3-column `PanelGroup` is gated on `appId` via an early-return `if (!appId) return (<EmptyState />)` at the top of the render, using the same `.lab-empty-state-card` class as Trace for visual consistency (whole-mode empty-state chosen over per-panel because none of the Manual Hooks panels — CallGraphView / HookBuilder / Sessions / CodeView / Chat — can do useful work without an `app_id`, they all gate on it internally and would render placeholder messaging in 4 of 5 panels otherwise). **Graph mode is explicitly unchanged** — the `<header className="lab-mode-head">` strip + AppPicker remain per operator scope choice on the v2.1.9 design questionnaire (single-pane CallGraphView with no non-empty body to render when `appId` is null — different ergonomics than the Trace / Manual Hooks cases where the whole-mode empty-state pattern fits naturally). **CSS:** `.lab-manual-hooks-mode` rule dropped (its wrapper is gone); `.lab-mode-head` family kept (Graph still uses); new shared `.lab-empty-state` + `.lab-empty-state-card` + `.lab-empty-state-title` + `.lab-empty-state-body` rules added. **Verification:** 1093 / 1093 pytest (no test changes — bug was UI-mounting-shape only); `tsc --noEmit` clean; `vite build` clean (JS +0.44 KB raw / +0.08 KB gzip vs v2.1.8 baseline; CSS +0.50 KB raw / +0.07 KB gzip — the new `.lab-empty-state*` rules slightly outweigh the dropped `.lab-manual-hooks-mode` rule); ReadLints clean across all 3 production-code files. **Operator impact:** the redundant-second-picker-stacked-under-global no longer ships; the picker now surfaces exactly when contextually useful (operator has no app selected and needs to pick one) and disappears entirely once an app is loaded — mid-session project switching relies on the global header at the top-right (always visible, single source of truth, eliminates the visual redundancy entirely).
  - **v2.1.10 (2026-05-07, same calendar day as v2.1.7 / v2.1.8 / v2.1.9) — three-bug operator-dogfood patch: Lab-mode state preservation across mode-hops + narrow-pane Trace JSON column repair + Settings sub-tab scroller migration to the page-right edge.** Three independent bugs landed in one ship vehicle because each was a small, surgical fix (none warranted its own commit) and the operator-feedback loop that surfaced them was a single dogfood session. **Bug 1 — Lab-mode state lost on hop-and-return.** Pre-v2.1.10 `LabTab.tsx` conditionally rendered `<LabTraceMode />` / `<ManualHooksMode />` / `<GraphMode />` keyed on `labMode === <id>`, fully unmounting inactive modes — the operator lost their entire Trace state (entry draft, loaded `BehaviorAnchor`, decompile tree, similar-classes candidates) every time they popped to Manual Hooks for a quick hook tweak and back. **Fix:** Trace + Manual Hooks always-mounted via a new `.lab-mode-content-pane` wrapper using `display: contents` (active) / `[hidden]` UA-`display: none` (inactive); children act as direct flex items of `.lab-mode-content` exactly as pre-v2.1.10. Graph mode stays conditional per operator scope choice on the v2.1.10 design questionnaire (its single-pane CallGraphView mounts a Cytoscape Web Worker that's measurably expensive to keep idle; operator's pan / zoom state already serialises through Cytoscape's own viewport cache). The 11.3 doc-block at LabTab.tsx lines 436-441 (which described the freshness-via-remount pattern Manual Hooks's `anchoredMethods` overlay used to rely on) is replaced by an explicit `traceCacheVersion: number` + `bumpTraceCacheVersion()` counter on `WorkbenchContext` (mirrors local `cachedReloadTick` / `sessionsRefreshTick` patterns inside LabTab.tsx itself, but lifted to context because Trace and Manual Hooks are now siblings rather than mutually-exclusive conditional renders). `LabTraceMode` calls `bumpTraceCacheVersion()` from the post-Build effect and from `onDeleteCached`; `ManualHooksMode`'s `anchoredMethods` effect adds the counter to its dep array. Counter is monotonic across `appId` changes — the consumer's effect already keys on `appId`, so a stale counter from a previous app is harmless; keeping it monotonic simplifies reasoning. **Bug 2 — Trace JSON column rendered as a tall narrow column instead of a row in narrow Lab panes.** The `.trace-row-meta` CSS Grid (`grid-template-columns: 1.2rem 6.5rem 3.4rem 4rem auto 1fr`) had no compressibility constraints on the `auto` label track — an unbreakable label like `onCreate$lambda$0_trace` forced the track to its full max-content width (~17 rem at 0.74 rem font), leaving the `1fr` payload track at near-zero in the Manual Hooks right column (defaults to 22% of Lab tab width = ~19 rem total). Expanded rows then wrapped JSON every ~8-10 chars via `overflow-wrap: anywhere`. **Fix:** added `min-width: 0; max-width: 14rem; overflow: hidden; text-overflow: ellipsis; white-space: nowrap;` to `.trace-label` so the label compresses with `…` truncation; full label still readable via the row's existing `title={compact}` tooltip + the chevron-expand renders the label inline in the payload's first key. **Bug 3 — Settings sub-tab vertical scrollbar offset from the page right edge.** The Settings panel chain (`.settings-tab` grid 240px | 1fr → `.settings-panel-host` overflow:hidden → `.settings-panel` max-width:1100px margin:0 auto padding:16px 24px 0 → `.settings-panel-body` overflow:auto) put the scroll on the inner `.settings-panel-body`, so the scrollbar sat at the inner panel's right edge — typically 24 px (panel right padding) inside the right column on a narrow viewport, more on wider ones (centered panel adds 30+ px margins). **Fix:** promoted the scroller from `.settings-panel-body` up to `.settings-panel-host` (`overflow-y: auto; overflow-x: hidden;`); reduced `.settings-panel-body` to a plain content block; made `.settings-panel-header` `position: sticky; top: 0; z-index: 2; background: var(--bg);` so it pins to the top of the host's scroll viewport (same operator UX — header always visible — but the scrollbar now lives on the host element which is the right edge of the `1fr` column inside `.settings-tab`, i.e. the absolute right of the page minus the 240 px nav). Header's `margin-bottom: 16px` folded into `.settings-panel-body { padding-top: 16px }` so the visual spacing stays the same (the margin would otherwise leave a transparent strip below the sticky header where scrolled content could show through). Affects all four Settings sub-panels (Global / App / Status / Diagnostics) since they share the `.settings-panel` shell — bonus consistency win. **Verification:** 1124 / 1124 pytest (no test changes from v2.1.10 itself — the +31 from v2.1.9's 1093 baseline come from two interim commits between v2.1.9 and v2.1.10 unrelated to the v2.1 follow-up release umbrella); `tsc --noEmit` clean; `vite build` clean (JS +0.28 KB raw / +0.10 KB gzip vs v2.1.9 baseline; CSS +0.21 KB raw / +0.04 KB gzip — well within LCP.0 ceiling); `ReadLints` clean across all 4 production-code files.
  - **v2.1.11 (2026-05-20, ~13 days after v2.1.10 — operator dogfood across the LCP-track infra surfaced a different bug class) — Frida-server Settings card surfaces "running but unverified-root" amber state + per-mode Diagnose playbook.** **What broke.** Operator hit a 503 from `POST /api/frida/attach` (`frida.attach(pid=2107, package='com.example.weakbank.low') failed: unable to connect to remote frida-server: closed`) while the Settings → Status card simultaneously reported `frida-server` as green / "running". Root cause: the probe's Layer 3 (`frida-ps -U`, host-side wire-protocol enumeration) had succeeded but Layers 1 (`pidof frida-server`) and 2 (`ps -A | grep frida-server*`) had both missed — typical of a renamed binary, a `frida-gadget` injected into the target app, OR a non-root `frida-server` whose comm name doesn't match the Layer 1/2 prefix filter. Pre-v2.1.11, `FridaServerStatusCard` painted the dot green whenever `card.ok === true`, AND the probe's `ok` mirrored `running` (any layer hit → green) — so a Layer-3-only detection with `uid === null` rendered identically to a Layer-1 / Layer-2 hit with `uid === "root"`. The `uidWarning` extras line was gated on `card.uid != null && card.uid !== "root"`, which silently skipped the `uid == null` case (the unverified-root branch). The card's `needsStart` signal (which drives the **Start frida-server (as root)** button) had the same gate, so the button was hidden for the unverified case too — operators were left with no in-UI path to recover from the broken state. **What v2.1.11 ships.** **Three changes in `SettingsTab.tsx`** (single file, ~120 LOC net add): (1) **`StatusCardView` gains an `overrideDot?: "ok" | "warn" | "fail"` prop** that lets callers paint the dot color when they know more about the card's health than `card.ok` conveys; all other callers (every other status card across Global / Per-app surfaces) keep their existing dot derivation — `overrideDot` falls back to that ladder when omitted (future-friendly seam: an LLM card that wants to flip to amber when `ping_ms > threshold` could reuse this without touching the generic StatusCardView shape). (2) **`FridaServerStatusCard` collapses both amber sub-cases into a single `unverifiedRoot` signal** (`card.running && (card.uid == null || card.uid !== "root")`) that drives both the amber-tint pass (`overrideDot={unverifiedRoot ? "warn" : undefined}`) AND the extended `needsStart = !card.running || unverifiedRoot` Start-button visibility. Two distinct `uidWarning` lines for the two sub-cases — same amber tint either way, but the diagnostic playbook below branches on this same signal. Pre-v2.1.11 host-confirmed copy ("running (host-confirmed via frida-ps — renamed binary or frida-gadget)") rewritten to ("running (host-side enumeration only — renamed binary, frida-gadget, or non-root server)") — adds the third cause AND ties the label to the amber state. (3) **New `FridaServerDiagnoseHint` component** (~60 LOC; rendered alongside the Start button + Install playbook in `actions`; reuses the `.frida-install-*` CSS classes for visual parity with the red-state Install playbook + zero new CSS). Two render modes: **`"known-non-root"`** surfaces the kill command `adb shell "pgrep -f frida-server \| xargs -r kill -9"` (matches the route's 409-error-detail text from `frida_routes.start_frida_server` lines 654-660 so the operator can preview the command instead of having to click-fail-copy-from-error-text-click) — needed because `start_frida_server` deliberately refuses to silently kill+promote a non-root server (operator might have started it that way intentionally for a different workflow; silently killing it would surprise them). **`"unverified"`** surfaces a two-step playbook: step 1 `adb shell "ps -A \| grep -iE 'frida\|gadget'"` (find the actual PID + binary name — the same command the operator used in their dogfood screenshot), step 2 `adb shell "su 0 kill -9 <PID>"` (kill explicitly; `<PID>` is a plain-text placeholder, not auto-substituted — auto-substitution would lie about device state at render time; the operator must read step 1's output first). Both playbooks omit the "start as root" command — the Start button next to the hint is the single source of truth for that step (sourcing the canonical binary path from the route's `_FRIDA_SERVER_BIN` constant — no risk of drift). **Status type doc-block updates** in `androscan/web/frontend/src/api/status.ts` `frida_server.uid` (8 lines): the spec's pre-existing "yellow warning when this is anything other than `"root"`" already implied the `null` case should warn; v2.1.11 brings the implementation in line and the doc-block now explicitly documents the `null`-as-unverified-presumed-non-root treatment + the per-mode playbook + the extended button visibility. **No backend changes, no `health_probes.py` changes, no `frida_routes.py` changes** — the backend probe's permissive-by-design `ok` posture (any layer hit → green so liveness alone never reads as broken) stays as-is; the "ok-but-broken-for-attaches" nuance is a UX-side concern that lives at the rendering boundary where the operator sees it. **Verification:** 1290 / 1290 pytest (no test changes from v2.1.11 itself — bug was UI-side rendering / signal-derivation; no backend behaviour shift, no schema change, no API change; the +166 from v2.1.10's 1124 baseline come from Phase 13 sub-step ships 13.0 → 13.10 + v2.0 + v2.0.1, all unrelated to the v2.1 follow-up release umbrella); `tsc --noEmit` clean; `vite build` clean (1511.76 KB JS / 472.85 KB gzip / 131.18 KB CSS / 21.03 KB gzip — absolute size is now Phase-13-v2.x-dominated; v2.1.10's pre-Phase-13-v2 baseline is no longer a meaningful comparison point); `ReadLints` clean across both modified files (`SettingsTab.tsx` + `status.ts`). **Operator impact:** (1) Settings → Status card now visually distinguishes "verified-up-as-root" (green) from "reachable-but-can't-confirm-root" / "verified-non-root" (amber) — operators no longer get the contradictory "green probe + 503 attach" experience that triggered this patch. (2) For the amber sub-cases, the Start button is now visible (was hidden for the unverified branch pre-v2.1.11), giving the operator a one-click recovery path. (3) The per-mode Diagnose playbook surfaces the exact kill commands inline + previewable (vs. buried in the post-click 409 `lastError` div) — the click-fail-copy-from-error-click loop collapses to copy-paste-click.
  - **Why neither patch warrants a new DEC.** All five (v2.1.7 + v2.1.8 + v2.1.9 + v2.1.10 + v2.1.11) are bug fixes within v2.1.4's scope (DEC-025's umbrella covers them); v2.1.7's stealth-patch posture, v2.1.8's single-commit-with-bundled-UX-request posture, v2.1.9's same-day placement-correction posture, v2.1.10's same-day three-bug-bundle posture, and v2.1.11's much-later single-bug-with-architectural-seam posture all fit the "post-release patch under the Phase's existing DEC" pattern (same shape as the post-DEC-024 MethodPicker patches that shipped under DEC-024's umbrella before Phase 11 / DEC-025 was ratified). None of the patches changes the v2.1 architectural commitments (workflow shape, schemas, bypass-plan templates, device-touching surface area — all unchanged). The "v2.1 closing note" above continues to read accurately for the v2.1.0 → v2.1.6 release window; this v2.1.7 / v2.1.8 / v2.1.9 / v2.1.10 / v2.1.11 sub-bullet records the post-release patch trail for the as-built history.
  - **Architectural lesson worth flagging for future contributors.** The v2.1.4 / 10.8 collision is a specific instance of a more general failure mode: **a feature that lives in a conditionally-mounted parent (e.g. one of three Lab sub-modes) reaching for a shared-state pattern (`pendingChatPrefill`) without checking whether the consumer is mounted in the current sub-mode.** The fix pattern — duplicate the consumer effect in every sub-mode that needs it (the two `chatRef.expand()` effects are mutually exclusive at runtime) OR lift the consumer up to the parent that's always mounted — is worth remembering when adding new cross-component plumbing in v3 / Phase 12. The shared-history-across-sub-modes posture (single `chats["lab"]` for both Trace and Manual Hooks) was a deliberate v2.1.8 choice but it is itself a Phase 12 input — operator dogfooding will tell us whether the unified "Lab conversation" reads naturally or whether v3 should split per-mode scratch surfaces. **A second lesson worth flagging from the v2.1.9 patch trail:** when adding a feature meant to mirror or supplement an existing global UI surface (like the v2.1.8 per-mode `<AppPicker />` mirroring the global header's AppPicker), check the visual placement against the global surface's own placement. If the two sit on consecutive vertical lines (or any pattern that reads as accidental duplication), the redundancy is a layout-bug-by-default — fix by either differentiating the visual context (e.g., embed in an empty-state card vs. a thin strip) or by hiding one of them conditionally (v2.1.9 chose the latter — the in-pane picker only shows when `appId` is null, so the two pickers are never visible simultaneously). **A third lesson worth flagging from the v2.1.10 patch trail:** when conditional-render is used for sub-mode switching to "avoid re-running effects on every mode hop" (the original 10.6 / 10.7 rationale on LabTab.tsx lines 213-217), it's worth tracing the operator-side cost: the inactive sub-mode's *entire local React state* is destroyed on hop, including in-progress operator artefacts (loaded BehaviorAnchor, hook builder draft, decompiled source cache, ChatDock scroll position). For sub-modes the operator may dwell-and-return inside a single investigation, the `display: none` always-mounted pattern (`.lab-mode-content-pane[hidden]`, mirroring the existing `.lab-tab-host[hidden]` precedent) is usually the right ergonomic — React keeps the DOM alive, the browser skips paint / layout for hidden subtrees, and operator state survives. The exception: heavyweight sub-modes (Cytoscape canvases, video / WebGL contexts, large EventSource subscriptions) where the always-mounted cost is measurable; v2.1.10 kept Graph mode conditional for exactly that reason. **A fourth lesson worth flagging from the v2.1.10 patch trail:** when a CSS Grid track uses `auto` sizing (intrinsic) and may receive unbreakable content (Smali identifiers, hashes, URLs without spaces / hyphens), pair it with `min-width: 0; max-width: <cap>; overflow: hidden; text-overflow: ellipsis; white-space: nowrap` so a long unbreakable value can't ratchet the entire grid open and starve adjacent `1fr` tracks. This is the CSS-Grid analogue of the long-standing flexbox `min-width: 0` workaround for `flex-shrink` and applies to any `<span>` / `<code>` cell that surfaces user-supplied identifiers. **A fifth lesson worth flagging from the v2.1.10 patch trail:** when a max-width-constrained centered container (`.settings-panel { max-width: 1100px; margin: 0 auto; padding: 16px 24px 0 }`) hosts a scroll region, the scrollbar lives on the inner element by default — at the inner panel's right edge, not the page's right edge. For long-content panels, operators expect the scrollbar at the page's right edge (the "all the other panels in the workbench follow this convention" implicit contract). Promote the scroller to the outermost grid / flex column (the page-edge container) and use `position: sticky` on the header to preserve the always-visible-header UX. Affects the entire family of sub-panels that share the shell — bonus consistency win, but worth budgeting docs / verification time for any header that has `margin-bottom` (would leave a transparent strip below the now-sticky header where scrolled content can show through; fold the margin into the body's leading padding to avoid). **A sixth lesson worth flagging from the v2.1.11 patch trail: green probe ≠ green UX.** A backend health probe is typically permissive about classification (`ok=True` whenever ANY of N detection layers hits, because the layers were designed for liveness, not feature-readiness — e.g. `frida-ps -U` proves "host-side enumeration works" but cannot prove "`device.attach(pid)` will succeed because the per-attach helper needs `CAP_SYS_PTRACE`"). The UI's rendering boundary is the right place to add the "ok-for-X-but-not-Y" nuance because that's where the operator-visible action (attach) maps to the operator-visible status (the card dot). When a status card's caller knows more than `card.ok` conveys, an `overrideDot?: "ok" | "warn" | "fail"` prop on the generic StatusCardView (v2.1.11 added this seam) lets the caller paint the right color without forcing the backend probe to become stricter (which would make the probe lie about liveness when feature-readiness fails). Future status cards should consider what their `card.ok` actually proves and whether the UX gains from an `overrideDot` pass — e.g. an LLM card that wants to flip amber when `ping_ms > threshold`, a filesystem card that wants amber when `free_gb < threshold`. **A seventh lesson, also from v2.1.11:** when a backend route deliberately refuses to auto-promote / auto-fix a misconfigured state (the `frida_routes.start_frida_server` 409-on-non-root posture: "operator might have started it that way intentionally for a different workflow; silently killing it would surprise them"), the UI should still surface a *preview* of the required manual step BEFORE the operator clicks the action button — not buried in the post-click error text. The pre-v2.1.11 UX forced operators into a click-fail-copy-from-error-text-click loop; v2.1.11's per-mode Diagnose playbook collapsed that to a copy-paste-click loop by previewing the same kill command (and the `<PID>` discovery step for the unverified case) inline with copy buttons. This is the UI-side mirror of the "fail-soft with structured hint" backend pattern — the hint is more useful surfaced AHEAD of the failure, not as its consequence.

### DEC-027: LCP track — `LLM_PROVIDERS` table refactor + llama.cpp local provider (parallel infrastructure track on `main`)
- status: Closed *(LCP.0 → LCP.6 all shipped 2026-05-06 across the same calendar day; the LCP track is **fully complete** — no remaining sub-steps. LCP.6 (GBNF grammar enforcement + Ollama JSON-schema mode) closes the JSON-validity-drift gap captured as `ISSUE-016` at LCP.5; the issue moves to the Resolved section of `docs/KNOWN_ISSUES.md`. Two closing notes are appended below — the LCP.5 v1-ship-complete note and the LCP.6 final closing note that records the as-built outcome of the GBNF follow-up.)*
- date: 2026-05-06
- owners: (project)
- context:
  Operator hardware reality (M3 MacBook Pro, 36 GB unified memory) is squeezed by the current `qwen3.5:35b` model running through Ollama (`global_config.yaml` line 7) — model weights at the model's chat-template-default Q-level dominate the unified-memory budget alongside the React/Vite/FastAPI/uvicorn/IDE workload, leaving the operator's box thrashing during real-app traces. The decision to switch the local LLM runtime from Ollama to **llama.cpp** (specifically `llama-server`'s OpenAI-compatible HTTP shim) addresses two operator-driven goals at once: (1) lighter runtime overhead per token thanks to llama.cpp's tighter Metal acceleration on M-series hardware (`-ngl 99` offloads all layers to unified memory with no host↔device copy cost) + flash attention (`-fa`) trimming KV-cache memory pressure, freeing budget for a meaningful Q-level + larger context; (2) operator agency over per-quant memory tradeoffs — operators can pick UD-Q5_K_XL (~20 GB, the unsloth-dynamic-quant sweet spot for AndroScan's structured-JSON workload on a 36 GB M3 Pro) or Q6_K (~22.5 GB) without re-pulling Ollama-managed model files. Per the previous turn's analysis (in this same Agent-mode session prior to DEC-027 ratification), AndroScan's "tool calling" is **not** OpenAI-native `tools`/`tool_calls` — it's JSON-mode-driven prompt orchestration over the `skill_requests` / `hypotheses` schema (verified via `Grep` on `tool_calls|tool_call|tools=|function_call|skill_request` returning only `androscan/llm/parser.py` + `androscan/llm/prompts.py` + `androscan/internal/workflow.py` + `androscan/skills/__init__.py` hits — the runtime swap is a transport-layer concern, not a protocol-layer concern). llama.cpp's `llama-server` exposes `/v1/chat/completions` with `response_format: {"type": "json_object"}` parity to Ollama's `format: "json"` field, so the JSON-mode discipline transfers without prompt or schema rewrites. The runtime swap is genuinely orthogonal to all v2 / v2.1 / Phase 12 v3 Behavior Trace work — closest precedent in `docs/TASKS.md` is **CI.0** (the parallel non-blocking thread DEC-025 promoted alongside the Phase 11 11.x backlog). Operator chose to ship LCP work on `main` (not a feature branch) per the established Phase 10 / 11 / v2.1 sub-step-by-sub-step cadence with selective staging, after the v2.1 follow-up release fully landed (HEAD `15e7c1a` post-v2.1.6, working tree clean, no in-flight v2.1 / Phase 12 work to coordinate with).
- decision:
  - **Scope locked: 7 sub-steps LCP.0 → LCP.6, strictly linear, one sub-step per Agent-mode session** (mirrors the Phase 10 10.0 → 10.8 / Phase 11 11.0 → 11.7 / v2.1.0 → v2.1.6 cadence operators are familiar with). LCP.0 (this commit — planning checkpoint + this DEC, docs only). LCP.1 (`LLM_PROVIDERS` table refactor + `Config.provider_kind()` + `CLOUD_PROVIDERS` backwards-compat alias — pure refactor, no behavior change). LCP.2 (new `_call_llamacpp()` HTTP path via `requests` + router branch in `complete()` + JSON-mode parity + optional `<think>` strip — backend behavior addition, Ollama path untouched). LCP.3 (`probe_llamacpp` health probe + Status panel block — backend probe + thin frontend addition). LCP.4 (Settings UI radio restructure: top-level "Local (Ollama)" / "Local (llama.cpp)" / "Cloud" with nested cloud dropdown — frontend; may split into 4a (UI restructure, defaults preserved) + 4b (llama.cpp sub-knobs land) if the diff balloons during planning). LCP.5 (docs sweep — STATE / TASKS flips, this DEC's closing note, KNOWN_ISSUES entry for the GBNF deferral, README pointer to upstream `llama.cpp` docs — docs only; **no `docs/LLAMACPP.md`** per Q5 (C)). LCP.6 (**committed follow-up**, not optional — JSON-validity hardening via GBNF grammar enforcement for the `skill_requests`/`hypotheses` schema; benefits all local providers including Ollama; ships separately so v1's runtime swap has a clean A/B-comparison surface and the JSON-schema → GBNF emitter gets its own design pass). **Explicitly NOT in LCP scope:** OpenAI-native `tools`/`tool_calls` migration (would require rewriting the `skill_requests` JSON convention in `androscan/llm/parser.py` + `prompts.py` + every skill handler — large blast radius for no operator-visible value since JSON mode already works for the existing convention); llama.cpp embeddings (operator already uses `fastembed` for lane-1 RAG per `global_config.yaml` line 44 — a second `llama-server` instance for embeddings is operator-demand-gated, not in v1); `tool_choice` schema-driven dispatch (deferred to LCP.6 alongside GBNF — both are JSON-validity hardening tools that compose); auto-spawn of `llama-server` as a managed sub-process (out of scope — operator runs `llama-server` themselves, AndroScan only consumes the HTTP endpoint).
  - **Q1 → (B) + table refactor.** Dedicated `_call_llamacpp()` codepath using `requests` directly (parallel structure to `_call_ollama()` — both `local-*` providers go through `requests`; `cloud` keeps the `openai` SDK path), **NOT** an extension of the existing `_call_cloud()` codepath. Provider-table restructure: introduce top-level `LLM_PROVIDERS` dict with two sub-sections — `LLM_PROVIDERS["local"]` (containing `"ollama"` + `"llamacpp"`) and `LLM_PROVIDERS["cloud"]` (containing the existing 6 cloud providers — gemini, openai, groq, deepseek, together, mistral — verbatim from current `CLOUD_PROVIDERS`). Backwards-compat alias `CLOUD_PROVIDERS = LLM_PROVIDERS["cloud"]` keeps existing imports in `androscan/llm/client.py` + elsewhere working without per-call edits during the LCP.1 refactor commit. Each provider entry gains a `"kind"` field — `"local-ollama"` (Ollama HTTP `/api/chat` with `format: "json"`) | `"local-openai-compat"` (llama-server HTTP `/v1/chat/completions` with `response_format: {"type": "json_object"}`) | `"cloud"` (OpenAI SDK against provider-specific base URL). New `Config.provider_kind() -> Literal["local-ollama", "local-openai-compat", "cloud"]` method replaces the binary `is_cloud()` magic-string check (`is_cloud()` is preserved as a backwards-compat alias returning `provider_kind() == "cloud"` so existing call sites in `androscan/web/health_probes.py` + Settings UI keep working through the LCP.1 refactor commit). Default `base_url` for the `llamacpp` entry: `http://127.0.0.1:8033/v1` (per Q4). `key_env: None` for both `local-*` entries (signals "no API key required" — `Config.resolve_cloud_api_key()` returns the empty string for these, and `_call_llamacpp` passes either no Authorization header or a dummy one since llama-server's OpenAI-compat shim ignores it).
  - **Q2 → (a) defer GBNF to LCP.6, but PROMOTE LCP.6 from "optional / telemetry-gated" → "committed follow-up".** v1 ships with `response_format: {"type": "json_object"}` only — Ollama parity baseline. GBNF grammar enforcement for the `skill_requests` / `hypotheses` schema (which would harden against quantization-induced JSON drift on Q4_K_M / Q5_K_M variants) lives in LCP.6 as a strictly-committed follow-up, not as an operator-demand-gated deferral. Reasoning: (1) shipping `response_format` first gives a clean A/B-clean baseline for measuring runtime swap effects in isolation (any JSON-validity drops attributable to model + quant, not to schema-enforcement changes); (2) a JSON-schema → GBNF emitter that handles `skill_requests` / `hypotheses` discriminated unions correctly is non-trivial and deserves its own design pass — bundling it with the runtime swap risks blocking the v1 LCP ship and conflating two concerns; (3) once GBNF lands in LCP.6, it benefits **all** local providers (Ollama also supports JSON-schema-style constraints via its `format: <schema>` mode in newer builds) — broader long-term win than llama.cpp-only enforcement. KNOWN_ISSUES entry at LCP.5 ratification documents the GBNF deferral with a cross-link to LCP.6 so a future contributor reading the gap doesn't re-open it as new.
  - **Q3 → (B) top-level radio + nested cloud dropdown.** Settings → Global section (currently a flat provider dropdown with sub-knobs that render conditionally) restructured to a three-way top-level radio: "Local (Ollama)" | "Local (llama.cpp)" | "Cloud", with the existing 6-cloud-provider dropdown nested under the "Cloud" radio when selected. Pre-existing Ollama users land in "Local (Ollama)" by default with no knob changes (the visual layout shifts but no semantic change — the Ollama `base_url` / `model` / `temperature` / `num_predict` / `num_ctx` knobs render under the radio, identical to today). When "Local (llama.cpp)" is selected, the sub-knobs include `base_url` (default `http://127.0.0.1:8033/v1`), `model` (free-text — operator's GGUF file label, e.g. `qwen3-27b-q5km`; llama-server typically returns a single model id, doesn't need a real picker), a read-only line "context size set at server start" (since `num_ctx` is not a request-level param in OpenAI-compat mode — operator passes `--ctx-size 16384` to `llama-server`), and `max_tokens` (mapped from the existing `num_predict` field on the wire — naming preserved internally so the field/env-var/yaml infrastructure doesn't churn). Settings UI plumbing aligned with the data-model refactor — same architectural shape lives in both layers (data model split in Q1 ↔ UX split in Q3). LCP.4 may split into 4a (UI restructure, defaults preserved — Ollama still default; pre-existing operators see new radio shape but identical knob behavior) + 4b (llama.cpp sub-knobs land — only visible when "Local (llama.cpp)" radio is selected) if the diff balloons during planning; the 4a/4b split decision lives in the LCP.4 planning checkpoint sub-bullet of `docs/STATE.md`.
  - **Q4 → (A) loopback-explicit base URL, port 8033.** Default `base_url` for the `llamacpp` provider entry is `http://127.0.0.1:8033/v1`. Loopback-explicit (`127.0.0.1`, not `localhost`) for parity with `web.host: "127.0.0.1"` safety-posture in `global_config.yaml` line 34 (DEC-006 / DEC-021 lineage on the loopback-default discipline). Port `8033` is an operator-chosen non-default — llama.cpp's upstream default is `8080`, so the LCP.5 README pointer notes that operators must pass `--port 8033` to `llama-server`. Port `8033` doesn't conflict with AndroScan's own `web.port: 8420` or any other known-bound port in the project.
  - **Q5 → (C) README pointer only.** No `docs/LLAMACPP.md` operator setup guide. LCP.5 lands a one-paragraph addition to the existing README pointing operators at upstream `llama.cpp` documentation (build / install / model download) and noting the AndroScan-specific knobs they should set (`--port 8033`, `--ctx-size 16384`, `-ngl 99` for Metal offload, `-fa` for flash attention, `--jinja` for chat-template parity). No in-repo guide to maintain. Operator's stated preference (rejected (A) single-page setup guide and (B) helper script + guide in favour of "minimal — README link to upstream docs only") locks in the smallest documentation surface that keeps LCP.5 lean and avoids drift between an in-repo guide and upstream's evolving build/setup process.
  - **Branch posture: ship on `main` with selective-staging discipline** (rejected the alternative of forking a dedicated `llamacpp` feature branch). Mid-DEC-027-ratification context shift: at the time the branch question was originally asked (six minutes before this DEC ratification, mid-Ask-mode planning), `main` had three unpushed v2.1 commits (`b513e5a` HEAD ahead of `origin/main` by 3); within those six minutes operator ran the v2.1.4 → v2.1.6 sub-steps to completion in parallel sessions, leaving HEAD at `15e7c1a` and `main` synced to `origin/main`. The branch-posture question (α fork-and-rebase vs β pause-and-resume) became moot — there's no in-flight v2.1 work on `main` to coordinate with, and Phase 12 v3 planning is operator-demand-gated with zero telemetry accrued, so `main` is quiescent for LCP work. Branch posture is therefore the same Phase 10 / 11 / v2.1 sub-step-by-sub-step cadence on `main` with selective staging — every LCP.x commit lands as its own atomic ship vehicle, scope-pinned to its sub-step's deliverable, no merge step at the end, no rebase concerns.
- rationale:
  - **Why a dedicated `_call_llamacpp()` HTTP path instead of reusing `_call_cloud()`?** The existing `_call_cloud()` codepath in `androscan/llm/client.py` pulls in the `openai` SDK (currently used for Gemini / OpenAI / Groq / Deepseek / Together / Mistral) — extending it to local OpenAI-compat servers would either require relaxing the API-key requirement (sending an empty string or a dummy key, which works in practice but is ugly) or introducing a code path inside `_call_cloud()` that's specifically for the no-key case (which is itself a code-smell — the function would have to branch on the absence of a key in a way that obscures the cloud-provider intent). The dedicated `_call_llamacpp()` is parallel-structured to `_call_ollama()` (both use `requests` directly, both are local-machine HTTP calls, both need to forward AndroScan-specific JSON-mode + temperature + max-tokens knobs but to different request shapes) — architectural symmetry: `local-*` providers go through `requests`; `cloud` uses the `openai` SDK. The router in `complete()` becomes a clean three-way switch on `Config.provider_kind()` instead of two overlapping checks. Cost: ~80-120 LOC of new code in `client.py` for `_call_llamacpp()` (mirrors `_call_ollama()`'s shape — body builder + streaming SSE parser + retry logic; substantially smaller than `_call_ollama()` because the OpenAI-compat shim doesn't need Ollama's `num_ctx` retry tier or the `format: "json"` quirks); +1 dedicated test file `tests/test_llm_client.py` for the new path (the existing `tests/test_config.py` covers the table refactor; the new file covers `_call_llamacpp()` end-to-end via mocked `requests`).
  - **Why a top-level `LLM_PROVIDERS` dict with `local` + `cloud` sub-sections, not a flat dict with a `kind` field per entry?** The flat-with-kind shape (`{"ollama": {"kind": "local-ollama", ...}, "llamacpp": {"kind": "local-openai-compat", ...}, "gemini": {"kind": "cloud", ...}, ...}`) is simpler in code (one lookup, one `.get("kind")`); the nested shape (`LLM_PROVIDERS["local"][...]` + `LLM_PROVIDERS["cloud"][...]`) makes the architectural division more visible at a glance to a future contributor reading `androscan/config/loader.py` for the first time. Operator's literal request was "create 'LLM_PROVIDERS' and move 'CLOUD_PROVIDERS' as one section under it and another called 'LOCAL_PROVIDERS' for Ollama, llama.cpp, etc." — so the nested shape is what the operator asked for. The `kind` field still lives on each entry (so `Config.provider_kind()` doesn't have to do a reverse lookup), giving us both the visible structural division and the cheap `kind`-field dispatch.
  - **Why GBNF as a committed LCP.6 follow-up instead of bundled into v1?** Three reasons. (1) Shipping `response_format` (Ollama parity) first gives a clean A/B baseline for the runtime swap — if operators report JSON-validity drops post-LCP, we can attribute them to model + quant rather than to the JSON-mode mechanism itself, and the response is "tighten the GBNF schema" (LCP.6) rather than "rebase the runtime swap." (2) The JSON-schema → GBNF emitter is a real piece of engineering — `skill_requests` / `hypotheses` are discriminated unions with nested optional fields and tuple-typed `widgets[]` (post-v2.1.5); a half-measure GBNF that just ratifies "outputs object {...}" is worse than no GBNF (false sense of safety), and a real GBNF needs to handle every variant of every union member. (3) GBNF isn't llama.cpp-only — newer Ollama builds support a `format: <json-schema>` mode that achieves the same constraint via grammar-derivation under the hood. Shipping GBNF as a separate LCP.6 sub-step lets us land the schema emitter in a way that benefits **both** local providers, not just llama.cpp.
  - **Why a top-level radio in Settings (Q3 B) instead of a flat dropdown extension (Q3 A)?** The flat-dropdown extension would have llama.cpp as one more entry alongside Ollama / Gemini / OpenAI / etc. — visually compact but architecturally misleading: it implies all providers are peers, when in fact the local-vs-cloud split is load-bearing (different transports, different request bodies, different health-probe shapes, different per-provider knob sub-trees). The top-level radio aligns the Settings UX with the data-model refactor — same architectural shape lives in both layers — so when an operator asks "should I run cloud or local?" the UI's first question matches the implementation's first decision. Cost: bigger LCP.4 frontend diff (the Settings tab currently has a flat dropdown; restructuring it as a radio + nested dropdown is a non-trivial Settings-component touch). Mitigated by LCP.4 splitting into 4a (UI restructure, defaults preserved) + 4b (llama.cpp sub-knobs land) if the diff balloons — same discipline that v2.1.5 used when an architectural deviation exceeded the original sub-step's scope.
  - **Why port 8033 instead of llama.cpp's upstream default 8080?** Operator-chosen non-default. Port 8080 is one of the most common dev-port collisions (every webdev's default), and AndroScan's `web.port` is already 8420, so picking 8033 for `llama-server` (a) avoids 8080's collision class, (b) keeps the AndroScan + llama-server pair on adjacent operator-memorable port numbers (8033 / 8420), (c) puts the choice firmly in the operator's hands (LCP.5's README pointer notes that operators must pass `--port 8033`). No conflict with any known-bound port in the AndroScan ecosystem; loopback-explicit binding (`127.0.0.1`) keeps the server unreachable from the network per DEC-006 / DEC-021 safety-posture lineage.
  - **Why README pointer instead of `docs/LLAMACPP.md` setup guide (Q5 C)?** Operator's stated preference for the smallest documentation surface that's good enough — llama.cpp's upstream documentation is well-maintained and evolves with the build/setup process, so an in-repo guide would inevitably drift behind upstream. The README pointer puts operators on the canonical source-of-truth and notes the AndroScan-specific knobs (port, context size, Metal offload, flash attention, Jinja chat-template parity) inline. Cost: operators new to llama.cpp need to read upstream docs to install + run `llama-server` — but the same applies to AndroScan's existing Ollama dependency (we don't ship an Ollama setup guide either; we point at https://ollama.com). Symmetric posture between the two local providers feels right.
  - **Why ship LCP work on `main` (not a feature branch)?** Operator's stated preference, ratified after the v2.1 follow-up release fully landed and `main` became quiescent (HEAD `15e7c1a`, working tree clean, no in-flight v2.1 / Phase 12 work). The Phase 10 / 11 / v2.1 sub-step-by-sub-step cadence with selective staging is the established discipline — every prior phase shipped this way, so the operator already knows how to roll forward atomic commits and how to selectively stage when in-flight unrelated changes appear. LCP.0 → LCP.3 + LCP.5 → LCP.6 are additive / refactor-without-behavior-change / docs — minimal main-branch risk. LCP.4 is the only sub-step where "lands on main" carries non-trivial rollback cost (the Settings UI radio restructure is operator-visible the moment it ships); the 4a/4b split (if the diff balloons) gives a cheap rollback story (revert 4b, leave 4a's UI restructure in place — pre-existing Ollama users still see the new radio shape, just without the llama.cpp sub-knobs).
- alternatives considered:
  - **Reuse `_call_cloud()` for llama.cpp via `CLOUD_PROVIDERS` extension** (rejected per Q1 (B) operator pick): smaller diff, fewer files touched, leverages existing `openai` SDK plumbing — but obscures the cloud-vs-local architectural split, requires ugly `key_env=None` special-casing in `_call_cloud()` for the no-key path, and conflates "AndroScan calling a remote vendor API" with "AndroScan calling a local-machine HTTP server" in the same function. The dedicated `_call_llamacpp()` is +80-120 LOC for a much cleaner architectural shape.
  - **Flat `LLM_PROVIDERS` dict with `kind` field per entry** (rejected per operator's literal "create 'LLM_PROVIDERS' and move 'CLOUD_PROVIDERS' as one section under it and another called 'LOCAL_PROVIDERS' for Ollama, llama.cpp, etc."): simpler one-level lookup, but loses the visible structural division between local and cloud providers. The nested shape is what the operator asked for and what makes the architectural split obvious to a future contributor.
  - **Bundle GBNF grammar enforcement into v1 ship** (rejected per Q2 (a) — but committed as LCP.6): would land JSON-validity hardening + runtime swap together, ratifying both architectural changes at once. Rejected because it (1) couples two independent ships, (2) adds a JSON-schema → GBNF emitter as new design surface inside the LCP track, (3) makes A/B comparison of the runtime swap's effects harder. LCP.6 ships GBNF on its own merits with its own design pass; benefits both local providers (Ollama supports JSON-schema mode in newer builds), so the GBNF investment isn't llama.cpp-locked.
  - **Flat dropdown extension for Settings UI** (rejected per Q3 (B)): visually compact, smaller LCP.4 frontend diff, but architecturally misleading — implies all providers are peers when local-vs-cloud is load-bearing (different transports, different probe shapes, different sub-knob trees). Top-level radio aligns the UX with the data model.
  - **llama.cpp's default port 8080** (rejected per Q4 (A) operator pick of port 8033): 8080 is the upstream-documented default but collides with the most common dev-port use cases; 8033 is operator-memorable + conflict-free + explicit operator choice.
  - **`docs/LLAMACPP.md` operator setup guide** (rejected per Q5 (C)): would land a polished in-repo setup guide with install / build / model-download instructions and an AndroScan-specific configuration walkthrough. Rejected because (1) llama.cpp upstream docs are the canonical source-of-truth and an in-repo guide would inevitably drift, (2) the README pointer + AndroScan-specific knob notes are smaller surface that's good enough, (3) symmetric posture with the existing Ollama dependency (we point at https://ollama.com without shipping an Ollama setup guide).
  - **`scripts/setup-llamacpp.sh` helper that invokes brew + downloads a GGUF** (rejected per Q5 (C) — the (B) option in the original Q5 framing): more polish + automated setup, but more code to maintain + risk of drifting against upstream's preferred install path on different OSes / brew tap moves. README pointer is the smallest surface.
  - **Feature branch `llamacpp` with rebase-onto-main at end** (rejected per operator pick of "ship on main"): cleaner branch hygiene, smaller blast radius mid-implementation, but adds a merge step at the end + a rebase concern if other work lands on `main` in parallel. The Phase 10 / 11 / v2.1 cadence on `main` with selective staging is the established discipline — operator chose consistency over branch isolation.
