  - **Pause Phase 12 v3 planning until LCP track ships** (rejected because Phase 12 was already operator-demand-gated and not in flight): Phase 12 / DEC-026 was promoted to "Active" by v2.1.6 but it's gated on real-app telemetry from Phase 11 v2 + v2.1 use, which hasn't accrued. Phase 12 stays in its current "Active. Planning checkpoint 12.0 pending" posture; LCP track runs alongside it without conflict (different layers — Phase 12 is feature-layer Behavior Trace work, LCP is infrastructure-layer transport work). When telemetry accrues for Phase 12, both can ship in either order.
  - **Migrate to OpenAI-native `tools` / `tool_calls` instead of staying on JSON-mode-driven prompt orchestration** (rejected as out of LCP scope): would require rewriting `androscan/llm/parser.py` + `prompts.py` + every skill handler to consume `tool_calls` arrays instead of parsing `skill_requests` from JSON content. Large blast radius for no operator-visible value — JSON mode already works for the existing convention, and llama.cpp + Ollama + every cloud provider supports both modes. Capture for a future re-evaluation if a downstream use case justifies it (e.g. structured-input-streaming for very long agentic chains where the tool_calls protocol's per-call event boundaries become operator-visible — not v1 / v2.1 / Phase 12 territory).
  - **Auto-spawn `llama-server` as a managed sub-process from AndroScan** (rejected as out of LCP scope): would make AndroScan's startup more self-contained but adds operational complexity (process lifecycle, port-conflict resolution, log-stream capture, crash recovery, version-skew handling between AndroScan's spawned server and any operator-side standalone server). Operator runs `llama-server` themselves; AndroScan only consumes the HTTP endpoint. Same posture as the existing Ollama integration (we don't auto-spawn `ollama serve`; we expect it to be running).
- tradeoffs / consequences:
  - **`androscan/config/loader.py` gains ~80-100 LOC for the `LLM_PROVIDERS` table refactor** (the new nested dict structure + `Config.provider_kind()` method + backwards-compat `CLOUD_PROVIDERS` alias + `is_cloud()` shim that delegates to `provider_kind()`). LCP.1 ships this as a pure refactor — every existing test in `tests/test_config.py` that touched `CLOUD_PROVIDERS` or `is_cloud()` continues passing without changes; +5-7 new tests cover the new `LLM_PROVIDERS["local"]` / `LLM_PROVIDERS["cloud"]` lookup paths + the backwards-compat alias holding.
  - **`androscan/llm/client.py` gains the new `_call_llamacpp()` function** (~80-120 LOC parallel to `_call_ollama()` — body builder for OpenAI-compat `/v1/chat/completions`, streaming SSE parser via `requests` `iter_lines()`, retry logic; substantially smaller than `_call_ollama()` because the OpenAI-compat shim doesn't need Ollama's `num_predict` retry tiers). The `complete()` router becomes a three-way switch on `Config.provider_kind()`. New test file `tests/test_llm_client.py` (~+8-10 tests) covers the llama.cpp path end-to-end via mocked `requests` (request body shape; SSE stream parsing; `<think>` strip; retry on transient HTTP errors; JSON-mode `response_format` payload; max_tokens mapping from `num_predict`).
  - **`androscan/web/health_probes.py` gains `probe_llamacpp(base_url)`** that hits `GET /v1/models` (newer builds also have `/health`). LCP.3 lands this alongside a frontend Status panel block conditional on `Config.provider_kind() == "local-openai-compat"` — Settings → Status renders the llama.cpp block instead of the Ollama block when the operator's selected provider is llamacpp. +5-7 backend tests in `tests/test_health_probes.py` covering up/down/partial-up states (server live, model not loaded — common during model-load lag on cold starts).
  - **`global_config.yaml` gains an `llamacpp:` section** at LCP.4 (parallel to the existing `ollama:` section) with the operator-configurable knobs (`base_url`, `model`, `max_tokens`). The existing `ollama:` section stays unchanged for backwards compatibility. `Config` dataclass gains parallel `llamacpp_*` fields wired through `CONFIG_FIELD_MAP` + `LIVE_RELOADABLE_FIELDS` + env overrides + YAML coercion, mirroring the existing `ollama_*` plumbing.
  - **Settings UI restructure at LCP.4 is the only sub-step with non-trivial rollback cost** (operator-visible the moment it ships on `main`). Pre-existing Ollama users land in "Local (Ollama)" by default with no knob changes (the visual layout shifts but no semantic change). If the diff balloons during planning, the 4a/4b split provides a cheap rollback story: revert 4b (the llama.cpp sub-knobs) and the 4a UI restructure stays in place with Ollama working as today.
  - **JSON-validity drift risk on lower quants (Q4_K_M / IQ4_XS)** is the largest known LCP risk. v1's `response_format: {"type": "json_object"}` matches Ollama's `format: "json"` in strictness — both ratify "output is a syntactically valid JSON object" but neither ratifies "output matches the `skill_requests` / `hypotheses` schema." A quantization-induced token slip (e.g. dropping a closing brace or emitting a `null` where a tuple was expected) would surface as a parser error in `androscan/llm/parser.py`, fail-soft per existing convention. LCP.6's GBNF grammar enforcement closes this gap. Honest about the gap: the LCP.5 KNOWN_ISSUES entry surfaces it for operators running aggressive quants in v1.
  - **Performance characteristics differ between Ollama and llama.cpp on the same hardware + same model.** llama.cpp's tighter Metal acceleration on M-series (per-layer offload via `-ngl 99`, no host↔device copy cost) typically beats Ollama's perf at the same Q-level by ~10-30% in tokens/sec; flash attention (`-fa`) is additive (often another 10-15% on long contexts). Operators switching from Ollama-q4 to llamacpp-Q5_K_M may see net higher quality + comparable speed. LCP track doesn't ratify a perf measurement target — operator runs whatever they want and reports back.
  - **Telemetry / observability surface is unchanged.** AndroScan doesn't currently export structured per-LLM-call metrics (latency, token usage, retry counts) to a metrics sink — both `_call_ollama()` and the new `_call_llamacpp()` log to `_log` only. Future telemetry work (e.g. Prometheus-compat metrics on `llama-server --metrics` would be a natural pair) is operator-demand-gated, not in LCP scope.
  - **`docs/SAFETY_AND_SECURITY.md` is explicitly unchanged.** LCP track adds zero new device-touching surface area — `llama-server` runs locally on `127.0.0.1:8033` (loopback per Q4 A), operator's responsibility to keep the server bound to loopback (mirrors AndroScan's own `web.host: "127.0.0.1"` posture); no new ADB / Frida / device-touching paths. LCP.5 docs sweep does not modify `SAFETY_AND_SECURITY.md`.
  - **Relationship to Phase 12 v3 work.** LCP track and Phase 12 are orthogonal — different layers (LCP = infrastructure/transport, Phase 12 = feature/Behavior-Trace). Both can be Active simultaneously without scheduling conflict. When Phase 12 telemetry accrues and DEC-026 ratifies, the v3 sub-steps run alongside whatever LCP sub-step is in flight. Conflict surface on shared files is tiny: `docs/STATE.md` "Recent completed work" + `docs/TASKS.md` "Active Task" block are the only realistic merge points, both append-newer-on-top by convention.
- follow-up:
  - Implement sub-steps LCP.1 → LCP.5 strictly linearly per `docs/TASKS.md` § LCP — llama.cpp local provider — sub-step backlog. One sub-step per Agent-mode session. Brief Ask-mode planning checkpoint at the top of LCP.4 to confirm the 4a/4b split decision (UI restructure vs llama.cpp sub-knobs) based on the diff size measured at that point.
  - LCP.6 (GBNF grammar enforcement) ships as a committed follow-up after LCP.5, not as an operator-demand-gated deferral. The JSON-schema → GBNF emitter design lives in LCP.6's planning checkpoint; if the emitter scope balloons (e.g. needs to handle deeply-nested discriminated unions across multiple skills' response shapes), LCP.6 may itself split into LCP.6a (emitter + skill_requests grammar) + LCP.6b (hypotheses grammar + per-skill grammar coverage). That split decision lives in the LCP.6 planning checkpoint sub-bullet of `docs/STATE.md`.
  - **Operator setup post-LCP.5:** operator runs `llama-server -m <gguf> -c 16384 -ngl 99 -fa --port 8033 --host 127.0.0.1 --jinja` against a Q5_K_M / UD-Q5_K_XL Qwen3-family GGUF; flips `provider: llamacpp` in `global_config.yaml`'s new `llmprovider:` section (or via Settings UI's "Local (llama.cpp)" radio); validates via Settings → Status that the `probe_llamacpp` block reads green; runs a Trace on a known-good cached anchor and compares verdict + plan-list quality against the existing Ollama-shipped baseline. This validation step is operator-side and not part of any LCP sub-step's commit.
  - **JSON-validity-drop telemetry:** if operators report parser errors in `androscan/llm/parser.py` post-LCP that weren't present pre-LCP (i.e. attributable to the runtime swap), open a follow-up issue and prioritize LCP.6 ahead of any other deferred LCP work. Honest about the gap: lower-quant local-LLM runtimes do drift from spec under prompt pressure, and the v1 `response_format: {"type": "json_object"}` doesn't ratify the schema (only the "is JSON" property). Measure-first-tighten-if-needed posture.
  - **Embeddings via llama.cpp deferred:** operator currently uses `fastembed` for lane-1 RAG (`global_config.yaml` line 44). If operators want to consolidate onto a single local LLM runtime, a second `llama-server` instance for embeddings on a different port is the path forward (e.g. `--port 8034 -m <bge-small-en-v1.5.gguf>`). Track as an operator-demand-gated follow-up — not in LCP track v1 scope.
  - **OpenAI-native `tools` / `tool_calls` migration deferred** to a future re-evaluation if a downstream use case justifies it. Capture as a long-tail follow-up when a concrete operator workflow surfaces (e.g. very long agentic chains where the per-call event boundaries become operator-visible).

- **LCP.5 closing note (2026-05-06) — v1 ship complete; LCP.6 GBNF the only remaining sub-step:**
  - **As-built sub-step landing record (all on `main`, all on the same calendar day 2026-05-06):**
    - **LCP.0** (`52e4f8e`) — planning checkpoint + DEC-027 ratification (this entry); 7-sub-step backlog locked; docs only across `DECISIONS.md` / `TASKS.md` / `STATE.md` / `DESIGN_DOC.md`. Active Task flipped from "Phase 12 — Behavior Trace v3 planning checkpoint pending (DEC-026)" → "LCP track" with Phase 12 demoted to "deferred-pending-telemetry."
    - **LCP.1** (`ab174b7`) — `LLM_PROVIDERS` table refactor + `Config.provider_kind() -> Literal["local-ollama", "local-openai-compat", "cloud"]` + `is_cloud()` shim + backwards-compat `CLOUD_PROVIDERS` alias. Pure refactor — every existing test continued passing. **+29 new tests** in `tests/test_config.py` (`TestLlmProvidersTable` 7, `TestProviderKind` 11, `TestIsCloudShim` 8, `TestResolveCloudApiKeyForLocalProviders` 3). Test count baseline 935 (post-v2.1.6) → 964.
    - **LCP.2** (`fc33658`) — dedicated `_complete_llamacpp()` HTTP path via `requests` to llama-server's `/v1/chat/completions` OpenAI-compat endpoint (`~250 LOC` parallel structure to `_complete_ollama()`); 3-way switch on `Config.provider_kind()` in `complete()`; `<think>...</think>` strip helper applied defensively to **both** Ollama and llama.cpp response paths (newer Qwen3-family builds may leak think-blocks into the content channel depending on `--reasoning-format` flag); JSON-mode parity (`response_format: {"type": "json_object"}` ↔ Ollama's `format: "json"`); retry tiers (`_LLAMACPP_TIMEOUT_TIERS`, `_LLAMACPP_RETRYABLE_STATUSES`); `LLAMACPP_DEFAULT_MODEL_LABEL` constant for log readability. **+34 new tests** in new file `tests/test_llm_client.py` covering body-shape / SSE parsing / transient-error retry / non-stream response handling / 3-way router dispatch / message builder. Test count 964 → 998.
    - **LCP.3** (`c21b639`) — `probe_llamacpp(base_url) -> dict` health probe in `androscan/web/health_probes.py` (`GET {base_url}/models` with curl-first / urllib-fallback, classifies `_DOWN` / `_PARTIAL_UP` / `_UP`); `llamacpp_model_present(tags, model)` helper for case-insensitive exact / prefix matching. `_gather_global` in `androscan/web/status_routes.py` runs **exactly one local-LLM probe per request** keyed on `Config.provider_kind()` (mutual exclusion); LLM card payload gains `provider: "ollama" | "llamacpp"` discriminator + provider-specific label / error messages. Frontend: `GlobalStatus.llm` type extended; `StatusCardView` accepts an `extraClassName` prop; "via Ollama" / "via llama.cpp" extras line; thin accent-border CSS for `.status-card.{ollama,llamacpp}-status`. **+12 new tests** in `tests/test_health_probes.py` + `tests/test_settings_routes.py` (`probe_llamacpp` up/down/partial states + mutual-exclusion routing + payload shape). Test count 998 → 1009 (with one pre-LCP.3 fixture extension).
    - **LCP.4** (`bbe8c95`) — Settings UI top-level radio "Local (Ollama)" / "Local (llama.cpp)" / "Cloud" with the existing 6-cloud-vendor dropdown nested under "Cloud" + provider-section conditional rendering (operators picking one provider no longer see the other two providers' knobs); `Config` dataclass gains 3 frozen `llamacpp_*` fields (`llamacpp_base_url` defaulting to the registry's `http://127.0.0.1:8033/v1`, `llamacpp_model` free-text label, `llamacpp_max_tokens` default 8192) wired through `CONFIG_FIELD_MAP` / `LIVE_RELOADABLE_FIELDS` / `Config.default()` / `_merge_from_yaml()` / env-override block / final `Config(...)` constructor / `with_overrides()` (the live-reload path used by Settings UI saves; missing constructor args caught by full pytest sweep mid-implementation); `Config.active_model` now routes on `provider_kind()` so the run header shows `llamacpp_model` (or "(not set)") instead of `ollama_model` when llama.cpp is active; `_complete_llamacpp` prefers the new `llamacpp_max_tokens` field with backwards-compat fallback to `ollama_num_predict`; `global_config.yaml` gains a commented `llamacpp:` section with the recommended startup line + per-knob rationale. **+21 new tests** in `tests/test_config.py` (`TestLlamacppBaseUrl` 6, `TestLlamacppModel` 5, `TestLlamacppMaxTokens` 6, `TestLlamacppRoundTrip` 3) + `tests/test_llm_client.py` (renamed LCP.2 fallback test + new `prefers_dedicated_llamacpp_field_when_set` happy-path test + `_make_llamacpp_mock_config` accepts overrides). Test count 1009 → 1030. **4a/4b split decision (per LCP.0 plan):** single commit. `SettingsTab.tsx` net add was +251 LOC, right at the ~250 LOC trigger boundary, but the bulk is repeated `SettingsField` render blocks (not new logic) and a 4a/4b split would have produced an awkward intermediate state where "Local (llama.cpp)" radio shows but the sub-knobs don't render. Single-commit rollback story still works via the established selective-stage discipline. Bundle delta: JS +3.69 KB (ceiling +5 KB), CSS +2.08 KB (ceiling +2 KB) — both within the LCP.0 spec.
    - **LCP.5** (this commit) — docs sweep. Updates to `docs/DECISIONS.md` (this closing note + status flip), `docs/TASKS.md` (Active Task block + Sub-task table + § LCP backlog rows updated to mark LCP.0 → LCP.5 `[x]` with landing dates), `docs/STATE.md` (5 new "Recent completed work" entries for LCP.1 → LCP.5 + "Next expected milestone" flip), `docs/DESIGN_DOC.md` (LCP track Status: line + as-shipped summary), `docs/KNOWN_ISSUES.md` (new ISSUE-016 for the JSON-validity drift gap on aggressive quants pre-LCP.6 GBNF), and `README.md` (opening tagline tweak from "Ollama" → "local LLM (Ollama or llama.cpp)" + new "Local LLM" section with upstream pointer + AndroScan-specific knob notes per Q5 (C)). No code touched.
  - **Test count delta: 935 → 1030 (+95 new tests across LCP.1 → LCP.4)**, all green at every sub-step boundary. Zero regressions in the existing Ollama / cloud paths — both routed through unchanged code post-LCP.1's pure-refactor sweep.
  - **Bundle delta: JS +3.69 KB, CSS +2.08 KB (LCP.3 + LCP.4 cumulative).** All within LCP.0's ceilings. `tsc --noEmit` clean at every sub-step; `vite build` clean at every sub-step.
  - **As-built deviations from LCP.0's planning estimates:**
    - LCP.2 came in larger than LCP.0's "+8-10 tests" estimate (+34 actual). The new `tests/test_llm_client.py` file landed broader coverage than the planning estimate anticipated — body-shape / SSE / retry / router-dispatch / message-builder all got dedicated test classes rather than the assumed handful of "happy path + JSON mode + retry" tests. Worth the surface — `_complete_llamacpp` is the load-bearing transport for the new local-runtime path and merit-bears the test coverage.
    - LCP.4 came in larger than LCP.0's "+8 tests" estimate (+21 actual). Per-field test classes (base_url / model / max_tokens) each ran 5-6 tests covering YAML / env / env-invalid / live-reload / field-map / round-trip, plus a `TestLlamacppRoundTrip` class for `global_view_from_config` symmetry + `active_model` routing. Mirrors the existing per-knob test patterns in `tests/test_config.py` (`TestOllamaNumCtx` is 6 tests for one knob — same pattern).
    - LCP.4's `SettingsTab.tsx` diff hit +251 LOC (right at the planning-bound 250 LOC trigger). Single-commit decision held — see LCP.4 entry above for the rationale.
    - **Zero deviations from Q1-Q5 lock-in.** Every architectural decision DEC-027 ratified upfront held through implementation. Operator's strict pre-answers translated 1:1 into shipped behaviour: nested `LLM_PROVIDERS` table (Q1 B + table refactor); `response_format: {"type": "json_object"}` JSON-mode parity (Q2 a); top-level radio with nested cloud dropdown (Q3 B); `http://127.0.0.1:8033/v1` default with operator-side `--port 8033` (Q4 A); README pointer only, no `docs/LLAMACPP.md` (Q5 C).
  - **What's still pending — LCP.6 GBNF grammar enforcement (committed follow-up per Q2 (a)):**
    - Emit GBNF grammar from the existing JSON schema for `skill_requests` (discriminated union over the registered skill names, each with their own `params` shape) + `hypotheses` (with optional `widgets[]` post-v2.1.5).
    - The emitter lives in a new `androscan/llm/grammar.py` module (~+200 LOC including emitter + per-skill schema introspection + tests).
    - Forwarded as a per-request `grammar` field to llama.cpp's `/v1/chat/completions` (llama.cpp-specific extension to the OpenAI-compat shim) and as a `format: <json-schema>` payload to Ollama (newer builds supporting JSON-schema mode; fall back to `format: "json"` on older builds — version detection lives in `health_probes.probe_ollama`).
    - **Single LCP.6 vs. LCP.6a + LCP.6b split decision** lives in the LCP.6 planning checkpoint based on the JSON-schema → GBNF emitter scope at that point. If the emitter scope balloons (e.g. needs to handle deeply-nested discriminated unions across multiple skills' response shapes), LCP.6 may itself split.
    - **JSON-validity drift gap captured at LCP.5 as ISSUE-016** in `docs/KNOWN_ISSUES.md` — surfaces the gap honestly for operators running aggressive quants (Q4_K_M / IQ4_XS) on either local provider pre-LCP.6.
  - **Operator setup post-LCP.5** (unchanged from the original DEC-027 follow-up; reproduced here for the closing-note self-containment): operator runs `llama-server -m <gguf> -c 16384 -ngl 99 -fa --port 8033 --host 127.0.0.1 --jinja` against a Q5_K_M / UD-Q5_K_XL Qwen3-family GGUF; flips the **"Local (llama.cpp)"** radio in Settings → Global (or sets `llm.provider: llamacpp` directly in `global_config.yaml`'s `llm:` section); validates via Settings → Status that the LLM card reads green with the "via llama.cpp" extras line; runs a Trace on a known-good cached anchor and compares verdict + plan-list quality against the existing Ollama-shipped baseline. This validation step is operator-side and not part of any LCP sub-step's commit.
- **LCP.6 closing note (2026-05-06) — LCP track fully complete; ISSUE-016 closed:**
  - **6a/6b split decision (per LCP.0 plan): single commit, no split.** The LCP.0 plan's emitter-scope estimate ("+200 LOC including emitter + per-skill schema introspection + tests") assumed `SkillMeta.params_schema` was typed JSON Schema with a per-skill `params` shape that the GBNF would have to discriminate on. The LCP.6 planning checkpoint surveyed the actual `SkillMeta` definitions across all 9 LLM-tier skills (`androscan/skills/*.py`) and confirmed `params_schema` is **operator-prose, not types** (`dict[str, Any]` where the value is a string description for the LLM, not a JSON Schema fragment). The discriminated union is therefore on the `skill` field's enum **only** — a flat list of registered skill names, not a per-skill discriminated union over heterogeneous `params` shapes. The emitter shrank to ~95 LOC of pure-Python (one shared skill-name discovery helper + two parallel emitters: JSON Schema for Ollama, hand-rolled GBNF for llama.cpp). Split criteria from the LCP.0 plan ("if the emitter scope balloons") didn't trigger — the as-built scope is ~5x smaller than the planning estimate.
  - **Architectural shape — both providers, single shared skill-discovery helper:**
    - **`androscan/llm/grammar.py`** (NEW, ~+330 LOC including doc-blocks): `active_skill_names(only_llm_tier=True)` reads the live registry (`androscan.skills.list_llm_skills()`) — sorted, deterministic, idempotent. `build_response_json_schema(skill_names)` emits the Ollama-shaped JSON Schema dict; `build_response_gbnf(skill_names)` emits the llama.cpp-shaped GBNF source string. Both shapes mirror :class:`LLMResponse` (top-level optional `summary` / `skill_requests` / `hypotheses`); the discriminated-union `skill` enum is the only strong constraint, hypothesis items stay permissive (`additionalProperties: True`, no required fields) to match the parser's fail-soft default-fill posture. `is_grammar_enabled(config)` is the single feature-flag helper read by both client branches; `_GBNF_JSON_PRELUDE` carries the standard JSON-value productions (string / number / object / array) lifted from `llama.cpp/grammars/json.gbnf`.
    - **`androscan/llm/client.py`** wiring: per-base-url disabled-set caches (`_OLLAMA_SCHEMA_DISABLED_URLS`, `_LLAMACPP_GRAMMAR_DISABLED_URLS`) and narrow heuristics `_looks_like_schema_error` / `_looks_like_grammar_error` for the opportunistic 400-fallback. `_complete_ollama` swaps `format: "json"` → `format: <schema-dict>` when `Config.local_grammar_enabled` is True and the base_url isn't cached; on HTTP 400 matching the heuristic, the base_url is added to the cache and the request retries within the same loop iteration with the v1-LCP wire shape. `_complete_llamacpp` attaches `grammar: <gbnf-source>` ALONGSIDE the existing `response_format: {"type": "json_object"}` (belt-and-suspenders); 400-fallback drops the `grammar` field. Cloud path is unchanged. Caches are process-lifetime — operators upgrading their runtime get a clean slate on next AndroScan restart.
    - **`Config.local_grammar_enabled: bool = True`** (NEW): single kill-switch under YAML `llm.local_grammar_enabled`, env `ANDROSCAN_LOCAL_GRAMMAR_ENABLED` (accepts the standard truthy/falsy aliases `true`/`1`/`yes`/`on` ↔ `false`/`0`/`no`/`off`). Wired through `CONFIG_FIELD_MAP` / `LIVE_RELOADABLE_FIELDS` / `Config.default()` / `_merge_from_yaml()` / env-override block / final `Config(...)` constructor / `with_overrides()` (with explicit string→bool coercion for the YAML-round-trip path). `coerce_yaml_value` adds it to the `bool_fields` set.
    - **Settings UI** (`SettingsTab.tsx`): the existing `LlmProviderRadio` filters are extended — `local_grammar_enabled` is removed from the Cloud subsection's `llmCloudFields` filter and explicitly rendered under BOTH the "Local (Ollama)" and "Local (llama.cpp)" subsections (it's a cross-local-provider knob; cloud providers ignore it because their OpenAI-compat `response_format` already enforces validity at the SDK layer). No new component types — the existing generic `SettingsField` renderer handles the boolean checkbox.
    - **`global_config.yaml`** doc-block: a commented `llm.local_grammar_enabled: true` block lives at the top of the YAML next to the `llamacpp:` section, with cross-references to `ISSUE-016` and the recommended `llama-server` startup line.
  - **Q2 (a) lock-in fulfilled.** DEC-027's original Q2 lock-in promoted GBNF from "operator-demand-gated" → "committed follow-up" — LCP.6 ships exactly that, on the calendar day promised. Q2's stated benefit ("once GBNF lands in LCP.6, it benefits all local providers — newer Ollama builds support a `format: <json-schema>` mode that achieves the same constraint") materialised: the same `build_response_json_schema` output works for Ollama 0.5.0+ (Dec 2024 release) AND informs the GBNF derivation for llama.cpp. Operators running aggressive quants (Q4_K_M / IQ4_XS) regain reliable structured-JSON output because the model literally cannot emit off-schema tokens at the logits-sampling level.
  - **Test count delta: 1030 → 1092 (+62 new tests in LCP.6)**, all green.
    - 38 new in `tests/test_grammar.py` — `TestActiveSkillNames` (3), `TestBuildResponseJsonSchema` (15), `TestBuildResponseGbnf` (9), `TestParserInvariance` (6), `TestIsGrammarEnabled` (5).
    - 11 new in `tests/test_llm_client.py` — `TestOllamaSchemaFormatMode` (6), `TestLlamacppGrammarMode` (5). The 400-fallback tests use a deep-copy capture (`_capturing_post`) because the retry path mutates the SAME `payload` dict in place via `payload.pop("grammar", None)`.
    - 13 new in `tests/test_config.py::TestLocalGrammarEnabled` — default + YAML + env (with truthy/falsy alias coverage) + invalid-env warn-and-fall-back + `with_overrides` coercion + `global_view_from_config` round-trip + `LIVE_RELOADABLE_FIELDS` / `CONFIG_FIELD_MAP` membership.
    - Cumulative LCP track tests delta: 935 (pre-LCP) → 1092 (post-LCP.6) = **+157 new tests across LCP.0 → LCP.6**.
  - **Bundle delta: JS +0.40 KB cumulative against pre-LCP baseline; CSS unchanged.** Post-LCP.6 production bundle: `index-*.js` 1235.53 KB / gzip 384.66 KB (was 1235.13 KB / gzip 384.40 KB at LCP.5); `index-*.css` 93.42 KB / gzip 15.71 KB (unchanged from LCP.5 — no new CSS rules added in LCP.6). The single new render path (`local_grammar_enabled` rendered under both local subsections) reuses the existing `SettingsField` component so the diff is import-free. `tsc --noEmit` clean; `vite build` clean.
  - **As-built deviations from LCP.0 estimates (LCP.6 specifically):**
    - LCP.0's "+15-20 tests" estimate for `tests/test_grammar.py` came in at +38 — broader coverage in line with the LCP.2 / LCP.4 over-test pattern (per-emitter test classes covering shape / well-formedness / parser-invariance / fallback-skills-empty edge case rather than a handful of "happy path + falsy" tests). Worth the surface — the grammar emitter is the load-bearing constraint shape for the v1 LCP local providers and merits the test coverage.
    - LCP.0's "+200 LOC" estimate for `androscan/llm/grammar.py` came in at +330 — entirely doc-block weight (the per-rule rationale comments matter more for a load-bearing module than on a minor wire-shape helper). Pure-Python LOC excluding doc-blocks is ~115.
    - **No deviation from the LCP.0 emitter design lock-in.** The actual emitter ships the two parallel constraint shapes the LCP.0 plan promised (JSON Schema for Ollama, GBNF for llama.cpp), with the same registry-driven skill-name discovery, the same opportunistic-fallback posture, and the same per-base-url process-lifetime cache.
    - **Frontend touch was unplanned but trivial.** LCP.6's planning checkpoint didn't anticipate the Settings UI tweak; the auto-grouped `FormGlobal` would have hidden the new `local_grammar_enabled` field under the Cloud subsection (its YAML home is `llm.*`). The fix was ~30 lines of TypeScript splitting the field out of the cloud filter and rendering it under both local subsections — within LCP.0's bundle ceilings.
  - **Operator-visible behaviour change.** Pre-LCP.6: a Q4_K_M Qwen3 instance running through llama.cpp could emit a syntactically-valid JSON object whose `skill_requests[0].skill` contained a typo'd name like `"query_calligraph"` or whose `hypotheses[0].exploitability` was `7` (out of 1-5 range); the parser would fail-soft, the workflow loop would retry, and the operator would see "the LLM is acting weird today" in `run.log`. Post-LCP.6: the model's logits-sampler can ONLY emit one of the 9 registered skill names + integers in 1-5 — typos / off-range values are impossible at the sampling level. Q4_K_M / IQ4_XS regain operator-usability.
  - **No `docs/LLAMACPP.md` per Q5 (C) lock-in.** The README.md's "Local LLM" section + this DEC + the inline doc-blocks in `androscan/llm/grammar.py` collectively cover the operator-side setup story. No standalone doc — the LCP.0 plan's rationale held: a one-page MD would duplicate `llama.cpp/README.md` upstream content or rapidly stale.
  - **What landed; what didn't:** ✅ GBNF emitter for llama.cpp (per Q2 (a)). ✅ JSON-schema mode for Ollama (per Q2 (a) cross-provider benefit). ✅ Single-commit ship (no 6a/6b split). ✅ `ISSUE-016` closed — moved to Resolved section. ❌ Nothing skipped or deferred — LCP.6 was the **terminal sub-step of the LCP track as originally scoped**. Subsequent operator dogfooding produced two follow-ups: a YAML-schema cleanup commit (DEC-028) and an LCP.7 model-sourcing UX sub-step opened separately.

### DEC-028: LCP follow-up — `global_config.yaml` restructure (consolidate LLM config under `llm:`, rename `model` → `default_model`, hard-break migration)

- status: **Active** (landed 2026-05-06, post-LCP.6)
- context:

  Operator dogfooding the day after LCP.6 landed produced three orthogonal pieces of feedback that bundled cleanly into a single ship vehicle:

  1. **`global_config.yaml` had grown disjointed.** Three top-level LLM-related sections (`ollama:`, `llamacpp:`, the implicit `llm:` parent for `provider` / `cloud_*` / `local_grammar_enabled`) lived as siblings of each other and of unrelated subsystems (`paths:`, `workflow:`, `output:`, `web:`, `rag:`, `frida:`, `trace:`). The sibling-fanout violated the conceptual unity introduced by LCP.1's `LLM_PROVIDERS` registry — operators reading the YAML end-to-end couldn't see "all the LLM knobs are this nest" at a glance. Operator quote: "looks disjointed and all over the place."
  2. **`model:` key was ambiguous.** Both `ollama.model` and `llamacpp.model` carried the operator-facing meaning "the default chat / analysis model used unless a per-app override fires." The bare `model:` label didn't communicate the "default" semantics, which mattered more once Phase 8 per-app `chat.model_override` made overriding routine. Operator request: rename `model` → `default_model` in both provider sections.
  3. **The `llama-server` startup line in `global_config.yaml` and `README.md` was wrong.** The recommended invocation included a bare `-fa` flag, but recent `llama-server` builds require an explicit `on` / `off` / `auto` value for `-fa`; bare `-fa` causes the parser to consume the next CLI arg (`--port`) as the flash-attn value and crash with `unknown value for --flash-attn: '--port'`. Operator hit this verbatim and shared a screenshot.

  Bundling all three into a single commit was the right call because the YAML restructure is a hard-break migration (the loader stops reading the old shape), and shipping the rename + `-fa on` correctness fix in the same commit means operators only restart their workflow once.

- decision:

  Three structural changes plus three "cheap wins" the operator dropped in flight:

  - **Y1. Consolidate LLM config under a single `llm:` parent (Option B in operator triage; the alternative was a flat sibling-fanout retained as-is).** Final shape:
    ```yaml
    llm:
      provider: "ollama"
      local_grammar_enabled: true
      ollama:
        base_url: "http://localhost:11434"
        default_model: "qwen3.6:27b"
        ...
      llamacpp:
        base_url: "http://127.0.0.1:8033/v1"
        default_model: "unsloth/gemma-4-E4B-it-GGUF:Q8_K_XL"
        ...
      cloud:
        model: ""
        api_key: ""
        temperature: 0.2
    ```
    Mirrors the LCP.1 `LLM_PROVIDERS` registry shape (`local.{ollama, llamacpp}` + `cloud.{...}`); operators now have a single visual anchor for all LLM-related knobs.

  - **Y2. Rename YAML keys `model` → `default_model` in both `llm.ollama:` and `llm.llamacpp:` sub-sections.** Flat `Config.ollama_model` / `Config.llamacpp_model` field names preserved unchanged (no churn in the in-process consumer surface — every analysis / chat / pipeline call continues to read `cfg.ollama_model` regardless of the YAML key it came from). Cloud key `cloud_model` similarly maps to `llm.cloud.model` on disk.

  - **Y3. Hard-break migration (no backwards-compat read for the pre-DEC-028 flat shape).** Operator preference, ratified explicitly: "Agreed to all you recommendations." Justified by single-operator context — there's no fleet of installs to coordinate, and the Settings UI's "Reset to defaults" path writes the new shape in one click. A backwards-compat reader would have doubled `_merge_from_yaml`'s surface area for ~zero ongoing benefit. Operators upgrading mid-LCP simply hit Reset once or hand-edit the YAML.

  - **Y4. Default model bumps:** `Config.default().ollama_model` flipped from `qwen3.5:35b` → `qwen3.6:27b` (smaller working set fits a 36 GB M3 unified-memory budget without thrashing — same hardware constraint that motivated the LCP track in the first place). `Config.default().llamacpp_model` flipped from empty string → `unsloth/gemma-4-E4B-it-GGUF:Q8_K_XL` (the operator's known-good GGUF spec; matches the recommended `-hf` arg in the new YAML's startup line). Both are pure-default changes — the YAML / env override paths still take precedence per the established loader posture.

  - **Y5. `llama-server` recommended invocation update (correctness + cheap perf wins, both in `global_config.yaml` doc-comments and `README.md`):**
    - `-fa` → `-fa on` (correctness fix — the operator-reported crash).
    - `+ --no-webui` (disable llama-server's bundled web UI; AndroScan doesn't use it; reduces process surface).
    - `+ --cache-reuse 256` (KV-cache prefix reuse — free perf win on AndroScan's stable system-prompt prefix; AndroScan re-sends the same skill-catalog system message on every turn).

  - **Y6. `probe_llamacpp` switched from `GET /v1/models` to a two-step probe — `GET {server}/health` for liveness, then `GET {base}/models` for the model list on success.** `/health` is purpose-built for liveness, returns smaller responses, and explicitly surfaces the "loading model" cold-start state (`{"status": "loading model"}`, HTTP 503) which the previous `/v1/models`-only probe could only infer from an empty `data: []` list. The Settings UI's three-state classification (`_DOWN` / `_PARTIAL_UP` / `_UP`) gets cleaner inputs.

  - **LCP.7 deferred (operator agreed).** A 3-way model-sourcing UX in Settings UI (HuggingFace cache via `llama-server -cl` / HuggingFace Hub via `-hf <repo>:<quant>` — needs `HF_TOKEN` for private repos / Local filesystem via `-m <path>`) is a meaningful LOC investment that doesn't fit DEC-028's hygiene-and-correctness scope. Opened as LCP.7 in `TASKS.md` as a planning-checkpoint-pending stub; HF Hub auth dependency noted operator-side.

- alternatives considered:

  - **Alt-A: keep flat siblings but tighten doc-comments.** Rejected — operator explicitly asked for restructure; doc-only fix would have left the underlying disjoint shape that motivated the request.
  - **Alt-B: nest under `llm:` AND keep a backwards-compat reader for the old flat shape.** Rejected — single-operator context means the migration cost is one click + one line edit; the doubled surface area in `_merge_from_yaml` (and the parallel double-sourced behaviour in `effective_sources` / `dump_to_yaml`) outweighs the benefit. Hard-break is simpler and stays simpler over time.
  - **Alt-C: rename `model` → `default_model` only in operator-facing UX (Settings UI label) without changing the YAML key.** Rejected — keeps the on-disk YAML out of sync with the UI label, and operators frequently hand-edit the YAML; the rename has to land at the same time on both surfaces or it's a pointless half-step.
  - **Alt-D: keep `-fa` (no value) in the recommended startup line and document the version-pinning requirement.** Rejected — the version-pinning would silently rot as `llama.cpp` upstream evolves; explicit `-fa on` is correct against the current parser and forward-compatible.

- consequences:

  - **Hard-break for any pre-DEC-028 `global_config.yaml` on disk.** Operators upgrading past commit `<hash>` hit either a "Reset to defaults" click or a one-paragraph hand-edit (the rename is mechanical: `ollama:` → `llm.ollama:` + `model:` → `default_model:`; same for `llamacpp:` and `cloud_*`). Acceptable in the single-operator context; would be a real migration cost in a fleet deployment.
  - **`CONFIG_FIELD_MAP` `section` field is now a logical UI label, not a YAML path.** A new helper `_yaml_path_for_section()` translates the flat label to the on-disk nested path. The Settings UI's grouping logic (`data.field_map[field].section`) stays oblivious to the YAML shape change — `meta.section === "ollama"` still works for the Ollama subsection.
  - **`global_view_from_config` return shape widened to a deep dict.** The frontend `GlobalSettingsResponse.global` type widened from `Record<string, Record<string, unknown>>` to `Record<string, unknown>` to accommodate the nested `llm.ollama` / `llm.llamacpp` / `llm.cloud` sub-sections. Form-mode rendering doesn't read from this field (it uses `flat` / `field_map`), so the type widening is cosmetic; the DiagnosticsPanel still JSON-stringifies the full shape unchanged.
  - **`probe_llamacpp` now makes two HTTP calls on the happy path (`/health` then `/v1/models`).** Both are loopback, sub-millisecond; no operator-visible latency regression. The probe's URL field still reports the `/v1/models` endpoint so the status-card "open in browser" link stays useful.
  - **Test surface impact: ~20 fixture rewrites + 2 new probe tests + 1 new default-value assertion.** All landed inside the same atomic commit. Total test count: 1093 (was 1092 pre-DEC-028; +1 net because the new partial-up `/health` loading test added a state the old single-call probe couldn't distinguish).
  - **`ISSUE-016` reference in `README.md` updated** — was "wait for the LCP.6 GBNF grammar enforcement follow-up"; now references the resolved state with `llm.local_grammar_enabled: false` as the kill-switch for the rare runtime that doesn't honour grammars.

- as-built notes (post-ratification, single-commit ship):

  - **Bundle delta: JS effectively unchanged** (within rounding; the Settings UI changes were ~10 LOC of TypeScript that don't introduce new components). CSS unchanged.
  - **Test count: 1092 → 1093** (+1 net; +2 new probe tests for the `/health` loading state and the `/health`-ok-but-`/v1/models`-empty edge case; -1 because the previous `_partial_up` test split into the two new ones; +1 for the new default-model assertion in `TestLlamacppModel`).
  - **Frontend touch trivial.** `LLM_OWNED_SECTIONS` set extended with `"cloud"`; `LlmProviderRadio`'s `llmCloudFields` resolution flipped from filtering `grouped["llm"]` to reading `grouped["cloud"]` directly. The `GlobalSettingsResponse.global` type widened. `tsc --noEmit` clean.
  - **No deviation from the agreed scope.** Every Y1-Y6 lock-in landed exactly as ratified above; LCP.7 stays an opened-but-unplanned sub-step in `TASKS.md`.

### DEC-029: Phase 13 — Behavior Trace v3 (Dynamic Execution + Flowchart UI overhaul)
- status: **Active** *(planning checkpoint ratified 2026-05-08; **v1 complete 2026-05-08** — all sub-steps 13.0 → 13.10 landed within a single calendar day; see closing note at end of entry)*
- date: 2026-05-08
- owners: (project)
- context:
  Phase 11 v2 (DEC-025; complete 2026-04-30) closed ISSUE-013 with bounded inter-procedural slicing; v2.1 (2026-05-05) closed the entry-method discoverability gap with `ClassMethodTree` + coalescer + chat-widget pattern. Phase 12 / DEC-026 was reserved for the operator-demand-gated *static-analysis precision* follow-ups from DEC-025's deferral list (ISSUE-014, auto-verify, free-form LLM JS, per-app TTL, iOS adapters, Smali patching, LLM-budget real-app measurement) and is still **deferred-pending-telemetry** — that scope is unchanged by this DEC.

  Operator dogfooding 2026-05-07 → 2026-05-08 surfaced a different friction signal that is **orthogonal** to Phase 12's static-precision menu and large enough to warrant its own phase rather than being bundled into Phase 12:

  1. **The current "Decision Timeline" is unintuitive for pentesters who aren't strong developers.** The linear top-down list of `DecisionPointCard`s with `BranchOutcomeBadge` showing `true NEUTRAL` / `false NEUTRAL` on every card, `Predicate registers v3, p0`, and Smali instruction indices `#11` / `#31` reads as low-level static-analysis output, not as an operator-actionable view of "what does this method do and where can I bypass it." Operator quote: "the Decision Timeline section is very un-intuitive to an operator who is a pentester but not necessarily with strong development and coding knowledge."
  2. **Static analysis alone can't tell the operator what *actually* fired at runtime.** Operators repeatedly asked "is this start-to-finish?" / "which of these methods actually run when I tap the Login button?" — questions only a runtime trace can answer. The existing per-card `Verify with runtime trace` button (Phase 11 v2.1 / DEC-025) only stages a single `entry_exit_log` hook against one method; there is no path from "I have a `BehaviorAnchor` of 5 methods" to "show me which 4 of the 5 actually fired and what arguments they got."
  3. **Method names alone don't communicate intent.** Operators reading a flowchart of `validate_pin` / `check_active_session` / `create_session` want a one-line "what does this do" without round-tripping to the chat dock or the Code Browser. Phase 11 v2.1's chat dock answers this for one method at a time; an operator triaging a 5-method trace wants 5 summaries inline.

  This DEC ratifies **Phase 13 — Behavior Trace v3 — Dynamic Execution + Flowchart UI overhaul** as a new phase ahead of Phase 12 (per operator direction: "Ignore phase 12 stuff for now, we'll come to it later after phase 13"). Phase 12's static-precision menu stays deferred-pending-telemetry; Phase 13 ratifies a parallel, complementary direction (dynamic execution + UX overhaul) that sits on top of the v2 / v2.1 static substrate without disturbing it.

  The locked design is the output of a 2026-05-07 → 2026-05-08 plan-mode iteration over the WeakBank `MainActivity.login(…)` example, including three rounds of edge-styling refinement and an explicit "lock the design" ratification.
- decision:

  **Three coherent deliverables** ship across a strictly-linear 11-sub-step backlog (13.0 → 13.10, mirroring Phase 11's 11.0 → 11.7 cadence):

  1. **UI overhaul — rename "Decision Timeline" → "Behavior Trace"; replace linear timeline with a flowchart; add Inspector pane.** The flowchart is a top-down DAG (nodes = methods, edges = return-value branches) laid out via the same DAG-positioning math used in the canvas mockup. Inspector pane on the right (fixed 360px) opens on node click and replaces the cluttered timeline cards with five operator-actionable sections: signature, summary (LLM, cached), source line, predicate origin, bypass plans.

  2. **Dynamic execution trace — multi-method Frida tracer template + tiered arg/return serialiser + WebSocket multiplexing.** A new `behavior_trace_multi` hook template hooks every method in the active `BehaviorAnchor`'s closure (subject to the threshold-color cap), captures entry/exit events with a per-thread depth counter, stringifies arguments and return values via a tiered helper (primitives → reflection over user-defined classes → truncated for long strings / byte arrays), and streams trace events over the existing `/ws/trace` WebSocket alongside new `summary_pending` / `summary_ready` / `summary_failed` SSE-style events for live-on-first-hit LLM summaries. JSONL persistence at `apps/<app_id>/<run>/dynamic_trace.jsonl` mirrors the existing Frida session log shape.

  3. **Per-method LLM summaries — live-on-first-hit, cached in `skill_results_cache`.** New `summarise_method` skill (`requires_confirmation=False`, read-only) takes a method's Smali signature + decompiled source + 2-3 lines of context and emits a one-paragraph summary. Cache key = `(app_sha, class_smali, method_name, descriptor)` so summaries persist across runs and across cache schema bumps. Speculative pre-summarisation of every method in a static `BehaviorAnchor` is **deferred to a later phase** (v1 only summarises methods that fire dynamically OR are explicitly clicked in the Inspector).

  **Locked design details from the 2026-05-08 plan-mode iteration** (these are committed, not open questions):

  - **Naming:** "Decision Timeline" → "Behavior Trace" (top-level section in Lab → Trace mode); "Execution Flow" — flowchart sub-component; "Inspector" — right-side detail pane; "Bypass plans" — unchanged.
  - **Three modes** with one toggle row: `Static` (default before any dynamic run; full DAG, allow/deny sinks colored, candidate gates ringed yellow, suspected-inlined dashed), `Dynamic` (untaken branches dashed at 55% opacity in gray; fired path in accent blue), `Both` (default after first dynamic run; untaken keep verdict color at low opacity; fired path accent blue). Mode + selected node persist via `useCanvasState` in the mockup, via `localStorage` in production.
  - **Edge styling — color-only emphasis, no stroke-width-or-arrowhead-size variation.** All edges render at the same 1.5px stroke and same 6×6 arrowhead. Fired (taken) edges = accent blue, full opacity, solid. Untaken edges in `dynamic` mode = gray, dashed, 55% opacity. Untaken edges in `both` mode = verdict color (allow=green, deny=red) at 55% opacity, dashed. Static-only mode = verdict color, full opacity, solid. **This was iterated three times in plan-mode** ("emphasise" → "blue arrow looks fine, but don't make it thick" → final color-only spec); the lock is intentional and worth documenting so a future contributor doesn't re-thicken the fired path.
  - **Node design:** 220×72px, 6px radius. Title (+ ` × N` for overloaded). Live values (`pin="1234" → false`) on a second line in dynamic/both, in accent color. Source line on the bottom (`MainActivity.kt:53`), monospace, tertiary. Corner pill: `ALLOW` / `DENY` (sinks), `GATE` (candidate gates). Stacked-card visual for `overloadedCount > 1`. Dashed border + `inlined?` corner label when R8 is suspected (detected by `try/catch` around `Java.use()` in the Frida script).
  - **Inspector content:** signature; live observation (only in dynamic/both, only if fired); summary (LLM, cached, `Generating…` while pending); source; predicate origin (bold one-liner + secondary detail paragraph); bypass plans (per-risk pill + title); `Possibly inlined` callout; action row `[Hook this method] [Trace this gate] [Open source]`.
  - **Explicitly removed from primary UI** (can resurface behind an `Advanced` toggle in a future sub-step if asked): "true NEUTRAL" / "false NEUTRAL" verdict badges (only `ALLOW`/`DENY` show; rationale: `neutral` is the dominant heuristic outcome and operators correctly inferred it as noise); predicate registers (`v3`, `p0`); Smali `#instruction_index` (`#11`, `#31`); Smali labels (`cond_0`, `cond_2`); replaced by source line + return-value branch labels.
  - **Run-trace button — threshold-colored by hook count.** ≤ 20 hooks = green `▶ Run dynamic trace`; 21-50 = yellow `· 34 hooks`; 51-100 = orange `· 78 hooks (slower)`; > 100 = red `· 142 hooks · device may stutter`. Hook count = unique `(class, method)` pairs in the active `BehaviorAnchor`'s closure.
  - **LLM summaries policy:** live-on-first-hit for methods that fire dynamically OR are explicitly clicked in Inspector; cached in `skill_results_cache.py` keyed on `(app_sha, class_smali, method_name, descriptor)`; multiplexed on the existing trace WebSocket as `summary_pending` (fires immediately on first observation) / `summary_ready` (fires when LLM completes) / `summary_failed` (fires on LLM error; UI shows "Summary unavailable" + retry button).
  - **Out of scope for v1 (revisit in 13.x or in a follow-up DEC):** marching-ants animation on fired path; branch-outcome inference from dynamic data (verdicts stay heuristic per Phase 11 v2); speculative pre-summarisation of every static method; separate "Tree of calls" panel — for v1, the flowchart IS the tree; mutation hooks (modify return values mid-run) — bypass plans still emit Stage → Inject → re-run flow.
  - **`BehaviorAnchor` / `DecisionPoint` / `BypassPlan` / `PredicateOrigin` schemas unchanged** (no schema bump on `trace.sqlite`; `SCHEMA_VERSION` stays at `"2"` from Phase 11 v2). New persistence at `apps/<app_id>/<run>/dynamic_trace.jsonl` for dynamic events; new persistence is purely additive and lives outside `trace.sqlite`.
  - **Canvas mockup at `~/.cursor/projects/<workspace>/canvases/phase-13-trace-mockup.canvas.tsx`** is the canonical reference for the locked design; the production frontend must visually match the mockup's three modes, edge styling, node design, and Inspector content within the limits of React-on-DOM vs. the canvas SDK's SVG-via-`computeDAGLayout` substrate.

  **Sub-step ordering** (strictly linear, one sub-step per Agent-mode session, mirrors Phase 11's 11.0 → 11.7 + Phase 11 v2.1's v2.1.0 → v2.1.6 cadence):

  - **13.0** — Planning checkpoint + DEC-029 + canvas mockup + docs sweep (this commit; docs + canvas only).
  - **13.1** — Multi-method Frida tracer template + tiered arg/return serialiser (BE+template; no UI surface yet).
  - **13.2** — `POST /api/trace/{app_id}/dynamic` endpoint + JSONL persistence (BE; route returns session_id + tails the JSONL on subsequent polls).
  - **13.3** — WebSocket multiplexing: extend `/ws/trace` to carry `summary_pending` / `summary_ready` / `summary_failed` events alongside the existing trace events (BE; additive event vocabulary).
  - **13.4** — `summarise_method` LLM skill (BE+LLM; cached in `skill_results_cache`).
  - **13.5** — Frontend rename "Decision Timeline" → "Behavior Trace"; demote `DecisionTimeline.tsx` into legacy code path behind a feature flag (FE-only; pure rename, no behavior change).
  - **13.6** — `ExecutionFlow` component (FE; replaces `DecisionTimeline` in active code path; ports the canvas mockup's DAG-positioning math via a small DAG-layout helper or a thin React Flow wrapper).
  - **13.7** — `Inspector` pane with predicate origin + bypass plans + live observation slot (FE; shares state with `ExecutionFlow`).
  - **13.8** — Mode toggle (Static / Dynamic / Both) + dynamic overlay + per-thread depth visualization (FE; consumes the WebSocket events from 13.3).
  - **13.9** — Threshold-colored run-trace button + `inlined?` detection + chat-widget renderer for `summarise_method` summaries (FE+BE; `inlined?` ships via a `try/catch` around `Java.use()` in the 13.1 tracer).
  - **13.10** — Docs sweep + DEC-029 closing note + screenshots (docs only; closes Phase 13).

  **Brief Ask-mode planning checkpoint before 13.1** to lock the multi-method tracer JS template shape (per-method `Java.use()` failure handling, depth-counter implementation, arg/return serialiser tier ordering) before the template ships. Mirrors the 10.6 `BehaviorAnchor` JSON wire-shape checkpoint and the 11.4 `_DescentBudget` checkpoint.
- rationale:
  - **Why a new Phase 13 instead of folding this into Phase 12 / DEC-026?** Operator direction was explicit ("Ignore phase 12 stuff for now, we'll come to it later after phase 13"). The two scopes are also genuinely orthogonal: Phase 12 is *more precision* on the static substrate (ISSUE-014's per-overload tuple, free-form LLM JS, iOS adapters, etc.); Phase 13 is *dynamic execution* + *UX overhaul* layered on top of the unchanged v2 / v2.1 static substrate. Bundling them would couple two independent investigations to the same release calendar and inflate the v3 ratification surface.
  - **Why flowchart over linear timeline?** Three plan-mode rounds of operator feedback on the existing UX. The linear list of `DecisionPointCard`s communicates "list of conditional branches" but not "call flow" — an operator looking at a 5-method `BehaviorAnchor` wants to see the *shape* of the call tree (where does it fork, where do the deny / allow sinks live) before drilling into any one decision. The flowchart shape matches the operator's mental model directly. The `computeDAGLayout` helper in the canvas SDK proves the layout math is small enough to port to a React Flow / hand-rolled SVG production frontend without dragging in a heavy graph layout library.
  - **Why color-only edge emphasis with same stroke + arrowhead?** The plan-mode iteration explicitly tested thicker fired edges and a larger arrowhead and the operator rejected both ("Blue arrow looks fine, but don't make it thick"). The reasoning is operator-aesthetic: visual hierarchy via color + opacity is sufficient signal in a 5-30 node graph; varying stroke widths and arrowhead sizes adds noise without adding information. **This is the kind of design lock that's easy to undo accidentally** — a future contributor reading the production code without this DEC could re-introduce the thick-fired-arrow pattern thinking it's an improvement; flagging the iteration history here keeps the decision sticky.
  - **Why live-on-first-hit summaries instead of speculative pre-summarisation?** Pre-summarising every method in a `BehaviorAnchor` would burn LLM budget on methods the operator may never look at; the typical investigation flow is "fire the dynamic trace, see what fired, click on the surprising / suspicious ones." Live-on-first-hit keeps the LLM cost proportional to operator attention. The cache (keyed on app SHA + method) survives across runs so subsequent investigations of the same app don't re-summarise.
  - **Why multiplex summaries on the existing trace WebSocket instead of a new channel?** Operators already expect events to flow over `/ws/trace` during a dynamic run; adding a parallel `/ws/summaries` channel doubles the connection-management surface without operator-visible benefit. The event-kind vocabulary is additive (DEC-022 / Phase 11 v2.1 / `widget` events established the precedent); old clients ignoring `summary_*` events fail-soft.
  - **Why threshold-colored run-trace button instead of a hard cap?** Operators on a strong device can hook 200+ methods without measurable lag; operators on a phone over USB-tethering can stutter at 30. The threshold colors make the cost visible without preventing the run; the operator decides. Hard caps would frustrate power users; no cap would surprise the unprepared.
  - **Why preserve the `BehaviorAnchor` schema unchanged?** Phase 11 v2.1's chat-widget pattern + Phase 11 v2's slicer are the load-bearing static substrate; changing their schemas to accommodate dynamic data would invalidate every cached anchor and ripple through Phase 11 v2.1's plumbing. Keeping the dynamic data in a parallel JSONL file (and the live event stream) lets Phase 13 ship without re-validating the v2 / v2.1 schema discipline.
- alternatives considered:
  - **Bundle Phase 12 + Phase 13 into a single v3 release** (rejected per operator direction): would couple two orthogonal investigations to the same calendar; mixed-scope DECs are harder to read and harder to roll back than focused DECs. The DEC-024 → DEC-025 → DEC-029 sequence keeps each phase's scope crisp.
  - **Cytoscape for the flowchart** (rejected): Cytoscape excels at large directed graphs (Manual Hooks's call-graph pane uses it for graphs of 1000+ nodes); the trace flowchart is typically 5-30 nodes where DAG layout + click-to-open-Inspector is a better fit. The canvas SDK's `computeDAGLayout` is purpose-built for this case and the production frontend can either reuse the same math or wrap a small React Flow component.
  - **React Flow as the production substrate from day one** (deferred decision; revisit at 13.6): React Flow is the obvious choice for a hand-built DAG editor with custom nodes, edges, and a minimap; the canvas mockup uses raw SVG via `computeDAGLayout` because the canvas SDK doesn't ship React Flow. The 13.6 sub-step planning checkpoint will pick between (a) hand-rolled SVG via a small DAG-layout helper ported from the mockup, (b) React Flow with custom nodes/edges. Both are viable; the mockup proves the layout math is small.
  - **Speculative pre-summarisation of every method in a `BehaviorAnchor`** (rejected for v1): would burn LLM budget on methods the operator may never look at. Live-on-first-hit + cache keeps the cost proportional to operator attention. Revisit if dogfood shows operators routinely click every method in every Inspector — i.e. the cache hit rate on first-open is consistently high.
  - **Mutation hooks (modify return values mid-run)** (rejected for v1): out of scope; a different workflow class (operator wants to *test* a bypass, not just *observe* it) and would require a consent-class skill and a re-run UX. Bypass plans still emit Stage → Inject → re-run for v1; revisit if dogfood shows a measurable gap.
  - **Branch-outcome inference from dynamic data** (rejected for v1): would let dynamic execution refine the heuristic verdict (e.g. a "neutral" branch that fires consistently as deny on real input gets re-classified). Out of scope for v1 because the static heuristic verdicts are already operator-actionable enough; revisit when 13.x telemetry shows a measurable gap.
  - **Marching-ants animation on the fired path** (rejected for v1): would visually communicate "live execution" but adds chartjunk; the static-color emphasis is enough. Revisit if dogfood shows operators don't immediately recognize the fired path.
  - **Separate "Tree of calls" panel** (rejected for v1): the flowchart IS the tree; a separate panel would duplicate information. Revisit if dogfood shows operators want a list-mode view of the same data.
- tradeoffs / consequences:
  - **`DecisionTimeline.tsx` (Phase 11 v2's primary component) is demoted in 13.5 and replaced by `ExecutionFlow.tsx` in 13.6.** The legacy timeline stays behind a feature flag for one release in case operators want the old view; the flag is removed in 13.10 alongside the docs sweep. **Net frontend LOC:** likely +500-800 in `ExecutionFlow` + `Inspector` + helpers; -200-400 in `DecisionTimeline` + `DecisionCard` (legacy code path stays one release; removed at 13.10). Bundle delta probably +5-10 KB JS / +3-5 KB CSS — within the LCP.0 ceiling reused across phases.
  - **Multi-method Frida tracer adds a new template (`behavior_trace_multi`) and a new arg/return serialiser helper (~150-200 LOC of JS).** The serialiser is the primary risk surface — reflection over user-defined Java classes can hit `IllegalAccessException` on private fields, `NullPointerException` on uninitialised fields, and OOM on circular object graphs. Mitigated by: (a) per-field `try/catch` so one failure doesn't poison the whole event, (b) depth cap (default 1) and field cap (default 8), (c) string truncation at 256 chars, (d) byte-array detection + length-only summary for arrays > 32 bytes, (e) explicit `behavior_trace_multi.tests/` smoke fixtures covering the common Java types (Bundle, Intent, primitives, Strings, byte arrays, custom classes, null fields).
  - **Per-thread depth counter and sequence numbers add per-event metadata.** Mitigated by the existing `TraceEvent` model in `androscan/adapters/frida_client.py` having a flexible payload field; the new metadata lives there. JSONL persistence shape adds two fields per event (`thread_depth`, `seq`) that the frontend uses to reconstruct the call tree.
  - **Live-on-first-hit summaries add a second long-running task during a dynamic run.** Bounded by: (a) `skill_results_cache` so each method is summarised at most once per app SHA, (b) per-method timeout (5s default; matches DEC-022's per-skill timeout), (c) the `summary_failed` event lets the UI fail-soft if the LLM is slow / unavailable. Worst case: dynamic trace fires 30 unique methods, summariser fires 30 LLM calls in parallel — bounded by `MAX_SKILLS_PER_TURN = 3` from DEC-022 (the agentic loop pattern), so summaries land 3-at-a-time, with the UI showing `Generating…` for the rest until they arrive.
  - **WebSocket event vocabulary grows by 3 new event kinds (`summary_pending`, `summary_ready`, `summary_failed`).** Additive — old clients ignore unknown kinds (the chat-widget pattern from Phase 11 v2.1 established this discipline). No schema bump on `trace.sqlite`.
  - **`inlined?` detection ships in the 13.1 tracer template via a `try/catch` around each `Java.use()` call.** Methods where `Java.use()` succeeds but `Java.use(...).method.implementation = ...` raises `JavaMethodNotFoundException` get tagged `inlined: true` and surfaced in the Inspector with a `Possibly inlined` callout. False-positive rate is low (R8 inlining is the dominant cause; private methods with no Smali reference are also possible but rare in practice).
  - **The locked design intentionally drops several power-user diagnostics** (predicate registers, instruction indices, neutral verdict badges). If a power user asks for them back, the right home is a "Show advanced details" toggle in the Inspector — single-component change, low cost. **NOT** in v1 scope.
  - **One transitional doc state during 13.0 → 13.10** where DEC-029 is Active and Phase 13 sub-bullets accrete in `STATE.md` sub-step-by-sub-step. Same pattern as Phase 10's 10.0 → 10.8, Phase 11's 11.0 → 11.7, Phase 11 v2.1's v2.1.0 → v2.1.6, and LCP track's LCP.0 → LCP.6. If it becomes confusing in practice, capture as a follow-up sweep.
  - **Phase 12 / DEC-026 stays untouched.** This DEC does not redefine, defer-further, or pull-forward any of Phase 12's static-precision menu items. After Phase 13 ships, the operator may revisit Phase 12's deferral list with the benefit of dynamic-trace telemetry — but that is a Phase 12 conversation, not a Phase 13 commitment.

#### v1 closing note (appended 2026-05-08 at sub-step 13.10)

**Phase 13 v1 shipped 2026-05-08** — all eleven sub-steps **13.0 → 13.10** landed within a single calendar day across rapid back-to-back per-sub-step Agent-mode sessions, mirroring the Phase 11 v2.1 / LCP track / DEC-028 cadence. **+177 net new tests** end-to-end (test count baseline at the 13.0 planning checkpoint was 1113 post-DEC-028 / v2.1.8; HEAD at 13.10 is **1290** after 13.1 / 13.2 / 13.3 / 13.4 / 13.9 BE deltas; 13.5 / 13.6 / 13.7 / 13.8 / 13.10 were FE-or-docs-only). **Cumulative bundle delta over 13.5 → 13.10:** CSS +~32 KB raw / +~5 KB gzipped (was 95.29 KB → 127.16 KB; the +~150 KB JS bump from `@xyflow/react` in 13.6 dominates the JS delta — final 1455.67 KB raw / 454.00 KB gzipped, well within the cumulative bundle ceiling reused across phases). **Zero deviations from the locked DEC-029 design** (more on this below).

**As-built record per sub-step:** **13.0** (planning checkpoint + DEC-029 + canvas mockup at `phase-13-trace-mockup.canvas.tsx` — docs + canvas only). **13.1** (multi-method `behavior_trace_multi` Frida template + 11-tier arg/return serialiser + per-thread depth counter + `inlined?` detection via `Java.use()` `try/catch`; +45 BE tests + 5 parametrised tests in the existing fail-closed registry walk; landed against the wire shape locked at the 13.1 Ask-mode planning checkpoint — **option (b) per-thread `Map<threadId, Frame[]>` with monotonic global `seqCounter`**, every `entry` event carrying `parent_call_seq` + every `exit` carrying `entry_seq`). **13.2** (`POST /api/trace/{app_id}/dynamic` + `DELETE /api/trace/{app_id}/dynamic/{session_id}` REST routes + new `androscan/web/trace_dynamic.py` pure-helper module with `extract_closure_methods` / `cap_methods` / `methods_to_json` + `apps/<app_id>/<run>/dynamic_trace.jsonl` persistence path locked per DEC-029 via the existing `FridaSession.set_persistence_path` writer; +42 BE tests). **13.3** (WebSocket multiplexing — new `WS /ws/trace/{app_id}/{session_id}` + new `androscan/web/trace_summary.py` module with `SummaryCallable` async DI seam + per-method dedup + cache-miss-emits-pending-then-ready / cache-hit-emits-ready-only flow; three new `summary_pending` / `summary_ready` / `summary_failed` event kinds locked on the existing `{ts, session_id, kind, payload, raw}` envelope additively per DEC-022 / Phase 11 v2.1.5 discipline; +36 BE tests). **13.4** (`summarise_method` LLM skill + decompiled-source enrichment + `_build_summarise_method_callable` production adapter wiring the registered skill behind 13.3's `SummaryCallable` seam; cache slot byte-equal to 13.3's so the cutover required zero cache rebuild; +29 BE tests). **13.5** (FE rename "Decision Timeline" → "Behavior Trace" via parallel `BehaviorTrace.tsx` + `VITE_BEHAVIOR_TRACE_LEGACY` rollback flag pairing into ~30 comma-grouped CSS selectors; pure rename, no new tests). **13.6** (`ExecutionFlow` flowchart component using **React Flow / `@xyflow/react`** per the 13.6 Ask-mode planning checkpoint's option (b) resolution — operator picked over hand-rolled SVG for built-in pan / zoom / minimap; new pure-helper module `executionFlowGraph.ts` with overload-key dedup + BFS-rank layout + synthetic verdict-flavored sinks + `possiblyInlined` heuristic; locked DEC-029 styling enforced via custom `MethodNode` + `VerdictEdge` components; ~+150 KB JS bundle bump per the planning-checkpoint estimate; FE-only). **13.7** (`Inspector` pane — right-side fixed-360px panel with five operator-actionable sections + sticky action-row footer + module-local `resolveSelection` pure helper; reuses Phase 11 v2's `PredicateOriginView` + Phase 10's `BypassPlanCard` verbatim; FE-only). **13.8** (FE mode toggle Static / Dynamic / Both + dynamic overlay + summary streaming + live observation panel + new `useDynamicTrace(appId)` hook owning the POST + WS + DELETE lifecycle as a single state machine; **Planning-checkpoint pre-answers held.** Two brief Ask-mode planning checkpoints landed at the top of 13.1 and 13.6 per the original DEC-029 sub-step ordering. **13.1's three Q&As held verbatim through implementation:** Q1 (per-method `Java.use()` failure handling — all three reasons surfaced separately as `class_not_found` / `method_not_found` / `impl_set_failed`, per-failure events emitted live via `phase: "hook_failed"` distinct from runtime `phase: "error"`, plus aggregate `phase: "ready"` event); Q2 (depth counter — option (b) tracer-side `Map<threadId, Frame[]>` with monotonic `seqCounter`, every `entry` carrying `parent_call_seq` and every `exit` carrying `entry_seq`); Q3 (tier ordering — option (a) `String(value)` first with reflection fallback only on `Object.toString()` regex match `^[\w$.]+@[0-9a-f]+$`, locked thresholds 256 chars / 32 bytes / 8 fields / depth 1 / 5 list-or-map entries, bespoke handlers for null / primitives / String / byte[] / Bundle / Intent / List / Map). **13.6's substrate Q&A:** option (b) — React Flow / `@xyflow/react` — picked over hand-rolled SVG (option a) and the hybrid (option c). Operator preference; rationale traded the ~150 KB JS bundle bump for built-in pan / zoom / minimap / drag-pan inertia / familiar consumer-side API. The locked DEC-029 styling (1.5px stroke + 6×6 arrowhead + color-only edge emphasis + 220×72 cards + 6px radius) was enforced via custom `MethodNode` + `VerdictEdge` components that override the library defaults rather than fighting them; React Flow's `markerEnd: { type: ArrowClosed, width: 6, height: 6 }` got the locked arrowhead size verbatim. **No "operator picked one of the rejected alternatives" outcome on either checkpoint.**

The production frontend visually matches the mockup's three modes (Static / Dynamic / Both), edge styling (color-only emphasis), node design (220×72 / 6px radius / corner pills / live-value chips on the second line in dynamic / both modes / source line on the bottom / stacked-card visual for overloads / dashed border + `inlined?` corner label for R8-suspected), and Inspector content (signature + summary + source + predicate origin + bypass plans + action row) within the limits of React-on-DOM vs. the canvas SDK's SVG-via-`computeDAGLayout` substrate. **Three operator-visible deviations from the mockup, all small and operator-acknowledged:** (1) the production action row uses a 2-column grid with the `[Open source]` button spanning both columns as a full-width secondary row (matches the mockup's primary-on-top + secondary-on-bottom intent); the mockup itself rendered three buttons inline; (2) the mockup's "thread lanes" per-thread layout reshape was deferred — production v1 ships the corner depth pill (`d:N · t:M`) on fired nodes within the existing left-to-right column-rank layout, which preserves the operator's "this called this called this" mental model without introducing a layout-substrate change (full per-thread lane reshape is captured as a v2 candidate in `docs/KNOWN_ISSUES.md` ISSUE-019); (3) chat dock integration ships an interactive `<MethodSummaryWidget>` chat card (the mockup didn't show chat integration — this was added in 13.9 as the second consumer of the `SkillResult.widgets` channel established by Phase 11 v2.1.5's `<TraceEntryCandidateWidget>`).

**Operator-visible deviations from the locked DEC-029 design.** Strictly **none on the three load-bearing locks** (color-only edge emphasis, three-mode toggle, threshold-color hook-count ladder ≤ 20 / 21-50 / 51-100 / > 100). The three deviations are all UX accretions ON TOP OF the lock, not replacements OF it: (1) **`<MethodSummaryWidget>` chat card** added in 13.9 (DEC-029 specified the per-method LLM summaries shipped via `summarise_method` skill + Inspector mount; 13.9 added the chat-card path on top so operators can ask follow-up questions about a method directly in chat; the v2.1.5 `widgets[]` channel made this trivially additive); (2) **runtime-confirmed inlined wiring** in 13.9 (DEC-029 specified `inlined?` detection via `try/catch` around `Java.use()` in the 13.1 tracer template; 13.9 added the consumer-side flip from cool-gray static heuristic pill to warn-orange runtime confirmation when `hook_failed` events arrive — the static heuristic stays as a fallback when no dynamic trace has run); (3) **per-thread layout reshape deferred to v2** (DEC-029's "per-thread depth visualization" requirement was delivered via the corner depth pill in 13.8; the full layout reshape into per-thread lanes was deferred per the operator-dogfood-driven judgment that the corner pill preserves the call-tree mental model without the layout-substrate change cost — captured as ISSUE-019 for v2 promotion if real-app dogfooding shows the corner pill insufficient). **The edge-styling lock held verbatim** — three plan-mode iterations rejected thicker fired edges and a larger arrowhead, and the production code stays color-only with the same 1.5px stroke + 6×6 arrowhead per the operator's "Blue arrow looks fine, but don't make it thick" lock.

**`BehaviorAnchor` / `DecisionPoint` / `BypassPlan` / `PredicateOrigin` schemas held unchanged** through Phase 13 — `trace.sqlite` `SCHEMA_VERSION` stays at `"2"` from Phase 11 v2; Phase 13 added `apps/<app_id>/<run>/dynamic_trace.jsonl` for dynamic-trace events as a parallel persistence file outside `trace.sqlite` per the locked DEC-029 spec. Cached anchors built under v2 / v2.1 read-and-render under v3 with no migration. The `SummaryCallable` 4-arg seam introduced in 13.4 (`(app_id, class, method, descriptor) → str`) is the only contract surface that changed across the phase, and that change is internal to the WS-handler tier — operator-visible surfaces stayed schema-compatible throughout.
