### DEC-030: Phase 13 v2 — Behavior Trace Flowchart UX iteration

- **Status:** Active (2026-05-19)
- **Refines (does not supersede):** DEC-029. Three load-bearing v1 locks (color-only edge emphasis, three-mode toggle, threshold-color hook-count ladder ≤ 20 / 21-50 / 51-100 / > 100) are **untouched**; v2 is a UX iteration ON TOP OF the v1 lock, not a replacement OF it.
- **Drives:** Phase 13 v2.0 (this sub-step) + Phase 13 v2.0-tests (committed follow-up) + Phase 13 v2.1 (operator-demand-gated)

#### Context

Operator dogfooded the v1 flowchart on 2026-05-08 → 2026-05-19 and surfaced four operator-visible friction signals on top of the DEC-029 lock that the v1 ship sequence (13.0 → 13.10) didn't address:

1. **Multiple `NEUTRAL` sinks crowd the right edge** — the v1 `executionFlowGraph` emitted one synthetic `sink_neutral` (and `sink_allow` / `sink_deny`) PER SOURCE METHOD per kind. On real apps with 5+ decision-source methods this produced 10-20 small redundant boxes scattered across the right-edge layout, each carrying the same operator-meaning (*"this branch terminates"*) without unique information.
2. **Bottom-left "fit view" button mis-reads as a fullscreen affordance** — React Flow's default `<Controls>` includes a `fitView` button that rescales the viewport but doesn't expand the container. Operators expected within-page fullscreen ("make the flowchart fill the workbench area").
3. **Arrowheads invisible** — `App.css:6855`'s `fill: currentColor` on `marker path` didn't inherit edge stroke colour because SVG `<marker>` is a single shared document-level definition; the marker's `currentColor` resolved against the marker's own context, not the referencing edge path. Net effect: arrowheads rendered in the default theme color (often invisible against the workbench dark palette).
4. **Boxes appear above the Entry method + no indentation** — the v1 layout used BFS-from-entry on the decision-edge subgraph for `layoutRank`. Methods that the entry didn't decide-into fell through `ranks.get(succ) ?? 0` to rank 0; alphabetic within-column sort then placed them above the entry. No call-graph caller→callee edges existed in the FE graph builder so the column-rank layout had no hierarchy beyond decision flow.

Plan-mode iteration 2026-05-19 broke the four signals into eight ratification questions (Q1-Q8) with concrete options per question; ratification proceeded turn-by-turn with operator feedback re-shaping Q1 mid-iteration (the original Q1 recommendation `(a) drop entirely` was rejected on false-negative concern grounds — the `branch_classifier` is keyword-based and known to misclassify on obfuscated strings / non-English deny copy / custom-predicate gates per `branch_classifier.py:69-83`, so neutral-classified branches may be real deny gates the operator wants to see; the operator counter-proposed "small de-emphasized + hover-progressive" which crystallised as `(g)` and won verbatim).

#### Decision

v2.0 lands four operator-visible flowchart fixes plus the consequent gate-count badge UX in a single sub-step (this commit):

1. **Synthetic sinks** — drop nothing; reshape: per-source `sink_neutral` STAYS (operator keeps a per-gate anchor for misclassification spotting; renders at 110×36 with dashed border + 60% opacity + lowercase `neutral` body label per the de-emphasized variant); global `sink_allow` / `sink_deny` COALESCE to one node per kind (`GLOBAL_ALLOW_SINK_ID` / `GLOBAL_DENY_SINK_ID` constants in `executionFlowGraph.ts`). The classifier is confident on allow / deny so per-source anchors aren't worth their visual weight. **[Q1 = (g), Q2 = (a)]**
2. **Gate-count badge** — new per-method-card pill `${totalGates}g · ${unclassifiedGates}?` rendered in the meta row when `totalGates > 0`. The badge HTML is always present in the DOM but CSS-hidden by default (`opacity: 0; visibility: hidden`) and revealed instantly (`transition: opacity 80ms`) on `.execution-flow-node:hover` OR `:focus-within` (keyboard navigation discoverability). Hover-progressive disclosure pattern — clean default view + drill-down on focus. The `-warn` modifier raises emphasis when `unclassifiedGates > 0` so operators can scan for classifier false-negatives without traversing the right-edge sinks. **[Q1 = (g) again — this is the second half of the false-negative-spotting affordance]**
3. **Arrowhead fill** — `App.css:6855`'s `fill: currentColor` → `fill: context-stroke`. One-character semantic change; SVG2 keyword that correctly inherits the stroke colour of whichever path references the marker. Supported in Chrome 99+, Firefox 90+, Safari 16+ — within AndroScan's desktop-pentester target. The pre-existing `MARKERS` registry in `ExecutionFlow.tsx:552` was specifically reserved for the per-verdict-marker fallback path (`(b)` in the option list); the v2.0 lock made it unnecessary. **[Q3 = (a)]**
4. **Within-column sort entry-first** — `ExecutionFlow.tsx::layoutNodes` sort comparator gains an entry-first override on top of the existing v1 `synthetics-last + alphabetic-between` rules: `if (a.kind === "entry") return -1; if (b.kind === "entry") return 1;` then preserved v1 logic. Fixes the "boxes above Entry method" complaint at rank 0; doesn't address the lack-of-indentation complaint (that's a v2.1 problem — call-graph edge injection). **[Q5 = (b)]**
5. **Within-page fullscreen toggle** — custom button mounted via React Flow's `<Panel position="top-right">` (bottom-left is reserved for `<Controls>`, bottom-right for `<MiniMap>`); `isFullscreen` state flips a CSS class on the container that expands it to `position: fixed; inset: 0; z-index: 1000;` so the flowchart fills the viewport without going OS-level fullscreen (operator-locked posture: within-page only — full-OS fullscreen would hide the workbench chrome the operator may want to switch back to mid-trace); `ESC` exits via a `window.keydown` handler scoped to the fullscreen state. Button label flips on toggle ("Fullscreen" / "Exit fullscreen") + `-active` class indicator + `aria-pressed` for accessibility. **[Q7 = (a)]**
6. **Sink hover tooltip** — per-source `sink_neutral` nodes carry `feedingGates: Array<{sourceTitle, instructionIndex, verdictKind}>` populated from the decisions feeding them; renderer composes a multi-line `title` attribute (browser-default tooltip) listing the gates so operators can hover the sink to see which decisions converge into it. Implementation dependency-free (no popover component); the `\n`-separated format renders as multi-line in every browser we care about. **[Q1 = (g) drill-down affordance]**

v2.0-tests (next sub-step, committed follow-up — NOT operator-demand-gated): introduce frontend test infrastructure (`vitest` + happy-dom-or-jsdom + a `npm test` script) and land retroactive unit tests for the `executionFlowGraph.ts` pure helper covering: entry / gate / method classification; overload coalescing; global ALLOW / DENY sink coalescing; per-source neutral sink emission + `feedingGates` population; unverdicted edge routing; gate-count fields; `layoutRank` assignment + within-column sort. **Carved into its own sub-step** because "introduce frontend test runner" is a load-bearing architectural decision (which framework? happy-dom vs. jsdom? test-file location? snapshot-test policy?) that deserves its own focused ratification surface — burying it under UX-iteration work would couple two orthogonal decisions. **[Q8 = (b), with the infra decision deferred to a dedicated planning checkpoint at the top of v2.0-tests.]**

v2.1 (operator-demand-gated, dogfood-triggered): call-graph caller→callee edge injection via the existing `call_graph.py::neighbors()` SQLite walker. New endpoint (or extension of existing `/api/trace` route) returns the closure's call subgraph; FE injects as `kind: "call"` edges (thin gray, no arrowhead, no label) so BFS ranks reflect the actual call hierarchy and the flowchart gains real indentation. Gated on operator dogfood evidence that the v2.0 lightweight wins (within-column sort + visual cleanup) leave the layout still feeling flat. **[Q4 = (c) defer; Q6 = (b) two-stage]**

**v2.1 gating trigger** — subjective / dogfood-based. Operator uses AndroScan on real Android apps with the v2.0 flowchart; if the column-rank layout still feels flat (no clear hierarchy beyond decision flow), v2.1 promotes. No quantitative threshold — UX iteration is qualitative; pretending we can quantify it leads to gaming the threshold. The friction is its own go-signal. **[v2.1 trigger = (a)]**

#### Planning-checkpoint Q&A record (verbatim)

Eight ratification questions landed in plan-mode 2026-05-19; the operator's answers + rationale + my recommendations are preserved here so a future contributor can see the full as-locked record without trawling the transcript:

| Q | Question | Locked answer | Recommendation history | Rationale |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Q1 | Neutral sink treatment | **(g) hover-progressive disclosure** — small de-emphasized per-source sinks always visible + gate-count badge revealed on method-card hover (+ `:focus-within` for keyboard) + sink-hover reveals feeding-gates tooltip | Original `(a) drop entirely` rejected by operator on false-negative grounds. Iterated through `(b)` global-only / `(c)` badge-only / `(d)` both / `(e)` small-only / `(f)` small + badge to `(g)` hover-progressive as the synthesis. | `branch_classifier` is keyword-based and known to false-negative on obfuscated strings / non-English deny copy (`branch_classifier.py:69-83`); preserving a per-gate anchor for misclassification-spotting is operator-required. Hover-progressive disclosure addresses the clutter concern without sacrificing the anchor. |
| Q2 | ALLOW / DENY sink treatment | **(a) global per-kind coalescing** | (a) recommended; locked first-pass. | Classifier is confident on allow / deny (those are the cases where keyword signals fired); per-source anchors not worth their visual weight. |
| Q3 | Arrowhead fill | **(a) `fill: context-stroke`** | (a) recommended; locked first-pass. | One-line CSS fix; SVG2 keyword; supported in our target browsers. The pre-existing `MARKERS` registry in `ExecutionFlow.tsx:552` was specifically bookmarked for the per-verdict-marker fallback `(b)` if `(a)` proved insufficient. |
| Q4 | Call-graph edge injection in v2.0? | **(c) defer to v2.1** | (c) recommended; locked first-pass. | Call-graph injection is the technically correct fix for the indentation complaint but adds visual edges to a view the operator already finds cluttered. Gate on dogfood evidence v2.0's lightweight wins are insufficient. |
| Q5 | Within-column sort | **(b) entry first → real methods alphabetic → synthetics last** | (b) recommended; locked first-pass. | Preserves v1's "synthetics dangle off to the right" property; adds the entry-first lock that fixes "boxes above Entry" without further changes. |
| Q6 | Ship vehicle | **(b) two-stage (v2.0 lightweight + v2.1 dogfood-gated)** | (b) recommended; locked first-pass. | Captures all four user-visible issues in v2.0 without the call-graph-injection risk; v2.1 stays gated. |
| Q7 | Within-page fullscreen affordance | **(a) custom button + ESC handler + `-active` class indicator** | (a) recommended; locked first-pass. | Discoverable, matches operator mental model ("fullscreen button → fullscreen behavior"), single affordance. |
| Q8 | Test surface budget | **(b) unit tests for `executionFlowGraph.ts`** — **deferred to v2.0-tests sub-step** | (b) recommended; locked. Realised mid-implementation that no FE test runner exists; carved into v2.0-tests via operator-ratified `(iii)` split. | Pure-helper unit tests fit the test seam bookmarked at `executionFlowGraph.ts:7-12`; "introduce frontend test runner" is its own load-bearing decision that deserves a dedicated sub-step. |

Two follow-up ratifications:
- **DEC-030 lands in v2.0 itself** (operator: "do it in v2.0 itself") — not a separate planning sub-step like 13.0 was; the docs sweep + UX fixes ship in a single commit.
- **v2.1 gating trigger = (a) subjective / dogfood-based** — operator uses AndroScan on real apps; friction is the go-signal. No quantitative threshold.

#### Rationale (high-level)

- **Single-ship-vehicle (Q6 = (a), all four issues + call-graph injection in v2.0) rejected** — adds edges to a graph the operator already finds cluttered; the heavyweight call-graph injection deserves dogfood-driven measurement before commitment, so it gates to v2.1.
- **Hover-progressive disclosure (Q1 = (g)) chosen over coalesce-or-drop** — standard pattern (Datadog / Grafana / GitHub all use it for dense dashboards); operators learn it from organic discovery (badge appears on every card the moment cursor moves into the canvas); doesn't require special training.
- **Within-page fullscreen (Q7 = (a)) chosen over OS-level fullscreen** — operators routinely switch back to the workbench chrome (chat dock / Settings) mid-trace; OS-fullscreen would hide that surface.
- **Test infra carved into v2.0-tests (Q8 = (b) deferred)** — introduced frontend testing as its own focused decision surface vs. burying it under UX iteration work; mirror the LCP.0 / 11.0 / DEC-027 cadence of "one architectural decision per planning checkpoint".

#### Out of scope for v2.0

- Unit tests for the `executionFlowGraph` pure helper → **v2.0-tests** (committed follow-up; planning checkpoint at v2.0-tests's top picks the test framework + happy-dom-vs-jsdom + test-file location + snapshot-test policy).
- Call-graph caller→callee edge injection → **v2.1** (operator-demand-gated on dogfood evidence the v2.0 layout is still flat; new ISSUE-022 in `docs/KNOWN_ISSUES.md`).
- Unreachable-from-entry node filtering → **deliberately skipped** even though Plan-mode draft Axis II included it as D.2 (lightweight). Real-app analysis showed unreachable-from-entry-via-decision-edges methods are often operator-relevant (decision-source methods whose decisions all flow to other-method targets, plan-target methods whose source isn't reachable from entry); filtering them would lose useful info. v2.1's call-graph injection will reshape ranks naturally; until then, every closure method stays visible.
- Marching-ants animation on fired edges → **ISSUE-017 in `KNOWN_ISSUES.md`** (carried forward verbatim from DEC-029 v1 closing note; operator-demand-gated).
- Branch-outcome inference from dynamic data → **ISSUE-018**.
- Per-thread layout reshape into thread lanes → **ISSUE-019**.
- Bootstrap-fetch GET route for cached summaries → **ISSUE-020**.
- `<MethodSummaryWidget>` "Refresh summary" + pan-to-fit-on-selection → **ISSUE-021**.

All five carried-forward v2 candidates stay open; v2.0 doesn't close any of them. v2.0 introduces ISSUE-022 (call-graph edge injection) as the sixth tracked v2 candidate, now also the trigger surface for v2.1.

#### Roll-back path

v2.0 changes are isolated to three files: `executionFlowGraph.ts` + `ExecutionFlow.tsx` + `App.css`. Revert is single-commit. The four UX deviations are additive — reverting would restore the v1 behavior verbatim (modulo the legacy `DecisionTimeline.tsx` that 13.10 already deleted, which is not v2.0's concern). No schema changes; no new endpoints; no new dependencies; the existing `@xyflow/react@^12.10.2` substrate from 13.6 absorbs the v2.0 additions natively (`<Panel>` was already exported in v12.10.2, no version bump needed).

#### v2.0 closing note (appended 2026-05-19)

**Phase 13 v2.0 shipped 2026-05-19** in a single commit covering DEC-030 ratification + all four user-visible flowchart fixes + the consequent gate-count badge + sink-hover tooltip. **Pure FE change** — backend untouched, no schema changes, no new endpoints, no new dependencies. **+0 BE tests** (pytest stays at 1290 unchanged); v2.0-tests will be the next sub-step landing the FE test infrastructure + retroactive unit tests for the `executionFlowGraph.ts` pure helper.

**Cumulative bundle delta (v2.0 alone):** CSS 127.16 → 128.99 KB / **+1.83 KB raw** (gzipped 20.39 → 20.71 KB / +0.32 KB); JS 1455.67 → 1457.85 KB / **+2.18 KB raw** (gzipped 454.00 → 454.71 KB / +0.71 KB). Net ~+4 KB raw / +1 KB gzipped for the four UX features + the badge + the hover-tooltip — well within the planning-checkpoint estimate of "small, ~150-200 LOC FE + ~10-20 KB CSS" (final LOC count: ~180 FE + ~3 KB CSS, both at the low end of the estimate).

**Verification at v2.0 close:** `tsc --noEmit` clean; `vite build` clean; pytest unchanged at 1290 (BE not touched per the FE-only sub-step pattern from 13.5 / 13.6 / 13.7 / 13.8 / 13.10); zero lint errors across all three touched files.

**Operator-visible deviations from the locked Q1-Q8 design:** strictly **none**. Every answer locked in plan-mode translated 1-1 to the production code:
- Q1 = (g) hover-progressive disclosure → small sinks render at 110×36 with 60% opacity + dashed border + lowercase "neutral" + sink hover tooltip listing feeding gates; gate-count badge in meta row with `opacity: 0; visibility: hidden` default + `:hover, :focus-within` reveal at 80ms timing.
- Q2 = (a) global coalescing → `GLOBAL_ALLOW_SINK_ID` / `GLOBAL_DENY_SINK_ID` module constants in `executionFlowGraph.ts`; all allow / deny verdict edges (when not routed to a `BypassPlan` target) converge on the two global sinks.
- Q3 = (a) → `fill: context-stroke` change in `App.css`; one line, no fallback needed (target browsers support).
- Q5 = (b) entry-first → `layoutNodes` sort comparator gains the `kind === "entry"` short-circuit at the top of the comparator chain; preserved synthetics-last + alphabetic-between rules.
- Q7 = (a) within-page fullscreen → `useState<boolean>` + `useEffect` ESC handler + `<Panel position="top-right">` button + `.execution-flow-container-fullscreen` CSS class flipping the container to `position: fixed; inset: 0; z-index: 1000;`.

**`ExecutionFlowNode` schema extended additively** with three new fields — `totalGates` + `unclassifiedGates` + `feedingGates` — populated from `anchor.decisions` at build time. Pre-existing v1 consumers (`Inspector.tsx`'s `resolveSelection`, `LabTraceMode.tsx`'s `selectedFlowNode` state) read the schema with no changes (TypeScript additive-field-extension preserves prior consumer compatibility). The `GLOBAL_ALLOW_SINK_ID` + `GLOBAL_DENY_SINK_ID` constants are exported so v2.1 can compare against them when injecting call-graph edges without re-deriving the id shape.

**Next slot — v2.0-tests** (committed; not operator-demand-gated). Planning checkpoint at the top picks the test framework (likely `vitest` to match the Vite build substrate, though `node:test` + `tsx` is a lighter alternative worth considering) + happy-dom-vs-jsdom (almost certainly happy-dom for speed unless real DOM behaviour is needed for a future test that doesn't yet exist) + test-file location (alongside source as `*.test.ts` or in a sibling `__tests__/` directory) + snapshot-test policy (none for v2.0-tests; pure-helper logic tests only). Estimated landing: ~50 LOC infra + ~250 LOC test cases + 2-3 new devDependencies. **Then v2.1** is operator's call based on dogfood signal.

#### v2.0.1 hotfix closing note (appended 2026-05-19; same calendar day as v2.0 ship)

**Phase 13 v2.0.1 shipped 2026-05-19** — single-file CSS hotfix on top of v2.0's commit `44e110b`. Operator immediately dogfooded v2.0 on the WeakBank `MainActivity.onCreate$lambda$0` anchor and surfaced TWO follow-up signals: (1) the within-page fullscreen toggle (Q7 = (a)) only covered the top ~560 px of the viewport instead of filling the full page — clear regression introduced by v2.0; (2) the overall flowchart shape "looks almost similar to before" — soft dogfood signal that the v2.1 promotion trigger has now fired.

**v2.0.1 addresses signal (1) only** — a minimal CSS-only hotfix sized for a "fix what's clearly broken, then re-plan" cadence per the operator's explicit choice (option (i) at the v2.0.1 planning fork: "commit the fullscreen hotfix NOW as a tiny v2.0.1 patch on top of `44e110b`, then plan the next iteration as a separate ratification surface").

**Root cause** — the base `.execution-flow-container` rule (App.css:6594-6608, in place since 13.6) pins `width: 100%` + `height: 560px` so the in-page flowchart card has a stable predictable height. The v2.0 `-fullscreen` modifier (App.css:7833-7840) added `position: fixed; inset: 0; z-index: 1000;` but did NOT override the base `width` / `height`. CSS spec: when `position: fixed` is set with a non-`auto` `width` or `height`, the corresponding `right` / `bottom` offsets from `inset` are ignored (the explicit dimension wins, and the opposite-side offset is computed as whatever the layout needs). Result: the fullscreen container fixed itself at `(0, 0)` with `width: 100vw` (since `100%` of the viewport-containing-block = `100vw` when fixed) but stayed clamped to `height: 560px` — exactly the operator's screenshot showing the workbench chrome leaking through below the flowchart.

**Fix** — explicit `width: 100vw; height: 100vh;` added to `.execution-flow-container-fullscreen`. The explicit values win over the base `width: 100%; height: 560px;` via cascade order (the `-fullscreen` rule is later in App.css), so when the modifier class is applied, the container actually fills the viewport. `auto` was considered as an alternative ("let `inset: 0` resolve width/height intrinsically") but explicit viewport units are more robust against future container-parent changes — flex children + complex cascades can have edge cases where `auto` resolves to the intrinsic flowchart height rather than the viewport. CSS-only patch; no JS changes, no schema changes, no test impact.

**Bundle delta (v2.0.1 alone):** CSS 128.99 → 129.01 KB / **+22 bytes raw** (+~0 bytes gzipped — round-off below the gzip granularity); JS unchanged at 1457.85 KB. Net trivial; well below any budget.

**Verification at v2.0.1 close:** `tsc --noEmit` clean (no TS change); `vite build` clean (CSS hash bumps, JS unchanged); pytest stays at 1290 unchanged (BE not touched); zero lint errors. Operator visual-verified the fullscreen now fills the page expected to confirm post-deploy.

**Signal (2) deferred to a separate ratification surface.** Operator's explicit option (i) at the v2.0.1 fork was: "commit the fullscreen hotfix NOW, then plan the next iteration as a separate ratification surface" — so v2.0.1 does NOT lock anything for signal (2). The dogfood-input record for the next ratification surface (the operator answered (d) "all three" at the Q1 "what feels similar" fork): three friction signals to address in the next surface — **(a) flat-layout** (column-rank still feels like a single tall column; expected method calls to look like a real hierarchy with indentation = ISSUE-022 / v2.1 territory; the v2.1 promotion trigger has now formally fired); **(b) neutral-count clutter** (5-6 small neutral sinks per flowchart still adds up; wants further merging — e.g., one collapsed "N neutral outcomes" chip per source method — or a default-hidden toggle); **(c) gate-count badge invisible** (hover-progressive-disclosure was a misread of operator behaviour; operator doesn't hover-explore by default, so the badge never registered — wants always-visible at rest, with `-warn` modifier still visually distinct for unclassified gates). The next ratification surface picks: do all three at once (likely a "v2.1" that's broader than DEC-030's originally-scoped v2.1) OR carve them into separate sub-steps (a → v2.1 = call-graph injection; b → v2.2 = neutral coalescing; c → v2.0.2 = badge always-visible toggle). That planning checkpoint runs next, AFTER v2.0.1 commits.

### DEC-031: Phase 13 v2-next — CFG-aware Behavior Trace flowchart ranking via slicer-side `method_invocations` data extension

- **Status:** **Active** (2026-05-20 Proposed → 2026-05-21 Active after the v3.X-next.1.0 Ask-mode planning checkpoint locked Q1-Q8 verbatim + nine adjacent UX / architecture lock-ins (N1-N9) that spun out of the same Ask-mode session. See § Planning-checkpoint Q&A record below for the as-locked record + § Adjacent UX/architecture locks for the non-DEC-031 lock-ins + § Post-ratification migration story for the updated migration shape under path-B + Q7=(i) + N2=(i). Sub-step backlog under Q1=(b′) split: **v3.X-next.1** slicer extension (Python; new `CallSite` dataclass + `method_invocations` field on `BehaviorAnchor` + slicer-pass extension in `androscan/analysis/slicing.py` + tests), **v3.X-next.2** emitter evolution + v3 default-promotion + v2-module deletion (TS; `executionFlowGraphV3.ts` evolves in-place to consume `method_invocations` + `?flow=v3` URL gate removed + `executionFlowGraph.ts` + `ExecutionFlow.tsx` deleted + FE test runner introduced per Q8=(b)).)
- **Refines (does not supersede):** DEC-030 (v2 UX iteration; this DEC closes the v2-next promised ratification surface and addresses all three friction signals (a)+(b)+(c) from DEC-030's v2.0.1 closing-note dogfood-input record). DEC-029's three load-bearing locks (color-only edge emphasis, three-mode Static/Dynamic/Both toggle, threshold-color hook-count ladder ≤20/21-50/51-100/>100) stay **untouched**.
- **Drives:** v2-next sub-step backlog (shape depends on Q1 answer — see § Tentative sub-step backlog below for the two candidate orderings).

#### Context

DEC-030 v2.0 shipped four operator-visible flowchart UX fixes 2026-05-19 (color-only emphasis preserved; global ALLOW/DENY sink coalescing; per-source neutral sinks de-emphasised + hover-progressive gate-count badge; `fill: context-stroke` arrowhead fix; within-column entry-first sort; within-page fullscreen toggle). v2.0.1 hotfixed the fullscreen width/height regression on the same calendar day. Operator dogfood within minutes of v2.0 deploying surfaced **three friction signals** captured verbatim in DEC-030's v2.0.1 closing note + the corresponding TASKS.md Active Task block:

1. **Signal (a) — flat-layout friction.** Column-rank still feels like a single tall column without indentation; operator expected method calls to look like a real hierarchy (caller above, callees indented below). Formally fires the v2.1 promotion trigger DEC-030 locked at (a) subjective/dogfood-based — was ISSUE-022 territory until now.
2. **Signal (b) — neutral-count clutter.** 5-6 small de-emphasised per-source `sink_neutral` nodes per flowchart still adds up visually; operator wants further merging (e.g., one collapsed "N neutral outcomes" chip per source method) or default-hidden behind a toggle.
3. **Signal (c) — gate-count badge invisible.** Hover-progressive disclosure was a misread of operator behaviour; the operator doesn't hover-explore by default, so the v2.0 badge never registered; operator wants always-visible at rest with `-warn` modifier still visually distinct for unclassified gates. Effectively retracts the Q1 = (g) hover-progressive lock from v2.0 for the badge specifically (sink-hover tooltip behaviour preserved — the small sink is the affordance the operator hovers when they want detail).

**v3 preview empirical findings (commit `c59d879`, 2026-05-20) — load-bearing diagnosis for this DEC.** The v3 preview shipped behind `?flow=v3` as exploratory code to test whether the call-graph injection deferred by DEC-030 (ISSUE-022 / originally-scoped v2.1) would resolve signal (a). The v3 preview took three categorical steps beyond v2's emitter — dropping synthetic ALLOW/DENY/neutral sinks (closing the hand-mockup's "why is ALLOW itself a box?" feedback), adding always-on `verdictSummary` chips on every gate card (per-verdict counts as the primary distribution surface), and routing real-callee terminals via `BypassPlan.target_method` resolution (when verdict is `allow`). The v3.1-fix iteration further added a `FRAMEWORK_CLASS_PREFIXES` denylist so `Intrinsics.checkNotNullParam` and friends no longer appear as rank-1 siblings.

Operator dogfooded the v3.1 preview on the same WeakBank `MainActivity.onCreate$lambda$0` anchor (the trigger anchor for v2.0.1 signal (a)). **Result: the flat-layout friction persists.** Three surviving app-code methods (`LabStore.getPin`, `MainActivity.renderState`, `MainActivity.validatePin`) still sit horizontally at rank 1 below the entry. Walking through the data flow in Ask mode (recorded in this same agent-transcripts entry) localised the root cause: v3 relies on (i) synthesised `entry → DecisionPoint.method` `call` edges (one per gate-bearing method, fanning out from the entry) and (ii) plan-target edges (only emitted for `allow` verdicts), and the anchor has **`0 plans`** (all 20 decisions reclassified to neutral by the heuristic `branch_classifier`) so no plan-target edges fire to extend chain depth via Dagre's longest-path layering. All three methods are direct callees of the entry as far as Dagre can tell, so they correctly land at the same rank.

**This is a slicer-side data-model limit, not a renderer limit.** The `BehaviorAnchor` payload (`androscan/analysis/trace_types.py::BehaviorAnchor`, mirrored TS-side at `androscan/web/frontend/src/api/trace.ts:177-187`) carries:

```python
@dataclass(frozen=True)
class BehaviorAnchor:
    entry_method: MethodRef
    hops: int
    truncated: bool
    incomplete: bool
    decisions: tuple[DecisionPoint, ...]
    plans: tuple[BypassPlan, ...]
    advanced_plans: tuple[BypassPlan, ...]
    rationale: str
    low_confidence_decision_indices: tuple[int, ...]
```

It knows **which methods contain `if`/`switch` instructions** (via `decisions[*].method` + `decisions[*].instruction_index`), **which bypass plans cross-reference which decision** (via `plans[*].source_decision_method` + `source_decision_instruction_index` + optional `target_method`), and **confidence + verdict for each branch**. It does **not** know:

- **Per-method invocation sequence.** The bytecode of `login` has `invoke check_active_session`, `invoke check_input`, `invoke validate_pin`, `invoke create_session`, `invoke PostLoginActivity`, `invoke Login_UI_text` at specific instruction indices in order — but none of that lives in `BehaviorAnchor`. The anchor only carries the if/switch instructions, not the invoke instructions.
- **Caller → callee relationships.** Even decision-free callees (e.g., `validate_pin` calling `validate_pin_final`, or `create_session` doing UUID gen + field write) wouldn't have any record of who calls them — the call graph is a separate artefact (`call_graph.sqlite`, ISSUE-022, not wired into the anchor yet).
- **CFG structure within a method.** The basic-block graph of `login` (which decisions dominate which calls, which calls live in which branch arm) is something Soot/the slicer computed in the slicer pass but **discarded** before producing `BehaviorAnchor`.

The v3 preview demonstrates that ISSUE-022's originally-scoped fix (inject `call_graph.sqlite::neighbors()` as `kind: "call"` edges before BFS) would help but would NOT produce the operator's mental model. ISSUE-022 alone gives a wide fan-out (call-graph depth = 1 for all of `login`'s direct callees: `check_active_session`, `check_input`, `validate_pin`, `create_session`, `PostLoginActivity`, `Login_UI_text` all sit at rank 1 as siblings). To get a **vertical chain** ranked by **statement order inside the entry's body** (operator's hand-mockup posture), the emitter needs:

1. A per-method **invocation sequence** (with instruction indices for ordering).
2. A per-invocation **branch-dominance label** (which `if`-arm of which decision does this invoke live in) so off-axis terminals (the deny/Ret: False branches in the operator's mockup) become siblings of the rank-below sequential call rather than collapsing into a verdict chip.
3. The **decision-free callees kept as first-class nodes**. v3.1's filter rule (`isEntry || isDecisionSource || isPlanTarget`) drops `validate_pin` (pure call to `validate_pin_final`), `validate_pin_against_store` (pure boolean return), and `create_session` (UUID gen, no decisions) — exactly the methods the operator's hand-mockup keeps as call points.

The Ask-mode trace through a worked Kotlin example (`com.example.mocklogin.LoginActivity.login(String, String)` — nested local functions `check_active_session`, `check_input`, `validate_pin` containing `validate_pin_format` + `validate_pin_against_store` + `validate_pin_final`, `create_session`, plus the outer `Login_UI_text` + `PostLoginActivity`) confirmed v3.1 would produce 6 siblings at rank 1 and lose 3 of the operator-meaningful methods entirely. The operator's response: "The ranking is simply done based on presence within a method (even when it is the entry). The idea of a rank is to put them in the same column of indentation, not lay them horizontally. And the order in which they appear in login's definition should decide how far down from login in the graph they are."

#### Proposed solution (subject to Q1-Q8 ratification below)

**Two-layer change** — additive at the schema layer, new emitter at the renderer layer.

##### Slicer side (Python; `androscan/analysis/`)

New field on `BehaviorAnchor`:

```python
@dataclass(frozen=True)
class CallSite:
    """One ``invoke-*`` instruction in a method's body, with the
    dominating decision (if any) and branch arm label so the
    renderer can reconstruct CFG-position-aware ranking.

    Captured by the slicer alongside the existing decision walk;
    same Soot CFG pass that computes basic-block dominance for
    the predicate-origin slicer can surface ``in_branch_of`` +
    ``branch_label`` essentially for free. The slicer already
    discards this information post-walk; v2-next preserves it."""

    caller: MethodRef
    instruction_index: int        # position in the caller's bytecode
    callee: MethodRef             # resolved invoke target (or external/unresolved fallback)
    in_branch_of: int | None      # the dominating decision's instruction_index in the same caller, if any
    branch_label: str | None      # which arm of that decision ("R0:if-true" / "fallthrough" / etc.; matches Branch.label)

@dataclass(frozen=True)
class BehaviorAnchor:
    # ... existing fields unchanged ...
    method_invocations: dict[str, tuple[CallSite, ...]]  # NEW; key = overload signature; default = empty dict for back-compat
```

Implementation locus: extends the slicer pass in `androscan/analysis/slicing.py` (or a new sibling module) to surface the invoke instruction list while it walks the entry's closure. Soot's CFG already computes basic-block dominance for the predicate-origin slicer; surfacing `(in_branch_of, branch_label)` requires no new analysis pass, only preserving data the slicer already had.

Backwards compatibility: `method_invocations` defaults to `{}` (empty dict). v2 / v2.0.1 anchors already in `trace.sqlite` deserialise correctly (the field defaults to empty); the renderer treats an empty dict as "no CFG data available, fall back to v3.x layout".

##### Renderer side (TypeScript; `androscan/web/frontend/src/components/trace/`)

New emitter `executionFlowGraphV4.ts` (or evolved-in-place `executionFlowGraphV3.ts` per Q7 below) that consumes `BehaviorAnchor.method_invocations`:

1. **Walk the entry method's `method_invocations[entryKey]` list in `instruction_index` order.**
2. **Emit a sequential `prev_invoke → next_invoke` `call` edge between consecutive callees in the same branch arm** — Dagre's longest-path layering naturally stacks them vertically.
3. **For each decision in the entry, emit sibling subtrees:** one column per `branch_label`, with each branch's call list at increasing rank. Off-axis (deny/Ret: False) branch terminals become siblings of the next sequential call in the happy-path arm at the same rank.
4. **Recurse into each callee that itself has `method_invocations` entries** (so `check_input`'s three internal gates show as deny-side terminals into `Login_UI_text("bad_input")`, not as a single chip; `validate_pin` → `validate_pin_final` → `validate_pin_format` + `validate_pin_against_store` becomes a real chain).
5. **Keep decision-free methods (`validate_pin`, `create_session`, etc.) as first-class nodes** because they're now ingested via `method_invocations`, not via `anchor.decisions` or `anchor.plans` — the filter rule expands accordingly.

Dagre still does the heavy lifting (`rankdir: 'TB'`, `network-simplex` ranker, no custom layout primitive). The new emitter changes only **which edges Dagre sees**.

##### Migration story

- v2 / v2.0.1 keeps serving as production until v4 lands and is operator-ratified.
- v3 preview (`?flow=v3`) keeps the cosmetic improvements (no synthetic sinks, verdict-summary chip, framework filter, real-callee terminals via plan resolution); its layout is unchanged because `method_invocations` isn't populated yet at the time v3 was authored.
- New emitter ships behind `?flow=v4` (or evolved-in-place per Q7) once slicer landings are operator-ratified.
- Promotion to default happens after dogfood confirms the CFG-aware layout matches the operator's mockup intuition.
- Slicer change is purely additive (new optional field on `BehaviorAnchor`). Old `trace.sqlite` caches deserialise cleanly; v4 falls back to v3-shaped layout when `method_invocations` is empty.

#### Open Q&As — Ask-mode operator ratification surface

This DEC ships **Proposed** with eight ratification questions awaiting operator answer. Each Q lists concrete options + my recommendation + rationale. Lock-in happens via a follow-up Ask-mode planning checkpoint (mirroring DEC-029 13.0 / DEC-030 plan-mode iteration); the answers get appended verbatim to this DEC entry under a "Planning-checkpoint Q&A record" sub-heading and the status flips `Proposed → Active` at that point.

**Q1. Ship vehicle — single broader v2.1 OR three separate sub-steps?**

| Option | Description |
|---|---|
| (a) Single v2.1 (broader than DEC-030's originally-scoped v2.1) | One sub-step covers signals (a)+(b)+(c) end-to-end including the slicer change. Largest ship vehicle; longest dogfood gap; one ratification surface. |
| (b) Three separate sub-steps in size order: **c → v2.0.2** (FE-only gate-count badge always-visible toggle, smallest ship vehicle, quick win); **b → v2.2** (FE-only neutral coalescing, medium); **a → v2.1** (slicer extension + new v4 emitter, biggest). | Smallest first ships fast; three ratification surfaces, three commits; each can dogfood independently. |
| (c) Two-stage: **(b)+(c) together as v2.0.2** (both small FE-only) then **(a) as v2.1** (slicer + emitter, larger). | Groups the FE-only fixes for a single quick-win commit; separates the slicer side. |

**Recommendation: (b).** The three signals are conceptually independent — (c) is a CSS/render-posture flip, (b) is a graph-build rule, (a) is a slicer + emitter overhaul. Folding them together inflates ratification surface area. (b) lets the operator dogfood (c)'s "is always-visible actually better?" and (b)'s "does the coalesced chip kill the clutter?" before committing to the (a) heavy lift. The slicer change is real software with its own test surface (Python `tests/test_decisions_slicing.py` extensions) and deserves its own ratification cadence apart from FE tweaks.

**Q2. CallSite shape — minimal vs. complete vs. external-graph-only?**

| Option | Description |
|---|---|
| (a) Complete — `(caller, instruction_index, callee, in_branch_of, branch_label)` per the proposal above. | Renderer gets everything it needs; trade-off is a slicer-side computation surface (must surface dominance + branch arm labels). |
| (b) Minimal — `(caller, instruction_index, callee)` only; branch dominance computed FE-side by walking `decisions[]` and comparing instruction indices. | Smaller slicer change; FE-side dominance reconstruction is brittle (needs basic-block boundaries the FE doesn't have). |
| (c) Wire to `call_graph.sqlite` directly — emit only the subset of the existing call graph that touches the anchor closure; CFG branch labels computed separately (or skipped, in which case the renderer falls back to v3 layout). | Reuses existing infrastructure; doesn't get branch-arm labels (siblings collapse). |

**Recommendation: (a).** Gives the renderer everything needed in a single field; avoids brittle FE-side reconstruction. The slicer already has the basic-block CFG in hand during the predicate-origin walk; surfacing dominance labels is essentially free. The +5-field-per-CallSite wire-cost is negligible relative to the existing `DecisionPoint` payload weight.

**Q3. Computation locus — slicer pass extension vs. separate post-pass vs. lazy-on-demand?**

| Option | Description |
|---|---|
| (a) Extend the existing slicer pass in `androscan/analysis/slicing.py` (or a new sibling module) to walk invoke instructions alongside decision-point walking. Same Soot CFG visit. | Single pass keeps the slicer the source of truth; consistent with how `BehaviorAnchor` is currently built. |
| (b) Separate post-processing module that walks the anchor's closure + decompiled sources to extract the invocation lists. | Decouples the slicer; allows incremental rollout; doubles the bytecode-walking cost per anchor. |
| (c) Lazy-on-demand — slicer emits placeholder; renderer requests `method_invocations` on-demand via new endpoint when an anchor is opened. | Smallest cache footprint; biggest per-render latency; adds a new endpoint to the API surface. |

**Recommendation: (a).** Single-pass discipline; the slicer is already the source of truth for `BehaviorAnchor`; reusing its Soot session avoids re-instantiating the CFG. The slicer's existing `_DescentBudget` + `MAX_SLICE_DEPTH` + framework denylists naturally apply to the invocation walk too.

**Q4. Schema version — bump 2 → 3 with cache wipe OR keep at 2 additively?**

| Option | Description |
|---|---|
| (a) Bump `SCHEMA_VERSION 2 → 3` with cache wipe — clean break, no backwards-compat code path. | Matches the Phase 11 v2 → v2.1 cadence; forces operators to rebuild traces to see the new visual. |
| (b) Keep at 2 with `method_invocations: dict = field(default_factory=dict)` so old caches still load (empty dict means renderer falls back to v3-shaped layout). | Additive-by-design discipline per DEC-022 / Phase 11 v2.1.5 widgets; renderer gracefully degrades. No cache wipe. |
| (c) Bump to 3 but write a one-shot migration that scans existing v2 caches and lazily computes `method_invocations` from `decisions[]` + decompiled sources. | Old caches survive without rebuild; biggest migration surface area. |

**Recommendation: (b).** The Phase 11 v2.1.5 `SkillResult.widgets` extension set the additive-by-design precedent for this kind of schema growth; honouring it here keeps the cache-bump count down (operators rebuild traces only when they want the new visual, not because we forced them). v4 emitter naturally handles `method_invocations: {}` by falling back to v3 layout — the empty-dict signal is unambiguous.

**Q5. Gate-count badge always-visible posture (signal c)?**

| Option | Description |
|---|---|
| (a) Always-visible at rest — drop the hover-progressive disclosure entirely. `-warn` modifier still raises emphasis when `unclassifiedGates > 0`. | Operator-confirmed preferred posture. Cleanest implementation. |
| (b) Always-visible only when `unclassifiedGates > 0` (the `-warn` case); hidden otherwise. | Cleaner default view; loses the badge-as-an-anchor-for-spotting-fully-classified gates. |
| (c) Always-visible with operator toggle — keep both default-hidden + always-visible modes available, surface a Settings toggle. | Discoverable; adds Settings surface area for what should be a posture flip. |

**Recommendation: (a).** Operator explicitly confirmed they don't hover-explore by default; (a) directly addresses the misread. (b) preserves a partial hover-progressive intuition that the operator already rejected.

**Q6. Neutral coalescing (signal b)?**

| Option | Description |
|---|---|
| (a) Collapsed "N neutral outcomes" chip per source method on the gate card itself; per-source `sink_neutral` nodes dropped entirely from the graph. | Cleanest visual; the `verdictSummary` chip (already v3.1's posture) is the right home. |
| (b) Default-hidden behind operator toggle — neutral sinks stay in the graph but hidden by default, surface a toggle in the section header. | Preserves the anchor for misclassification spotting; one more piece of chrome. |
| (c) Aggregate to single global neutral sink (parallel to v2.0's `GLOBAL_ALLOW_SINK_ID` / `GLOBAL_DENY_SINK_ID`). | Symmetric with allow/deny coalescing; the global neutral sink loses the per-source feeding-gates tooltip. |
| (d) Leave as-is — v2.0 small-de-emphasised per-source neutral sinks are already minimal; only the visual count is operator's complaint. | Zero change; banks on (a)+(c) of THIS DEC tightening the rest of the visual to make the residual neutrals tolerable. |

**Recommendation: (a).** The `verdictSummary` chip is already the always-visible per-verdict count surface (`2 allow · 1 deny · 8 ?`); the per-source `sink_neutral` node is redundant once the chip is always-visible per Q5. The feeding-gates tooltip (the v2.0 sink-hover affordance) gets re-homed to the verdict-chip tooltip — same data, same hover affordance, fewer nodes.

**Q7. v3 preview disposition?**

| Option | Description |
|---|---|
| (a) Keep `?flow=v3` URL gate as-is until v4 ships, then deprecate both v3 and the URL gate (`?flow=v4` replaces it; v3 stays accessible for one release as a rollback target). | Conservative; preserves v3 as a known-good preview state. |
| (b) Deprecate v3 now — remove the preview code, ship v4 directly when ready. | Smallest preview-code surface; loses the v3 cosmetic improvements until v4 lands. |
| (c) Roll forward incrementally — `executionFlowGraphV3.ts` evolves into v4 as the slicer changes land. One preview module, no separate v4. Promote to default when CFG-aware ranking lands. | Smallest commit surface area; conflates two versions in one module's history; complicates rollback. |

**Recommendation: (a).** v3 already lives as a self-contained module pair (`ExecutionFlowV3.tsx` + `executionFlowGraphV3.ts`); paying the slightly-larger preview-code surface buys clean rollback ("flip the URL param to compare v3 vs. v4 side-by-side"). The v3 cosmetic improvements (no synthetic sinks, verdict-summary chip, framework filter) are also operator-visible signals during v4 dogfood — the operator can isolate "did the CFG-aware ranking help?" from "did the cosmetic improvements help?" by URL-swapping.

**Q8. Test coverage scope?**

| Option | Description |
|---|---|
| (a) Slicer-side only — pin the `method_invocations` shape via `tests/test_decisions_slicing.py` extensions; FE side gets the existing `tsc --noEmit` + `vite build` verification (no FE test runner shipped yet per Phase 13 v2.0-tests deferral). | Smallest infra change; FE regressions only caught at dogfood time. |
| (b) Slicer + FE — ship the FE test runner (the deferred v2.0-tests sub-step) alongside, cover both the new shape parsing + the v4 emitter's CFG-edge-construction logic. Folds v2.0-tests into the v2.1 ratification surface. | Symmetric test surface; resurrects the v2.0-tests scope that was deprioritised vs v2-next. |
| (c) Slicer + FE + integration — end-to-end test from anchor build through render via a headless browser harness. | Best coverage; biggest infra commitment; introduces a new test layer (headless browser) the project doesn't have yet. |

**Recommendation: (b).** The FE test infra was already a committed follow-up (Phase 13 v2.0-tests sub-step) that got deprioritised vs v2-next because operator-visible friction won — but the v4 emitter is the biggest FE change since 13.6 and absolutely deserves test coverage. Folding v2.0-tests into the v2.1 sub-step ratification surface kills two birds (test infra + v4 emitter unit tests) with one planning-checkpoint decision (which FE test runner — `vitest` vs `node:test` vs others — locked at the v2.1.0 planning checkpoint top).

#### Tentative sub-step backlog (shape depends on Q1 answer)

**If Q1 = (b) three separate sub-steps:**

| Sub-step | Vehicle | Signal | Estimated scope |
|---|---|---|---|
| **v2.0.2** | FE-only CSS + render posture flip on `.execution-flow-node-gate-badge` | (c) gate-count badge always-visible | ~10 LOC CSS + ~5 LOC render comparator; smallest commit; quick-win |
| **v2.2** | FE-only emitter rule + render flip; per-source `sink_neutral` nodes dropped from graph; `verdictSummary` chip already always-visible from v3.1 — but lands here in production v2 path | (b) neutral coalescing into chip | ~30 LOC graph-builder + ~20 LOC render delta + verdict-chip tooltip re-home |
| **v2.1.0** | Ask-mode planning checkpoint locking FE test runner choice + v4 emitter contract | meta — gates v2.1 | docs only |
| **v2.1.1** | Slicer extension — new `CallSite` dataclass + `method_invocations` field on `BehaviorAnchor` + slicer-pass extension + `tests/test_decisions_slicing.py` extension covering `method_invocations` shape on at least 3 anchor types (linear / branchy / nested-locals) | (a) part 1 — backend | ~+350 LOC slicer + ~+30 tests |
| **v2.1.2** | FE v4 emitter — `executionFlowGraphV4.ts` consuming `method_invocations` + `ExecutionFlowV4.tsx` renderer + `?flow=v4` URL gate + FE unit tests on the new emitter | (a) part 2 — frontend | ~+450 LOC FE + ~+25 tests |
| **v2.1.3** | Dogfood window + promotion to default (operator-ratified after side-by-side comparison v3 vs. v4 on real anchors) | (a) part 3 — promotion | docs sweep |

**If Q1 = (a) single broader v2.1:**

One v2.1 commit covering all three signals end-to-end including the slicer change. Bigger ratification surface; one commit. Sub-step backlog collapses to v2.1.0 planning checkpoint + v2.1 implementation + v2.1-tests follow-up.

#### Rationale (high-level; assumes recommended answers)

- **Slicer-side data extension is the right home, not FE-side reconstruction.** The slicer is already the source of truth for `BehaviorAnchor`; bolting CFG/invocation-sequence reconstruction onto the renderer would duplicate logic that already lives upstream and would lose context (Soot already computes basic-block dominance during the predicate-origin walk). The Q3 = (a) recommendation honours single-pass discipline.
- **Additive schema (Q4 = (b)) preserves operator workflow.** Operators with v2-era trace caches can keep using them; the renderer falls back gracefully. Avoids the cache-wipe pain Phase 11 v2 → v2.1 (`SCHEMA_VERSION 1 → 2`) imposed.
- **Three separate sub-steps (Q1 = (b)) optimises for dogfood feedback.** Each fix surfaces its impact independently — the operator can answer "is always-visible the right badge posture?" before committing to "do collapsed neutrals work?" before committing to "does CFG-aware ranking match the mental model?". (a) bundles them and forces a single yes/no, which loses the diagnostic signal.
- **v3 preview kept as rollback (Q7 = (a))** because v3.x cosmetic improvements remain operator-visible signals during v4 dogfood; isolating "cosmetic" from "structural" helps diagnose any v4 regressions.

#### Out of scope for v2-next (regardless of Q1 split)

- **Marching-ants animation on fired edges** — ISSUE-017, operator-demand-gated.
- **Branch-outcome inference from dynamic data** — ISSUE-018, operator-demand-gated.
- **Per-thread layout reshape into thread lanes** — ISSUE-019, operator-demand-gated.
- **Bootstrap-fetch GET route for cached summaries** — ISSUE-020, operator-demand-gated.
- **`<MethodSummaryWidget>` "Refresh summary" + pan-to-fit-on-selection** — ISSUE-021, operator-demand-gated.
- **Cross-platform `BehaviorAnchor` adapters** (iOS / WebView / native-binary) — DEC-024 / DEC-026 territory; orthogonal to v2-next.
- **Frontend rebrand or restructure of unrelated tabs** — out of scope.

#### Roll-back path

Per-sub-step roll-back stories (assuming Q1 = (b)):

- **v2.0.2 (signal c) roll-back:** single CSS file revert; ~10 LOC change. Restores v2.0's hover-progressive disclosure.
- **v2.2 (signal b) roll-back:** revert the graph-builder + render changes (~50 LOC). v2.0's per-source `sink_neutral` nodes return.
- **v2.1 (signal a) roll-back:**
  - Slicer side: schema is additive (Q4 = (b)); rolling back the slicer change means `method_invocations` is always empty on new traces. Old traces from v2.1.x continue to deserialise.
  - FE side: revert the `?flow=v4` wiring; v3 preview stays untouched per Q7 = (a); production stays on v2 / v2.0.1.
- **Whole-DEC roll-back:** the DEC entry stays as a Superseded record; the v2.0.2 / v2.2 / v2.1 commits get reverted independently. v3 preview stays as a non-default URL-gated preview.

At that point: the locked answers get appended to this DEC entry as a "Planning-checkpoint Q&A record" table (mirroring DEC-030's format); status flips `Proposed → Active`; the sub-step backlog row in TASKS.md gets fleshed out per the Q1 = (?) outcome.

Status flipped `Proposed → Active`. Sub-step backlog under Q1=(b′) split-under-path-B: v3.X-next.1 slicer extension (Python) + v3.X-next.2 emitter evolution + URL-gate removal + v2-module deletion (TS).

#### Adjacent UX/architecture locks (same Ask-mode session, recorded for traceability)

Nine non-DEC-031 lock-ins surfaced in the same Ask-mode session via the operator's UX walk-through of the v3 production card + the hover-expand-card affordance + the verdict-summary chip relabel + the source-line click affordance + the Settings → `trace.max_slice_depth` tooltip CTA. None individually warrants its own DEC; together they shape the implementation surface area for v3.X-next.1 + v3.X-next.2 and need to be discoverable as as-locked decisions:

| # | Item | Lock | Rationale |
|---|---|---|---|
| N1 | v3 default-promotion timing | **(i) standalone micro sub-step** — v3 default-promotion happens as its own beat inside v3.X-next.2 (URL gate removal + v2-module deletion + `executionFlowGraph.ts` / `ExecutionFlow.tsx` deletion), not bundled with the emitter evolution work | Separating "data-driven layout change" from "remove the URL gate + delete v2 module" keeps the ratification beat tight; emitter evolution gets dogfood before the safety net (the v2 module) goes away. |
| N2 | v2 module fate | **(i) delete immediately at v3 default-promotion** (no migration window) | Operator: *"this tool is not live yet, so v2 can go away and replace v3.X"* — no production operators to break. Harmonizes cleanly with Q7=(i) (no URL gate ⇒ no runtime path to v2 anyway). |
| N3 | Hover-expand-card shape | **`transform: scale()` + `z-index: 10` lift on the hovered card** (CSS-only; edges remain attached to the un-transformed bounding box — React Flow doesn't relayout on hover) | Cleanest of the three considered alternatives — (β) render-twin (DOM-debt-heavy, a11y double-announce risk) and (γ) side-popover (operator explicitly rejected "tooltip-style" framing) both inferior. ~15 LOC CSS + no React Flow plumbing changes. |
| N4 | Hover-expand-card sizing | **(A) 1.5× uniform** — nominal 330×108 px | 1.3× → 286×94 was too cramped for the four reveal rows after dropping the descriptor. Operator's "30% larger" mental model is uniform-scale, not vertical-accordion; rejected (B) vertical-only growth. |
| N5 | Expanded-view content set | **Drop method descriptor** (operator: *"doesn't help a pentester as much"*). Keep: (1) summary preview (~80 chars from `summarise_method` cache; falls back to *"No summary yet · run dynamic trace"* if absent); (2) top 1-2 decision predicates sorted DENY > ALLOW > neutral with color-coded verdict tag; (3) bypass plan preview (count + first plan one-liner); (4) live runtime status (`hooked · N entries · last Ns ago`) when active dynamic trace is in play | The four kept rows fit comfortably at 1.5× sizing and each carries operator-actionable signal. Descriptor is a Smali-shape artefact, not a security-relevant detail. |
| N6 | Verdict-summary chip relabel | **`9 ?` → `9 neutral?`** AND symmetric **`9 unv` → `9 unverdicted?`** (sub-hover on either span opens a small generic tooltip explaining the verdict-category semantics in plain language — no "slicer" / "max-walk" jargon; unverdicted tooltip includes a CTA pointing to **Settings → Global → trace → max_slice_depth**) | The bare `?` glyph alone was cryptic to anyone who hadn't built the system; the word + glyph pair is self-explanatory at a glance. Symmetric relabel for `unv → unverdicted?` preserves visual consistency between the two adjacent uncertainty chips. |
| N7 | Source-line pill clickable | **Yes** — wrap `.execution-flow-node-source-line` in a `<button>` triggering the existing `pendingCodeNav` plumbing (same single-line nav the Inspector source-line click already uses); `event.stopPropagation()` to avoid bubbling into the node-click → Inspector flow | ~10 LOC FE; the plumbing already exists (see `Inspector.tsx::onOpenSource`). Operators jumping from card → Code Browser is the single highest-friction nav today; this collapses it to one click without an Inspector intermediate step. |
| N8 | Title shape | **`validatePin` primary, `MainActivity.validatePin` secondary** (was `MainActivity.validatePin` primary + bare class chip secondary) | Bare method name reads at a glance; fully-qualified name is the disambiguator when overload count > 1 or two methods with the same short name exist in different classes within the closure. |
| N9 | Per-app `trace.max_slice_depth` override | **No** — stays workspace-wide-only; per-app whitelist in `SettingsTab.tsx` stays at `["rag", "decompile", "inspect", "exploit", "chat"]` with `trace` NOT added. Global Settings UI already auto-renders `trace.max_slice_depth` under the `trace` section per the existing `CONFIG_FIELD_MAP` plumbing — no FE work needed | Slice-depth is a coarse-grained analysis knob; per-app variance is over-engineered for the actual operator workflow (operators set it once based on app-class complexity and forget). Avoids fattening the per-app YAML schema for a knob no one has asked to vary per-app. |

Two items deferred from the same session pending later resolution:

- **Chip-click behaviour** — the *hover* tooltip on the `9 neutral?` / `9 unverdicted?` spans is locked (per N6 + the approved tooltip-wording drafts below); the *click* affordance — (a) multi-line highlight in Code Browser / (b) popover list with predicate snippets / (c) hybrid (b)-now + (a)-follow-up — is deferred per operator direction *"let's keep Chip-click for a little later, for now I want to get the graph right"*. Re-open after v3.X-next.1 + v3.X-next.2 land the CFG-aware ranking.
- **Tooltip wording specifics** for the `9 neutral?` / `9 unverdicted?` hover tooltips — approved in plain-language form during the same Ask-mode session, will live in a frontend constants module (`androscan/web/frontend/src/components/trace/copy.ts` or similar) so wording iterations don't require touching the chip render. Approved drafts (each ~40 words, plain-language, no "slicer" / "max-walk" jargon): **Neutral** — *"AndroScan's analyzer looked at this branch and couldn't tell if it's allowing or denying access. Usually that means it's a routine check (length, null, type) rather than a security gate. Occasionally it's a real gate using a pattern the analyzer doesn't recognize yet — those automatically get a second pass from the LLM. The `?` reflects that ambiguity. Click the card to inspect the branch."*; **Unverdicted** — *"AndroScan's analyzer didn't reach this branch before hitting its analysis budget. Different from neutral (which means 'analyzed but inconclusive'). To analyze deeper branches, raise **Settings → Global → trace → max_slice_depth** (current default 2, hard cap 4). Higher values surface more branches but slow down analysis."* Both strings ship verbatim in v3.X-next.2 alongside the chip relabel from N6.

#### Post-ratification migration story (supersedes the pre-ratification proposal at § Migration story above)

Under the locked Q4=(b) additive + Q7=(i) URL-gate-removed + N1=(i) standalone-promotion-beat + N2=(i) immediate-v2-deletion combination, the migration plays out as follows:

- **v3.X-next.1 (slicer extension; backend-only)** — new `CallSite` dataclass + `method_invocations` field on `BehaviorAnchor` ships behind no URL gate (additive, defaults to empty dict so old `trace.sqlite` caches deserialise cleanly). The current v3.1 emitter (`executionFlowGraphV3.ts`) ignores the new field — it doesn't yet know how to consume it — so production rendering is unchanged. Tests pin the field shape on three anchor classes (linear / branchy / nested-locals) per Q8=(b). Optional: dev-overlay JSON dump on `?flow=v3` for operator inspection of `method_invocations` content before the renderer commits to it.
- **v3.X-next.2 (emitter evolution + URL-gate removal + v2-module deletion; frontend-only)** — `executionFlowGraphV3.ts` evolves in-place to consume `method_invocations`: walks per-method invocation list in `instruction_index` order to emit sequential `prev_invoke → next_invoke` edges; uses `in_branch_of` + `branch_label` to fork into per-branch chains under decision-source nodes; includes all `method_invocations` callees as nodes (closes the "validate_pin / create_session missing from graph" concern). At the same commit: `?flow=v3` URL gate removed from `LabTraceMode.tsx` (per Q7=(i)); v2-pair `executionFlowGraph.ts` + `ExecutionFlow.tsx` deleted (per N2=(i)). FE test runner picked at the v3.X-next.2 planning-checkpoint top (vitest recommended; matches Vite build substrate); FE unit tests cover the new emitter's CFG-edge-construction logic on the same three anchor classes used by the slicer tests. Adjacent UX items from N3-N8 ship in v3.X-next.2 alongside the emitter change (no separate FE-only commits).

No straddle commit, no migration window, no `?flow=v2` rollback affordance. Catastrophic regression path = git revert / point-release. v3.X-next.1 lands the wire-compatible substrate; v3.X-next.2 lands the operator-visible visual change + the cleanup.

- **Provenance-split bug caught + fixed in same v3.X-next.2 commit.** First-pass implementation tagged `method_invocations` callees with `isPlanTarget: true`, which (a) incorrectly bypassed `gatesOnly` framework filter + (b) false-flagged every invoke target as `possiblyInlined: true` (the opposite signal). Surfaced by the framework-filtered-callee test failing on first vitest run. Fixed by introducing a third provenance flag `isInvokeTarget` in `CallerCollector` distinct from `isEntry` and `isPlanTarget`; framework-filter bypass stays scoped to operator-opted-in `isEntry + isPlanTarget`; `possiblyInlined` heuristic stays `!isDecisionSource && isPlanTarget` (provenance-correct). Demonstrates the value of the new FE test infra — the bug would have shipped silent without it.
